TD-11's first two bullets are UNCHANGED — instants are UTC, class times are wall-clock values with no zone — and this revision is what makes its third bullet true in a running system; its parenthesis *«pin tzdata updates in the Docker image»* is superseded: an image cannot be current, the host can. **Host contract:** the Staging and Production hosts keep `tzdata` current (unattended upgrades) — recorded in the deployment handbook and verified at deploy by `GET /clock` answering `source: host-zoneinfo`.

**§3 — «إتمام المستوى»: THE ADMINISTRATION'S ATTESTATION, ITS CERTIFICATE, AND ONE ENROLMENTS DIALOG.** **(a) One door.** On المستفيدات, «تسجيل» and «تعديل» become one row action, «إدارة التسجيلات», available to every مستفيدة including one with no placement: one dialog lists every placement she holds, edits or ends each (the existing dialogs, opened over it), places her in another Level, and carries (b)–(c) under each Level. No enrolment rule moved: `enrolAtPlacement`, R27, BR-21 and the scope checks are where they were. **(b) The mark is a NEW, STORED fact and BR-11 is not changed by it.** BR-11 stays derived and is never stored (R166 §1). `LevelCompletionMark` records that an Admin or Super Admin attested she completed a Level — one row per (student, Level), because completion is about her and the Level and R122 lets her hold several enrolments at it. **She may be marked while BR-11 is unmet — never unknowingly:** the dialog states under the Level, before anything is pressed, what is missing in words (الحفظ *n* من *m* سورة؛ اختبارات التفسير *n* من *m*; or that the Level has no «مقرر الحفظ»), the write is refused `409 REQUIREMENTS_NOT_MET` until the caller sends `acknowledge_unmet: true`, and `requirements_met` keeps what BR-11 read at that moment, permanently. Scope is the enrolment's branch (§7, R24); out of reach is `404` (§20 rule 17). Removing a mark is a hard delete, audited — no Trash entry, because nothing of hers is lost. **(c) The certificate is a SECOND confirmation**, separate on purpose: a mark is a record, showing a certificate is a publication. Confirming draws a number from a sequence at first issue, kept for ever (withdrawing hides the certificate and keeps the number; a mark whose certificate is showing cannot be removed — `409 CERTIFICATE_ISSUED`). **(d) «شهاداتي»** — a new module of the beneficiary portal under child context (§4.3): `GET /students/me/certificates` returns only CONFIRMED certificates of the acting student (`certificate_number`, her recorded name, Level, Category, branch, and the two dates as Morocco calendar dates). The certificate is drawn in HTML/CSS at A4-landscape proportions and **«تحميل PDF» is the browser's own print-to-PDF** of that one certificate: the browser's text engine shapes the Arabic, so the file holds real text; nothing is generated, stored or sent, and no dependency is added. Attendance still gates nothing; no promotion or notification is triggered. **TD-3 adds:** `GET /admin/students/{id}/level-completions`, `PUT`/`DELETE …/{levelId}`, `PUT`/`DELETE …/{levelId}/certificate`, `GET /students/me/certificates`.

**§4 — «تثبيت التطبيق»: THE PLATFORM INSTALLS FROM THE BROWSER.** A web-app manifest (`/manifest.json`: standalone, RTL Arabic, 192/512 and a maskable icon), and a button in the top menu — desktop bar and mobile sheet, signed in or not — that does what the DEVICE can do (`installOffer`): the browser's own install sheet where it announced one (Chrome, Edge, Samsung Internet, Opera); the Share-sheet steps on iPhone and iPad, where no browser can install any other way; the browser-menu steps on other phones; and nothing at all once installed or on a desktop that cannot install. **The service worker caches NOTHING, by decision:** it exists because some browsers still ask for one before offering installation. The API is never cacheable (tiers, consent and child context are decided per request) and a private recording must never be readable from a device after its permission is gone; an offline mode would be a separate decision with privacy consequences, not a side effect of being installable.

**§5 — RECORDINGS: NEVER LEFT BEHIND, AND ADDRESSED TO A WHOLE CATEGORY.** **(a) Kept:** a recording is filed under ONE Level — the first, in the Levels' own order, of those its class addresses (R166 §4). **(b) «كل مستويات الفئة».** `EducationalContent.whole_category` says an item is addressed to EVERY Level of its Level's Category — those that exist and those added later, because the Category is read through `level_id` at the moment of the read and never copied. The ingest sets it when the class addresses every live Level of exactly one Category (some Levels, or Levels of two Categories, stay with the first Level); staff may set it on any item (`PATCH /content/{id}`). §4.9's tiers are unchanged in WHO may read — a private item is readable by a member of ANY Level of that Category, never by anyone else — `branch_id` keeps its meaning (NULL is every branch), `GET /library?level_id=` returns a Level's own items AND its Category's, `?whole_category=` isolates either set, and المحتوى التعليمي opens each Category with a «كل مستويات الفئة» shelf. Under a session's «التسجيلات» it appears as before, through the same one tier predicate. **(c) A recording is never left to a delivery that may not happen.** Two hand-offs were each attempted a fixed number of times and then never again: the provider's completion callback, and the import job (four retries, TD-7). **`session-recording-reconcile` (TD-7, every fifteen minutes)**: a recording still open after three minutes is ASKED about (`reportRecording`) and the answer travels the same door a verified callback does; where the provider has no answer — it forgets old jobs, or is down — **the staged object is the fact** (the recorder uploads it in one atomic PUT after finalising the file); every completed recording that is not yet library content is re-queued, indefinitely, so a deployed fix heals without an operator; and a recording the provider POSITIVELY no longer knows, a day on, with nothing staged, is recorded `failed` so the class can be recorded again. It deletes nothing, and no collector addresses the recording staging bucket. **What cannot be repaired is prevented:** a recorder restarted mid-class loses what it had captured, so a deployment first asks `npm run ops:active-recordings` (exit `3` names the recordings still open) and waits. **Declared residual risk:** a recorder CRASH mid-class (host reboot, out of memory) still loses that class's capture; segmented output uploaded during the class would close it and is not built.

**§6 — One registration form for several roles, decided per role, with ordered circle preferences for a first-time مستفيدة: NOT BUILT.** Drafted as [`SRS-PROPOSAL-R168.md`](SRS-PROPOSAL-R168.md) with the six decisions only the Owner can make — chiefly whether administrative staff may ask through the public form at all (R49 says no today).

**Revision 166 (Document Owner decisions — Level completion, everything about one session in one dialog, a class is called what it is, and recordings of filter-built classes, ratified 2026-09-21):** **FOUR BUILT AND VERIFIED; ONE DEFECT FOUND IN PASSING IS PLATFORM-WIDE AND IS FIXED HERE BECAUSE IT HAD TO BE BEFORE THERE IS PRODUCTION DATA.** One migration (`20260921090000_r166_class_title_is_composed` — two columns stop being required; nothing is dropped or rewritten). The class and session write contracts LOSE a key (`title`); everything else is additive.

**§1 — Level completion: BR-11's second clause is defined.** BR-11 always read *100 % memorisation coverage, plus the Level's final exam only if one is configured*, and its second clause was unreachable — nothing in the model could mark an exam as a Level's final one, so the engine reported `final_exam_configured: false` rather than invent a marker. Revision 165 gave an exam its Surah and gave Subjects `requires_surahs`; this is what those were for. **A مستفيدة completes a Level when, for EVERY Surah of its «مقرر الحفظ», (1) the Surah is memorised — §4.5's coverage is 100 %, `new_memorization` only (R95) — and (2) she has TAKEN an exam of that Surah in a Subject that works by Surah and is not the memorisation tracker** (today: تفسير القرآن — found by its two columns, never by its name).
  * ***Taken* means a recorded mark that is not «غائبة», or a remote paper she submitted. No pass mark is applied, because the platform has none** (§4.6 records a score out of a maximum and nothing calls a score a failure); the Owner's word was *taking*. If a pass mark is ever wanted it is a new rule, and this clause is where it goes.
  * **Clause 2 applies only where it can be met** — BR-11's own *"only if one is configured"*: a Level that teaches no such Subject is completed by memorisation alone, exactly as before. A Level with no «مقرر الحفظ» is `null`, never complete. Like memorisation, *examined* is a fact about (student, Surah) and does not fork per Level. Attendance still gates nothing.
  * **One rule, one home** (`policies/level-completion.ts`, a pure `decideCompletion`), used by both reads so they cannot disagree: `GET /admin/levels/{id}/completion` (each row gains `examined_surahs` and, per Surah, `exam_taken`; `final_exam_configured` keeps its name and becomes truthful) and the per-beneficiary progress read behind «حفظي» and the مؤطِّرة's Quran screen (each Level gains `completion { complete, configured_surahs, memorised_surahs, examined_surahs, exams_required }`, each Surah `exam_taken`). **It is SHOWN where progress already lives** — the Owner's 2026-08-17 decision that completion does not belong on the syllabus page stands — and what is still missing is named, Surah by Surah, not only «لم يكتمل».
  * Nothing is stored: completion is derived on read, like the coverage it is made of. No certificate, notification or promotion is triggered by it.

**§2 — «تعديل الحصة» changes everything about ONE session, in one save.** Who a session is for — branch, Category, Level, group, circle (R92/R161) — lived behind a separate «الحضور» row action and who takes it behind «طاقم التدريس» (R43.4), so the edit dialog looked as though it could not change them. For «هذه الحصة فقط» it now offers both, beside the Subject, the Surah, the room, the delivery, the date, the times, the visibility and «الوصف»: the same shared editors the two row actions still open, so the three cannot seed, offer or compare differently. **One «حفظ» is one decision**: `PATCH /sessions/{id}` gains an optional `audience` object (the five lists of `PUT /sessions/{id}/audience`, all or none), written in the same transaction by the same writer — its own `session.audience` audit row and consent re-evaluation included — under ONE version bump. **Only what she changed is sent**: re-sending an inherited audience untouched would turn it into an override nobody made. Authority is unchanged.

**§3 — a class is CALLED what it is; nobody types its title.** Revision 165 pre-filled «العنوان» from the type, the Subject, the Surah and the main teacher, and the form stored it. A stored suggestion goes stale the first time anything it was built from changes — a cover teacher for one date, a Surah changed for one occurrence, a class moved an hour — and keeps saying what used to be true. The Owner's proposal is adopted **for the items a title can be composed for**: a **class and each of its occurrences** have no typed title; every reader is given one composed at read time (`lib/item-title.ts`) — *type — Subject — Surah(s) — main teacher's public display name — when* (a repeating class's own row carries its time and no date; each occurrence carries its own date and time, which also retires R165's compromise). What somebody wants to add in her own words goes in **«الوصف», which the calendar dialog now shows for a class** (the server sent `null` there, on reasoning R138 had made untrue).
  * **Kept, deliberately:** an **activity and a holiday keep their typed title** — «حفل ختم القرآن», «عطلة عيد الفطر» ARE the item, and nothing could compose them; and a **sitting scheduled from an authored paper keeps the paper's title**, which is that paper's identity in «بناء الاختبارات». A **bare sitting** (no paper) is composed like a class — but STORED, because `exam.title` is shared with authored titles and read in a dozen places that must not each learn to tell the two apart: written by `scheduleExam`, and recomposed by `PATCH /exams/{id}` only while it is still the composed one, so a title somebody typed is never overwritten.
  * **Contract.** `title` is REMOVED from the class create/update bodies, from `PATCH /sessions/{id}` and from `bare` on `POST /exams/schedule`; `.strict()` refuses the key rather than accepting and dropping it. Every read keeps `title`/`item_title`, now composed. This supersedes R57's *"a class carries its own name"* and R138's per-occurrence **title**; R138's per-occurrence **description**, and its resync/override/protection machinery, stand unchanged.
  * **Storage.** `recurring_course_schedule.title` and `session.title` are retired in place — nullable, no longer written or read, every value already there left exactly as typed. Dropping the columns is a later cleanup with no behaviour attached.
  * **A bounded column is respected.** `EducationalContent.title` and `Exam.title` are `VARCHAR(120)`; a composed title that overflowed would REFUSE the row, not shorten it — for a recording, one more way to reach nobody. `composeItemTitle` takes a limit: Surahs give way first (visibly, «…»), then the main teacher, and **«when» is never shortened**. R165's recording title is routed through it.

**§4 — a recording of a filter-built class is imported like any other.** «إيقاف التسجيل» answered *«انتهى التسجيل لكن تعذّرت تهيئته للنشر»* and the recording appeared nowhere. The import job read a class's Level from the three single-target columns only; a class built through the five filters — **every class created since Revision 163 §5** — has none of them, so each recording stopped at *"the occurrence resolves to no Level"*, used its four retries on the same refusal, and was left with no job to try it again. It now resolves the Level through `scheduleLevelIds`, the one resolution the Subject and Surah rules already use; a class addressing several Levels files its recording under the first in the Levels' own order (`EducationalContent.level_id` is single — a known limit: a *private* recording of a two-Level class is listed for the first Level's beneficiaries only). `npm run ops:requeue-recordings` (`src/ops/`, so the release image ships it) re-queues imports that were stranded, idempotently, printing counts and ids only; `-- --dry-run` lists without writing.

**FOUND IN PASSING, PLATFORM-WIDE — every instant was written an hour early and read an hour late.** `@prisma/adapter-pg` exchanges `timestamptz` as text and assumes a UTC session; the database's own default zone is `Africa/Casablanca`. The two errors cancel on a round trip, which is how it passed every test, and cancel nowhere else: a `DEFAULT now()` read by the application, raw SQL comparing a written instant with `now()`, a backup, an export, an operator's `psql`. **Every session the application opens is now UTC** (`options: '-c TimeZone=UTC'` on the adapter's pool — the adapter's assumption, so it travels with the adapter to every tier and every disposable stack; an operator's own session still reads the association's clock). Nothing in the codebase's SQL depends on the session zone: dates and wall-clock times are `date`/`time` columns (TD-11). **Consequence, accepted because there is no Production data yet:** instants the application wrote before this revision are one hour earlier, in absolute terms, than was meant, and now read as such — on Localhost and Staging only, under the Owner's standing authorization for both.

**Verified:** backend typecheck, lint and unit tests; the full disposable integration suite; frontend typecheck, lint and unit tests; on Localhost in a real browser — `verify-class-filters` 20/20 (journey D: «تعديل الحصة» offers all of it and ONE `PATCH /sessions/{id}` carries the changed audience and not the untouched staff), `verify-schedule-edit` 13/13, `verify-teacher-scheduling` 14/14, `verify-exam-scheduling` 92/92; and the Owner's own seven stranded recordings on Localhost imported after the fix.

**Revision 165 (Document Owner decisions — nine reported items across the online classroom, إضافة عنصر, the series editor, the Subjects screen and the browser harnesses, ratified 2026-09-20):** **EIGHT BUILT AND VERIFIED; THE NINTH (§9) IS AN ASSESSMENT, NOT A BUILD.** One migration (`20260920100000_r165_surahs_in_scheduling` — one column, one CHECK, two join tables); additive wire changes only; no new route.

**§1 — the recording line follows the recording, and the recording is named for what it is.** *(a)* After «بدء التسجيل» the classroom kept saying «جارٍ بدء التسجيل…» beside a banner that already read «جاري التسجيل», and «جارٍ إيقاف التسجيل…» after it had stopped: the recording's state was read once and never again. While a recording is in a transitional state (`starting`, `stopping`, `processing`, `importing`) the classroom now re-reads it every three seconds and stops when it settles; and the state line is not printed at all when it would only repeat the live banner. *(b)* The platform's own capture of a class is **titled** *the type of the session — the Subject — the Surah(s) — the main teacher's public display name — the date and time «إيقاف التسجيل» was pressed* (`sessionRecordingBaseName`), second and later recordings of one occurrence still suffixed ` 2`, ` 3` (R75.6). **The instant is the recording's own `stopped_at`, never *now***, so every retry of the ingestion job answers the same. **The object's storage key and file name are deliberately NOT changed**: a key carries a slug of its file name (TD-9) and must never carry a person's name, and it must resolve identically on a retry (R99.15) — so the richer name is the title she reads, and the key keeps the person-free *Subject — date*. Importing into the content library, linking to the occurrence and listing under «التسجيلات» were already R99's and are unchanged.

**§2 — a Subject that works by Surah says WHICH Surah whenever it is scheduled.** Each Level's Surahs are set by the Super Admin in «مقرر الحفظ» (`LevelSurah`); حفظ القرآن memorises them and تفسير القرآن studies the same ones. **Which Subjects work by Surah is a column — `subject.requires_surahs` — never a match on a Subject's name** (R27 made Subjects editable reference data; §4.4b requires rules checked generically). The Production baseline sets it on حفظ القرآن and تفسير القرآن; the Super Admin may set it on any Subject from المواد; and **the Subject that tracks memorisation necessarily carries it** (`subject_tracker_requires_surahs_check`, with a coded `TRACKER_REQUIRES_SURAHS` refusal in front of it so the constraint never reaches anybody as a `500`). This **supersedes §4.4b's description of تفسير القرآن as "an ordinary unmarked Subject"** in exactly one respect — it is now marked as working by Surah — and in no other: it still does not authorise, configure or contribute to memorisation coverage, the progress log is still student + Surah, and `tracks_quran_progress` is still حفظ القرآن's alone.
  * **One rule, one home** (`resolveSurahs`, `policies/curriculum.ts`), asked at every surface that schedules: creating a class, editing the whole series, the successor of a «from this date onward» split, one occurrence, and an exam (both creation paths and both edit routes). The Subject works by Surah and none is named → `SURAHS_REQUIRED`. It does not, and one is named → `SURAHS_NOT_APPLICABLE` (a Surah on a fiqh class is an invented value, not a harmless extra). A Surah outside the «مقرر الحفظ» of **every** Level the item addresses → `SURAH_NOT_IN_SYLLABUS`; one Level is enough, so a class for two Levels may study a Surah only one of them has reached. All three are `VALIDATION_FAILED` with the stable reason in `details.reason`, **and the rule is asked only after every authorization check on that surface**: a caller with no authority over the Level is owed her refusal, never a `400` describing something she may not create (§20 rule 17).
  * **A class names one or more Surahs** (`course_schedule_surah`); **an exam names exactly one** (`exam.surah_id`, which existed and was written by no route), and **any number of exams may examine the same Surah**. A split's successor inherits the class's Surahs unless the edit names its own; if the split moves the class to a Subject that has none, they are dropped rather than carried as an invented value. Nothing already scheduled is rewritten by this revision or by toggling the marker: the rule is asked when something is written.
  * **Contract (additive).** `surah_ids` on the class create/update bodies and `surah_id` on the three exam write bodies; `surah_ids` + `surah_names` on the class row, `surah_id` + `surah_name` on the exam row, `surah_names` on every calendar occurrence (empty where there is none, always for an Event); `requires_surahs` on the Subject row and its update body; and on `/me/scope-options` and `/me/course-schedule-options`, `subjects[].requires_surahs`, `levels[].surah_ids` and `surahs[]` — inline for the reason `subject_ids` is: the alternative is an Admin-only read a مؤطِّرة scheduling her own class is refused. The author's paper read gains `subject_id`, because a sitting scheduled from a paper never asks the form's own Subject.
  * **The title is SUGGESTED** in إضافة عنصر for a class and an exam — *type — Subject — Surah(s) — main teacher — when* — and follows the form until she types in it; an existing item keeps the title it has. **A repeating class carries its time and no date**: its title is snapshotted onto every occurrence (R138), so a date would print the first session's date on all the others. A one-off carries both. An activity is not suggested one: its title is its identity.
  * **The certification rule the Owner described — a Level is completed by memorising its Surahs AND passing the تفسير exams of the same Surahs — is recorded as the direction this data serves, and is NOT built here.** Nothing in this revision computes completion.

**§3 — two browser harnesses that had stopped running now run, and running them found three defects.** `verify-schedule-edit` wrote into a native date input the platform's own picker had replaced; `verify-teacher-scheduling` never chose an item type (the form opens on none since R110), compared the type's *values* to kind names when they are catalogue ids, and read her class table while the list was still loading — and under a view («قائمة») the page no longer opens on. Both now drive the form as a person does. What they then found: **(i)** a مؤطِّرة's exam was refused with a bare `400` — R140 narrowed her scope options to her *declared capability* for every item type, and the scope hook drops a value its options do not hold, so the Level and Subject her own class had supplied (R94) were silently cleared. The narrowing now applies **only while she composes a class**. **(ii)** the same exam then went out addressed to the whole Level and was refused `WHOLE_LEVEL_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, because the group list the hook reads is Admin-only; her sitting's group now comes from the class she named. **(iii)** the development scenario attached its class to a **soft-deleted** academic year, which no form offers.

**§4 — «هذه الحصة وكل ما بعدها» at a class's FIRST session no longer answers `500`.** A split closes the predecessor the day before the split; at the first occurrence that day precedes the class's own `anchor_date`, which `course_schedule_effective_until_check` refuses — and a CHECK violation is not a coded error. It was not a mistake on her part: it means *the whole series, identity included*, which no other scope can say (§4.4 freezes identity on `all_sessions`). Now: when the predecessor keeps nothing (every occurrence moved to the successor) it is **retired** — soft-deleted, with `predecessor_retired` in the audit row and **no Trash snapshot**, because nothing was deleted from anybody's point of view and a restore would re-materialize the series on top of its own successor. When it still owns protected history (held, attended or carrying content from the first date on) it stays alive to own it — `readSessionPage` hides the sessions of a deleted schedule — closed on its anchor date, the earliest the CHECK allows; that cannot mint a duplicate, since materialization skips any date already holding a row of the same schedule, removed ones included.

**§5 — the Surah joins the fields editable for ONE occurrence.** Branch, Category, Level, group and circle (R92/R161, «الحضور»), Subject, room and delivery (R97/R161, «تعديل الحصة») and staff (R43.4, «طاقم التدريس») already were. `session_surah` holds one occurrence's own Surahs. **Unlike an audience dimension, which is ADDED to the class's, an occurrence's Surahs REPLACE the class's for that date** — a Surah is what is taught that day. No rows means *inherit*; `[]` returns an occurrence to the class's own; and an occurrence that simply names the class's own Surahs is stored as *inherit*, so it keeps following the class. The rule is asked of one occurrence only when Surahs are named or the Subject it teaches **really changes** — not merely because the key was sent — so an unrelated edit of a class scheduled before this revision (a room change) is never held to a Surah the screen cannot offer. The list the series editor reads now carries each occurrence's `subject_id` (declared by the client since R161 and never sent, so a saved Subject override reopened as the class's and the next unrelated save silently cleared it) and `surah_ids`.

**§6 — إضافة عنصر asks for the branch once.** «فروع» (who the class is for) and «الفرع المنظِّم» (whose administration runs it, §4.4) read as the same question twice. The second is no longer asked: a class still belongs to exactly one branch, **derived** (`homeBranchOf`) — the one branch she chose; else the branch of the room she chose; else the branch the class already belongs to while her choice still includes it; else the first branch she chose; else the first she may act on. Rooms are offered across the branches in play, each named with its branch. `branch_id` on the wire is unchanged.

**§7 — a multi-choice dropdown names what was chosen.** «1 محددة» said how many and not which. The trigger now reads the chosen items' names, separated by «، », with the full list as its tooltip; unchosen still reads the field's own empty label («الكل», «اختاري…»).

**§8 — the sentence explaining how the filters combine is removed** from إضافة عنصر and the series editor. The rule itself (R163 §5) is unchanged.

**§9 — the Production hosting quote was assessed, not acted on** (Nindo Host «Cloud Maroc», 4 vCPU / 8 GB / 100 GB SSD, with «Sauvegarde 100 GB»; recorded in [provider acceptance](operations/provider-acceptance.md#assessment-of-one-quotation-against-this-matrix-2026-09-20)). CPU and memory meet the measured requirement (R164 §5) and the Production preflight floor; **the 100 GB disk is about half of what this platform's own planning assumed**, which is a constraint on how long video recordings and same-host recovery points can share it, not a blocker to launching; and six facts must be confirmed by the provider in writing before it is ordered — above all that its anti-DDoS layer passes inbound UDP 7882. **Production go-live remains on hold; nothing was ordered, provisioned or deployed.**

**Also found and fixed in passing:** a filter-built class addressed by group or circle alone published `level_id: null`, so its «from this date onward» editor opened with the Subject EMPTY and refused to save until it was chosen again; `level_id` now names the first Level such a class addresses, `dimensions` remaining the authority on its whole audience.

**Verified:** backend typecheck, lint and 345 unit tests; 16 new integration tests for the Surah rule plus 3 for the first-session split, and the full disposable integration suite; frontend typecheck, lint and 1,388+ unit tests; on Localhost in a real browser — `verify-class-filters` 16/16 (names not counts, one branch question, the required Surah, the suggested title, the inherited Surah, and the first-session split answering `200` and leaving one class), `verify-schedule-edit` 13/13 and `verify-teacher-scheduling` 14/14, each leaving zero residue.

**Revision 164 (Document Owner decision — online classes are SELF-HOSTED, on one architecture, on every tier, 2026-09-20):** **BUILT, VERIFIED ON LOCALHOST IN FULL, AND DEPLOYED TO STAGING; PRODUCTION RUNS THE IDENTICAL ARCHITECTURE AND AWAITS ITS HOST.** This answers Revision 163 §4. **LiveKit Cloud is not used**, and beneficiaries' audio, video and recordings never leave this infrastructure: no third-party media service, no third-party STUN or TURN, no external bucket. No schema change and no application-contract change; the only application edit is one recorder option.

**(1) One media model, defined once.** LiveKit, its Egress recorder and the Redis they coordinate through live in `docker-compose.yml` itself — not in an overlay — for exactly the reason Revision-era storage unification gave: a tier must not run a different model because a Compose file was included or omitted. Localhost, Staging and Production resolve to the same nine services; tiers differ only in published ports, memory ceilings and the recorder's CPU budget. The API does not depend on the media stack: with it down, «دخول الحصة» answers R98's truthful `503` and nothing else degrades.

**(2) §19.1's port posture is REVISED, deliberately and minimally.** It read *only Nginx publishes ports, and only 80/443*. WebRTC media is UDP with a TCP fallback and cannot pass through a web proxy, so that rule as written made a self-hosted class impossible. It now reads: **exactly four ports are published, and each has one owner — Nginx 80/443, and the media server 7881/tcp and 7882/udp.** One multiplexed UDP port serves every participant, so no port RANGE is opened. **Signalling stays on 443**: the browser opens `wss://<domain>/rtc`, Nginx proxies it, and the media server's own signalling port (7880) is never published. PostgreSQL, the object store, Redis and the recorder still receive no host binding. Host preflight and CI enforce the new rule as strictly as the old one and are proven able to fail on a published 7880, any further published port, a loopback-bound media port, a wrong advertised address, mismatched keys, external STUN, and a third-party media URL.

**(3) The host's address is STATED, never discovered.** LiveKit's default asks a public STUN server (Google's) for the host's own address. `use_external_ip: false` with `LIVEKIT_NODE_IP` removes that call; preflight holds the value to the host's approved public IPv4. **A second third-party contact was found only by testing Staging from outside, and is closed too:** with no STUN server configured LiveKit hands every client **Twilio's and Google's public STUN servers**, so each beneficiary's browser would have disclosed its address to both. There is no setting for *none*; the media server therefore names only a **dead port on its own host** (3478, where nothing listens and which no firewall opens — enforced by preflight, which also refuses any third-party entry), covering the recorder and every other client, and the classroom passes an explicit empty ICE list so a browser attempts no STUN at all. A publicly addressed media server needs none. **Not the media port itself:** that was the first fix, and an outside A/B test showed it pushing media onto the TCP fallback every time, because a plain STUN request to the media port spoils that port's handling of the same client's real connection; with a dead port, or none, media uses UDP every time. TD-13 gains `LIVEKIT_NODE_IP` (read by Compose, not by the application), and on release tiers the `LIVEKIT_*` group is required rather than optional — `LIVEKIT_URL` is simply the deployment's own origin.

**(4) Recordings are written straight into this deployment's object store** — unchanged in design, now true on every tier. The API hands Egress its target per request: the internal S3 endpoint, path-style, the staging bucket. The platform verifies and imports the file into the content lifecycle and sweeps the staging object (R99.13). **Egress's own `EG_*.json` manifest is switched off**: nothing read it, ingestion deliberately sweeps only the media object it asked for, and in Production — where recordings accumulate for years — one orphan per recording in a bucket nothing lists is unbounded residue. **Retention is the same architecture on every tier:** a recording is `EducationalContent` on the tier's persistent `seaweedfs-data` volume, removed only through the ordinary content lifecycle, and included in every recovery point; on Localhost and Staging the Owner additionally authorizes discarding and recreating the object store outright.

**(5) Resources were MEASURED before anything was built, and the measurement found a defect the design would otherwise have shipped.** With real rooms and real recordings: the media server peaks at 0.35 core and 140 MiB; a 720p/30 room recording costs the recorder **1.7–1.9 cores and ~700 MiB**; an audio-only one settles at **0.2–0.35 core and ~360 MiB**. With the WHOLE stack confined to two cores the video still landed as true 30 fps (888 frames in 29.6 s against 893 unconfined) while using 1.98 of the 2 cores and 1.33 GiB. **Egress, however, prices a room recording at 4 cores by default and refuses any request it thinks the host cannot afford** — reproduced under a 2-CPU quota: every VIDEO recording answered `503` while audio worked. The recorder's costs are therefore set from measurement (2.0 video, 1.0 audio; 1.5 and 0.5 on Staging, whose two cores can never show 2.0 idle), which on Staging admits one video recording and refuses a second. **Verdicts:** Localhost ample; Staging (2 vCPU, 3.8 GiB) carries one recording at a time with the CPU saturated for a video's length — acceptable for synthetic data, and stated in its overlay rather than hidden, including that its memory ceilings now sum past its RAM; **Production requires at least 4 vCPU**, which host preflight now enforces (2 on Staging) and which the provider matrix already specified. Capacity from the measured bitrates: ≈59 MB per recorded audio hour, ≈0.63 GB per recorded video hour.

**Verified:** all three tiers' resolved Compose graphs (nine services; release tiers publish exactly 80, 443, 7881/tcp, 7882/udp); Localhost in a real browser through the same-origin `/rtc` route — `verify-livekit-join` 61/61 with a real MP4 and OGG, `verify-livekit-ingest` 27/27 with the staging bucket left exactly as it was found — and again with a stated host address and media ports on every interface, the release network shape, 61/61 including recordings, which proves the recorder reaches the media server through the host's own address.

**Revision 163 (Document Owner decisions — five reported items across إضافة عنصر, the occurrence dialog, the attendance register and online classes, ratified 2026-09-20):** **THREE BUILT AND VERIFIED IN FULL (§1, §2, §3); §5 BUILT FOR CLASSES AND HELD FOR ACTIVITIES AND EXAMS; §4 IS NOT A CODE DEFECT AND AWAITS A PROVISIONING DECISION.** One additive wire change (two keys on the calendar occurrence, one fallback on a schedule's `target_name`); no new column, table or migration.

**§1 — «صوت فقط» is what an online class starts as.** «نوع الاتصال» defaulted to «صوت وصورة». Most of the association's remote teaching is voice, and a beneficiary at home is not asked for her camera unless somebody chose that. An UNSET value now reads as audio-only in إضافة عنصر and when an in-person occurrence is moved عن بُعد; a «صوت وصورة» already saved is kept. One shared function (`initialMediaMode`) so the three call sites cannot disagree.

**§2 — «العنوان» in the dialog of every item.** A calendar chip shows a class by its Subject (R43), and so did the dialog's heading — so the title somebody actually typed (R57; snapshotted per occurrence by R138) reached no reader anywhere. The occurrence now carries `item_title` and the dialog shows it first, for every kind. For an Event and an Exam it equals the heading, because their chip already is their own title. `title` is unchanged, so every chip, filter and harness that reads it is unaffected.

**§3 — «الحضور» belongs to the administration and to the main AND assistant staff of that occurrence — never the public calendar.** Two changes. **(a) Where it is offered.** The panel decided by ROLE, so every مؤطِّرة saw «الحضور» on every class in the association (and met a refusal on opening it), and an administrator browsing the PUBLIC calendar saw it beside every public occurrence. The occurrence now carries `viewer_may_mark_attendance` — the server's own answer, computed in batch from rows the calendar read already loads — and the public page passes the same `canManage={false}` it already passes for every other management action. The flag is **advisory**: `assertMayMark` remains the authority on every read and write, and an integration test holds the two to each other occurrence by occurrence, actor by actor. **(b) Who may mark.** A Session already admitted co-teachers and assistants alike (`staffsSession`); an Event admitted only its responsible person and an Exam only its supervisor. **An Event's and an Exam's assistants now mark too.** R71.3 stands untouched — *"an assistant sees the event and does not change it"*: marking who came is not editing the item, and `assertMayEdit` still refuses her every write to the Event itself.

**§4 — «دخول الحصة» answering *«خدمة الحصص عن بُعد غير مهيّأة بعد»* is the platform telling the truth, not failing.** The classroom asks the provider seam for a room; with no provider configured the API answers `503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` and the page says so (R98). **Staging has never had a media provider**: no `LIVEKIT_*` settings and no media server, since R98. Localhost has one (the dev overlay's LiveKit, Redis and Egress), which is where every R98/R99 verification ran. This is a **provisioning decision the readiness ledger never recorded**, and it is the Owner's: `online-class-provider.md` chose LiveKit with a self-hosting path, and the two ways to give a release tier a provider differ in ways engineering must not pick between. **LiveKit Cloud** needs an account, credentials and (beyond ~two classes a month) a paid tier, routes beneficiaries' live voice and video through infrastructure outside Morocco, and cannot write recordings into this deployment's object store (its Egress requires a public `https://` S3 endpoint). **Self-hosting** keeps media on the association's own VPS, but needs UDP/TCP media ports published to the internet — reversing §19.1's *only Nginx publishes ports, and only 80/443*, which host preflight and CI both enforce — plus memory the 3.7-GiB Staging host does not obviously have once Egress's headless browser is counted. Recorded as an open readiness item; nothing was provisioned.

**§5 — a class is addressed through filters, and «نمط التدريس» is asked nowhere.** This is the confirmation Revision 160 §2 was held for. **Built:** an administrator's class is always built from five filters — فروع, فئات, مستويات, مجموعات مستوى, حلقات — each reading «الكل» until narrowed, each taking one or more values, in إضافة عنصر **and** in the «from this date onward» editor, through one shared module so the two cannot drift. Each filter narrows the ones beneath it (a Category leaves only its own Levels; the Levels and branches in play leave only their own groups and circles — a circle spans branches, so a branch never hides one), and a choice its parent no longer offers is dropped rather than submitted unseen. Every group and circle is loaded, not the first hundred. The combination rule is Revision 155's and unchanged: branches, Categories, Levels and groups intersect; a circle is added on top. `multi_dimension` remains the storage and is never shown: the schedule list names WHO the class is for instead of printing the mode's label, which every new class would otherwise have shared. **A class still belongs to one branch** (`branch_id`: whose administration runs it, where its room is booked) — exactly one branch in the filter answers that; with «الكل» or several the form asks for «الفرع المنظِّم» outright. **Kept, not reversed:** *a class must name a Level, a group or a circle* (`MULTI_DIMENSION_NEEDS_A_LEVEL`). «الكل» on all three is refused in words before any request, because a class delivers a curriculum Subject and R43/R155 require that Subject to be taught at every Level the class reaches. **A self-service مؤطِّرة is unchanged** apart from no longer being shown a one-option picker: her grant covers one whole Level (`TEACHER_ENTIRE_LEVEL_ONLY`), so she keeps the branch/Level pair and is offered none of the administrator's filters (rule O). A class created before this revision keeps the mode it was created with, which now decides only how its locked target is displayed.

**§5, HELD — two real semantic decisions, reported rather than guessed at.** **(i) Activities.** An activity's four dimensions are UNIONED (R139: *this branch OR that Category*), and an unselected dimension therefore means *nobody through this one*, not «الكل». Giving activities the same «الكل»-by-default filters means making them INTERSECT like classes — which changes who existing multi-dimension activities reach and reverses R139's stated rule. The labels were deliberately not changed, because «الكل» over a union would be a false statement. A circle dimension for activities is additionally a new table. **(ii) Exams, and «سورة».** An exam is single-Level by schema (`exam.level_id`), and grading, the grade sheet's audience (BR-7) and a مؤطِّرة's exam scope all read that one Level; several Levels per sitting is a redesign of those, not a form change. `exam.surah_id` exists but no route has ever accepted it, so «سورة» is new API surface whose placement (exam only, or a حفظ class too) is the Owner's. Rooms, the main teacher and her assistants were already on every form they apply to, and a عطلة still carries no staff at all (R137).

**Also found and fixed while verifying §3 in a real browser: the back office's own calendar read the calendar ANONYMOUSLY.** `/admin/schedules?view=calendar` called `GET /calendar` with no credential — its comment claimed the adapter read the session itself, which it never did. So the management calendar showed an administrator the PUBLIC tier only: every private and hidden class and activity was simply missing from it. It now sends the caller's token like the other three calendars.

**Verified:** backend typecheck/lint clean, unit 342/342; frontend typecheck/lint clean, unit 1,372/1,372 (up from 1,361); focused integration on a disposable stack — attendance, calendar and course-schedule services 145/145 and four HTTP suites including the pinned calendar wire key set; the full disposable-stack gate — 2,674 passed with ONE failure, the static soft-delete guard, because lines added above `listCourseSchedules`'s `count({ where })` pushed it more than forty lines from the `where` that carries its `deletedAt: null` (the read was always filtered; the select was hoisted to a constant and the guard's rule re-checked clean); real browser on Localhost — `verify-attendance` 6/6, `verify-unsaved-guard` 24/24, and a new `verify-class-filters` 12/12 that creates a class through the five filters, reads it back, and then drives the «from this date onward» editor through a real split. Two older harnesses (`verify-schedule-edit`, `verify-teacher-scheduling`) were found stale for reasons unrelated to this revision and are recorded as such in `qa-inventory.md` rather than reported as passing.

**Revision 162 (independent codex review, 2026-09-20 — eight defects found across Revision 161's own generalisation and pre-existing `multi_dimension`/attendance code; one already-tracked item left as-is):** **FIXED, VERIFIED, NO SCHEMA CHANGE.** No new column, table or migration — every fix corrects the RESOLUTION of an audience/scope/capability question against data the schema already carries.

**§1 — HIGH: consent withdrawal now discovers every session a `multi_dimension` schedule or an R161 occurrence override can reach, not only the three legacy modes.** `consentSessionIdsForStudent` (`consent-reevaluation.service.ts`) hand-derived its own "does this session's audience include her" test, built before `multi_dimension` (Revision 155) existed and never extended — a withdrawal against a beneficiary reached only through `multi_dimension` or through Revision 161's four newer override tables created NO reevaluation obligation at all, so BR-2's public-recording gate could keep answering `false` indefinitely rather than merely until the next restart sweep. Rewritten in two phases: a deliberately over-inclusive SQL candidate set (now covering all four modes plus any of the five override tables), narrowed to an exact answer by resolving each candidate through the SAME `audienceForSession` + `audienceWhere` every other reader composes (R92 §B7) — never a second hand-rolled evaluation of the combination rules.

**§2 — HIGH: personal calendars (`GET /calendar?mine=true`) reach `multi_dimension` Sessions and all five R161 override dimensions, not only R92's original branch table.** `personalFilters`'s own Session predicate independently reconstructed audience membership from raw enrolment fields, built before Revision 155 and never extended to it or to Revision 161's four newer tables — a beneficiary of a `multi_dimension` class, or of an occurrence combined by Level/Category/Administrative Group/Circle, could be entirely absent from her own calendar. The SQL predicate is now a deliberate superset (a `multi_dimension` schedule's own AND-across-kind rule, restated via the same `dimensionMatch` shape `eventAudienceWhere` already uses for Events; a broad "any override table names one of my ids" arm) — REPLACEMENT overrides on five independent dimensions are not expressible as one static Prisma filter, so precision is restored by a new `filterSessionsByPersonalAudience` post-fetch pass, resolving each surviving candidate through the identical canonical resolver, mirroring `filterExamsByAudience`'s own established pattern one function up.

**§3 — HIGH: الحضور's roster editor no longer synthesises the venue branch as a chosen audience.** `readSessionRoster` fell back to `[schedule.branchId]` whenever a mode genuinely has no branch dimension of its own (`administrative_group`, `teaching_group`, a group/circle-scoped `multi_dimension` class) — silently seeding the branch picker with the VENUE. Saving from that state, even having touched only another dimension, submitted the phantom branch and turned a group- or circle-scoped occurrence into *every enrolled beneficiary at the venue branch*, combined with whatever else was chosen — a population nobody selected. The fallback is removed; an empty audience dimension now reaches the editor exactly as empty as it is, and the venue is shown only from its own separate field, which is where it already lived.

**§4 — MEDIUM: a `multi_dimension` teaching assignment now carries examination and Hidden-event scope identical to its legacy-mode equivalent.** `teacherEventScope`, `assertExamInTeacherScope` and `examScopeWhereForTeacher` all derived a schedule's Level (and, for the exam pair, its reachable Administrative Groups) from three legacy singular columns alone — every one `NULL` by construction on a `multi_dimension` row — so a مؤطِّرة whose only assignment for a Level used Revision 155's newer mode was refused exam authoring and saw an empty exam list for a Level and group she demonstrably teaches, and missed Hidden events scoped to her own teaching. All three now also read the five `multi_dimension` scope tables directly. A `multi_dimension` schedule scoped ONLY by Level (no Administrative Group, no Circle) now carries the SAME whole-Level authority `entire_level` mode does — a direct, mechanical consequence of `audienceWhere`'s own multi_dimension arm applying no group/circle constraint in that shape, not a new policy; a schedule that ALSO names a group or circle does not.

**§5 — MEDIUM: a session's own Subject override (Revision 161) is now the Subject every consumer reads, not only the calendar title.** The calendar's occurrence projection and its `subject_id` filter, `teachesQuran`'s one-off-occurrence arm, and `studentsTaughtBy`'s occurrence arm all read `schedule.subject` unconditionally — an occurrence retaught to a different Subject for one sitting kept its old title on the calendar, could not be found by filtering for its NEW Subject (and stayed findable by filtering for its OLD one), and a one-off cover مؤطِّرة's حفظ القرآن menu and memorisation-write scope disagreed with what she was actually covering that day. All four now prefer the occurrence's own `subject_id` and fall back to the schedule's, on the identical inherit-when-absent footing every other override field already has. Separately, `updateCourseSchedule`'s "overwrite manually edited Sessions" resync (R138) now also clears a Subject override alongside `overridden`, on the same footing room/delivery/visibility already had — an overwrite that left a stale Subject override in place while reporting the occurrence as no longer overridden was a direct consistency defect, not a policy question: `subject_id` is governed by the identical `overridden` flag every field this resync already resets is.

**§6 — MEDIUM: a Subject override against a group-only or circle-only `multi_dimension` schedule is now validated against the Level that group or circle actually implies.** `scheduleLevelIds` returned the schedule's own directly-named `level_ids` alone — empty for a valid class scoped only by Administrative Group or Circle — so the curriculum-validation loop in `overrideSession` silently ran zero times and accepted a Subject the Level never teaches. Now unions in the Levels implied by every named group and circle, reusing the identical computation `course-schedule.service.ts`'s own `effectiveLevelIds` already performs at creation.

**§7 — MEDIUM: two concurrent «تسجيل حضوري» marks for the same person now both answer successfully, never one `200` and one `409 DUPLICATE`.** `markPresent`'s check-then-insert is not itself atomic — the migration's own comment already says so — and relied entirely on `attendance_occurrence_student_unique` to keep the DATA correct while leaving the RESPONSE to whichever side lost the race to throw an unhandled `P2002`, contradicting the function's own "idempotent by construction" claim and the controller's documented `200`-on-repeat contract. The loser now re-reads the row the winner just committed and answers exactly as if she had found it there to begin with — proved with a genuine `Promise.all` race against real PostgreSQL, not a mocked error.

**§8 — LOW: الحضور's group/circle pickers now offer every row, not only the first hundred.** `listAdministrativeGroups`/`listCircles` were each called once at a fixed page size with `meta.total` discarded and no rejection handler; an institute past that count had entries the dialog could never offer, silently, and a failed load produced no notice at all. A new `fetchAllPages` walks every page until `meta.total` is satisfied (behaviourally proven directly, not through a source-string pin — a malformed-total floor included) and a fetch failure now sets the same load-failure notice the roster read already used.

**Not addressed here, left exactly as already tracked:** the pre-existing, independently-documented Library↔calendar cancelled-Session backlink inconsistency (`docs/TASKS.md`, "Calendar/media regression correction — 2026-09-07") — unrelated to Revision 161, not a regression from it, and its own entry already asks for a deliberate lifecycle/navigation-contract decision rather than a quick fix.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint clean; backend unit 342/342 (unchanged — every fix is exercised only by new/extended integration coverage, since none of these were unit-testable without real PostgreSQL); frontend unit 1,361/1,361 (5 new, including genuine behavioural tests of `fetchAllPages` rather than source-string pins). New/extended integration coverage: `personal-calendar-r161.integration.test.ts` (new — multi_dimension inclusion/exclusion, an occurrence Level override reaching a student outside the schedule's own audience, a Circle override, clearing an override, and a dedicated non-leak precision test proving the SQL superset's extra candidates are correctly excluded); `roster-resolution-r161-subject.integration.test.ts` (new — a one-off cover teacher gaining AND losing حفظ القرآن capability by occurrence-level Subject override alone); `consent.integration.test.ts` (+3 — multi_dimension discovery, a non-member excluded, an occurrence Level override discovered); `session-audience.http.integration.test.ts` (+2 — the roster no longer reports a phantom branch, before and after combining another dimension); `session.http.integration.test.ts` (+3 — group-only multi_dimension Subject validation, both directions; the calendar reading/filtering by an occurrence's own Subject); `teacher-exam-scope.http.integration.test.ts` (+4 — the list and write halves for both a group-scoped and a Level-scoped multi_dimension assignment, plus the negative whole-Level boundary); `attendance.integration.test.ts` (+1 — the real concurrent-race idempotency proof); `course-schedule.integration.test.ts` (+1 — the overwrite-clears-Subject-override case). Full disposable-stack integration suite: **2,670 passed / 18 skipped (2,688)**, up from 2,648 before this pass by exactly the 22 tests this pass added, all-table isolation intact, no leftover disposable containers/networks/volumes. All 31 CI guards, `check-openapi-current.sh`, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass. Full run detail in `docs/CHANGES.log`.

**Revision 161 (Document Owner instruction — Revision 160 §3 implemented: R92's per-occurrence audience override generalised from branches alone to every dimension `multi_dimension` schedules already resolve, plus a per-occurrence Subject override, ratified 2026-09-17):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. FOUR NEW ADDITIVE JOIN TABLES, ONE NEW NULLABLE COLUMN; NO DROPPED TABLE OR COLUMN.**

**§1 — the override mechanism, generalised without touching `audienceWhere`'s own well-tested arms.** Four new tables mirror `SessionAudienceBranch` exactly — `SessionAudienceCategory`, `SessionAudienceLevel`, `SessionAudienceAdministrativeGroup`, `SessionAudienceTeachingGroup` — each independent and empty-means-inherit on the identical footing branches already had. `audienceForSession` (`roster-resolution.ts`) is the only function that changed: when ANY of the five override tables carries a row for a session, it now returns a SYNTHETIC `teachingMode: 'multi_dimension'` spec whose `dimensions` are built by a new `naturalDimensions()` helper — each of the five takes its override list if the session has one, else the SCHEDULE's own natural value for that dimension, restated as the one-element array `multi_dimension`'s own composition already expects (`entire_level`'s Level and branch; `administrative_group`'s group; `teaching_group`'s circle; `multi_dimension`'s own five join tables, via the existing `scheduleDimensions`). Every OTHER caller of `audienceForSession` — the personal calendar, cancellation/reschedule notifications, the roster, the audit row's `audience_size`, teacher-scope resolution — already composes through `audienceWhere(spec)` and needed NO changes at all: the generalisation is invisible to them by construction. When no override exists anywhere, the returned spec is byte-for-byte what it always was, so `audienceWhere`'s `entire_level`/`administrative_group`/`teaching_group` arms remain exactly as tested for every ordinary occurrence.

**§2 — every teaching mode now, not `entire_level` alone.** `setSessionAudienceBranches`'s own `AUDIENCE_OVERRIDE_MODE_UNSUPPORTED` refusal (R92 §B6's deliberately narrow "report the rest rather than invent semantics nobody asked for") is REMOVED — replaced by `setSessionAudienceOverrides`, which writes all five dimensions in one transaction (delete-then-recreate per table, exactly as branches always worked) and accepts any schedule mode. **What "combine" means per mode, and why it stays correct**: naming two values of the SAME kind a mode's own natural audience already turns on (two Branches for `entire_level`, two Administrative Groups for `administrative_group`) unions them, on the identical AND-across-kind/OR-within-kind rule `multi_dimension` already established (Revision 155) — this is why the write generalises safely: it was never really "branches only", it was always "the dimension multi_dimension's own composition already turns into a union when several values of it are named", and R92 had simply never been asked to accept any dimension but that one. Naming a CIRCLE, on any mode's occurrence, unions unconditionally regardless of every other dimension (the established "a circle is already Level-locked, so it reads as *these two populations together*" rule) — this is what makes "two circles combined for one sitting while the rest are for one circle" work on a `teaching_group`-mode schedule exactly as stated. Naming a Level or Category on top of a mode whose OWN natural constraint is a different, incompatible dimension (an Administrative Group, say) intersects rather than unions, by the same AND rule — an administrator combining an audience across dimensions uses the SAME dimension the schedule's own mode already turns on, exactly as branch-combination for `entire_level` always required staying on the same Level.

**§3 — a per-occurrence Subject override, on the identical `room_id`/`delivery_mode`/`visibility` snapshot-plus-`overridden` footing.** `Session.subjectId` (nullable, no default — `session.materialize` never writes it) joins `PATCH /sessions/{id}`'s existing field set. Validated against the SCHEDULE's own effective Level(s) — never the occurrence's own audience-dimension override, a deliberately independent question — through the identical `assertSubjectTaughtAtLevel` CREATE already uses (`409 STATE_CONFLICT`, `SUBJECT_NOT_IN_LEVEL`, the same code all curriculum surfaces raise), looped where the schedule names more than one effective Level. `null` clears an existing override; omitting the key leaves it untouched, on the same convention every other field in that endpoint already follows.

**§4 — the interface: one unified «الحضور» dialog, five independent pickers; a Subject select beside room/delivery/visibility in «تعديل».** `SessionAudienceDialog` (formerly branch-only) now renders five `MultiSelectField`s — branches, categories, levels, administrative groups, circles — each seeded with its own currently-effective value(s) (`GET /sessions/{id}/roster`'s `audience` object, generalised the same way) and submitted together through `PUT /sessions/{id}/audience` (renamed from `/audience-branches`, since it now sets all five). Administrative-group and circle OPTIONS are read UNSCOPED (`listAdministrativeGroups`/`listCircles`, `pageSize: 100`), the identical reasoning the `multi_dimension` class picker's own unscoped reads already established (Revision 157): combining across a Level or branch boundary is exactly the case a chained, scoped list cannot answer. The row action offering the dialog («الحضور») is no longer gated on `teachingMode === 'entire_level'` — every mode now offers it, since the write refuses nothing on mode grounds any more. `ScopeDialog`'s own «تعديل» form gains a Subject `SelectField`, manager-only (the same `token`/`identity` gate the schedule-identity-replacement section already uses), fetched unscoped (`mode: 'filter'`) so it needs no Level context this dialog does not otherwise track — the server is the real boundary regardless.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; frontend unit 1,356/1,356. New coverage: `session-audience.http.integration.test.ts` (27 tests — the mode restriction proven gone via a real `administrative_group`-mode occurrence accepting an override; a second Level folding into an `entire_level` occurrence's audience; two Circles combining on the union rule; clearing one dimension's override while another stays set; every existing branch-only assertion re-verified unchanged) and `session.http.integration.test.ts`'s new Subject-override block (4 tests, dedicated far-future sessions rather than the file's own shared `freshSession()` pool, to keep multi-write tests uncontended). Focused integration run: 73/73 across both files plus `consent-safeguarding.integration.test.ts` (R92's consent-reevaluation trigger, re-verified against the renamed route), all-table isolation intact — a pre-existing teardown gap in `session-audience.http.integration.test.ts`'s own `beforeEach` (a `deleteMany({ where })` shorthand the repository's static isolation guard cannot see through a variable reference) was found and fixed alongside. Migration `20260917090000_r161_session_audience_dimensions_and_subject`: four `CREATE TABLE`s mirroring `session_audience_branch`'s own schema exactly, and one `ALTER TABLE "session" ADD COLUMN "subject_id"` plus its `RESTRICT` foreign key — no existing table altered destructively, no data migrated.

**Revision 160 (Document Owner decisions — a batch of eight reported items across نقاط الاختبارات, the الجدولة occurrence-edit form, registration, بناء الاختبارات, مستجدّات and the content library, ratified 2026-09-16):** **SIX OF EIGHT IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED (§1, §5, §6, §7, §8, and §3 as generalised by Revision 161 below); TWO HELD FOR OWNER CONFIRMATION (§2, §4) — each is a real schema or workflow decision, not a rendering choice, and neither was guessed at while the Owner was unavailable to confirm it.**

**§1 — نقاط الاختبارات no longer offers a redirect to a second screen, and stops offering a button that opens nothing.** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND.** Two things, both reported together: (a) the exam-wide «فتح في بناء الاختبارات» link (Revision 158 §5's own relabelling of it) is REMOVED outright, along with بناء الاختبارات's own students-and-answers inbox table it pointed at (`assessments.inbox`/`assessments.eligible`, `OnePaper`'s `SubmissionDialog`) — نقاط الاختبارات now lists the same students with their submission status AND their grade in one place, so a second, poorer listing of the same students on a different screen was removed rather than kept beside it (§20 rule 16 does not apply to a capability the newer screen has fully absorbed). `frozen`'s own freeze-once-anyone-answers check (R124) still reads `rows` from the SAME `listSubmissions` fetch — that call, and the fixture it drives, is kept. (b) `GradeSheetRow` gained a new field, `submitted` (`readGradeSheet`, `grade.service.ts`) — `true` exactly when `StudentExamSubmission.state !== 'in_progress'`, the IDENTICAL predicate `readSubmission` itself refuses on, computed for every online exam's audience in one extra query and left absent entirely for a physical sitting (nothing to ask about). نقاط الاختبارات's table gained a «حالة الإجابة» column showing it, and the per-row «عرض الإجابات» button is now withheld for a student who has not submitted — there is nothing for it to open.

**§2 — the `multi_dimension` picker's own filter UX: explained, and a redesign held for confirmation.** The Owner asked what Revision 157 §2's own `multiDimensionHint` sentence (*"الفروع والفئات والمستويات والمجموعات الإدارية تتقاطع فيما بينها؛ الحلقات تُضاف إليها دون أن تُقلّص الجمهور"*) actually means, and separately asked that the picker never present a "teaching mode" choice at all — every filter shown directly, «الكل» pre-selected everywhere, and choosing a value in one filter narrowing what the OTHERS offer.

**The explanation, traced to `audienceWhere`'s own `multi_dimension` case (`roster-resolution.ts`):** naming a branch and/or category and/or level and/or administrative group builds ONE enrolment condition — a student's SINGLE enrolment row must satisfy every one of them at once (an intersection: *"women, at level X, at branch Y"* — one precise target). Naming a circle on top does not narrow that further; it is UNIONED in as *"and these named people too"*, because a Teaching Circle is already a fixed, Level-locked roster rather than another axis to filter on. **This does follow what the Owner described**: the cascading UX she asked for (choosing a category narrows the level list to only levels that exist under it, and so on) is the natural UI complement of an intersection — narrowing the options TO exactly the combinations that could match is what makes an AND-composed picker usable, and a circle needs no cascading at all since it is added regardless of the other four.

**What is NOT yet built, pending confirmation rather than guessed at:** collapsing the three teaching-mode choices (`entire_level`/`administrative_group`/`multi_dimension`) into always showing the five filters directly is a real behavioural change, not only a label change — `multi_dimension` can already represent a single Level or a single Administrative Group as a degenerate case (one dimension array filled, the rest empty), so the collapse is mechanically possible. But `scheduling.tsx`'s own `MODES` are ALSO the exact set a self-service Teacher is offered (`modes={canAssignStaff ? MODES : (['entire_level'] as const)}`) — always showing all five filters to everyone would hand a self-service Teacher controls (naming other branches, other categories, other groups, circles) §4.4c's own scope check may refuse server-side but that a menu should not offer in the first place (rule O). True cascading (each filter's OPTIONS narrowed by every OTHER filter's current selection, not just parent-to-child) additionally needs a live faceted-options read this platform does not have today — the existing chained reads (`scope.options.groupId`) only narrow child-from-parent (Level+branch → group), not the reverse. Confirmed as the intended shape before being built.

**§3 — تعديل الحصة (editing one occurrence) cannot override its audience-shape fields; two of the fields named already can, elsewhere.** Checked field by field against `schedule-sessions.tsx`'s `ScopeDialog` (`this_session` scope): **طريقة الحضور and القاعة are already editable there** (`DeliverySection`, rendered and forwarded unconditionally for `this_session` — R97/R43.4's own per-occurrence snapshot-plus-`overridden` mechanism). **الأستاذ المسؤول والمؤطرون المساعدون are already editable, through a separate «طاقم التدريس» row action** (`SessionStaff`, R91 §11 — a per-occurrence snapshot on the identical footing). **فروع has a NARROW existing override — branches only** (`SessionAudienceBranch`, R92, reached through a separate «الجمهور» row action) — its own comment states the boundary explicitly: *"Combining two Administrative Groups across branches is a different business question... R92 §B6 says to implement the whole-Level case and report the rest rather than invent semantics nobody asked for."* **فئات، مستويات، مجموعات مستوى، حلقات، نمط التدريس والمادة have NO per-occurrence override at all** — `Session` carries no category/level/administrative-group/teaching-group/subject/teaching-mode column; every occurrence inherits these live from its schedule with no exception. **Now built, on explicit Owner instruction — see Revision 161.**

Generalising R92's branch override to the other four dimensions does **not** conflict with §20 rule 22 (*"a session's audience is never snapshotted, because enrolment changes must be reflected live"*) — `SessionAudienceBranch` is not a snapshot of WHO, only of WHICH branches to resolve live against, exactly as `multi_dimension`'s own five dimensions already resolve live at the schedule level (Revision 155/157); the same shape, scoped to one occurrence, would resolve live too. It IS a new schema decision (new join tables mirroring `SessionAudienceBranch` for level/category/administrative-group/teaching-group, service changes to `audienceForSession`, and UI to reach them from `تعديل الحصة`) on the exact boundary R92's own comment marked as deliberately deferred — reported here rather than built, on that same convention.

**§4 — the registration form admits only one requested role per submission, and the blocker is a single column, not the form.** `User.requestedRole` (`schema.prisma`) is a single nullable `String?` — one pending role per account, read and written as a scalar everywhere a registration is created, listed or approved (`registration.service.ts`, the approvals controller, the notification triggers). A person who is, at once, a beneficiary AND a هيئة التأطير applicant cannot ask for both in one request today; she must choose, and whichever she does not choose she must return and request separately (or never, if she does not realise she could). Supporting several requested roles at once — the Owner's own shape: request any subset of «مستفيدة»/«ابن أو ابنة»/«مؤطرة»/«مديرة» in one submission, one full-outer-join form with no field asked twice, and a super admin who may approve any subset — needs `requestedRole` widened to a SET (a join table, most likely, since a role can be independently approved or declined) and every one of those call sites updated to a plural read, plus a real answer to what a PARTIAL approval leaves behind (does the account activate on its first approved role while the rest stay pending and re-approvable later, or must the whole set be decided together?). This is a schema-and-workflow decision with more than one defensible shape, reported rather than guessed at, on the same convention as §2/§3 above.

**§5 — the self-managed-account claim option is hidden from the registration dropdown, not removed.** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, FRONTEND ONLY.** `register.kindSelfManaged` (R132) is withdrawn from the `SelectField`'s own options array; every part of the capability it names — `intent === 'self_managed'`'s validation, `requestSelfManagedClaim`, the admin review queue at `/admin/self-managed-claims` — is untouched, reachable the moment the option is restored. Deferred for this release on the Owner's own word, not because anything about it was found wrong.

**§6 — بناء الاختبارات's question list redesigned; the rest of the screen was found already in reasonable shape.** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, FRONTEND ONLY.** `<ol className="assessment-questions">` had carried NO stylesheet rule at all since it was written — a genuinely bare, un-styled list, which is what actually made the screen read as "messy and very basic" rather than any of its data or actions being wrong. Each question is now its own bordered card with a numbered circle (CSS `counter`, not a second copy of `{n}` in the markup), its kind/points shown as the platform's own badge chips instead of muted, dash-separated text, and its edit/reorder/remove actions in one row below a hairline instead of a run-on paragraph of ghost buttons. The exam's own status/target summary above the list, and the library's own filterable table one screen up, were read and left as they were — a working, ordinary `DataTable` is not the defect being reported. No field, handler, request or i18n string changed; every `t()` call and every prop passed to `move`/`act`/`removeQuestion`/`QuestionDialog` is identical.

**§7 — مستجدّات redesigned for scannability, on the same rules AG already binds it to.** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, FRONTEND ONLY.** Each notice is now an icon beside a text column, in its own soft card — the icon is a NEW category glyph (`CATEGORY_ICON`, a `Record` over `NotificationItem['type']` mirroring `HEADLINE_KEYS`'s own exhaustiveness discipline, so a future notification type without one fails the type check the same way a headline-less one already does), grouped into three: `user` (registration/family-link/role/ownership/enrolment facts), `calendar` (session/event/exam scheduling facts — a NEW icon added to the shared set), `book` (grade/assessment facts). **The unread marker stays exactly what [AG](development/ux-architecture.md#ag--a-notification-is-a-delivered-fact-and-the-screen-must-not-pretend-otherwise) requires** — the inline-start border, a shape and not only a colour — with a soft background tint added as a second, non-load-bearing cue; nothing about WHEN a notice is marked read, or whether a read one still renders, changed. Every headline/reason string is byte-for-byte unchanged — this is a container and spacing change, not a copy change.

**§8 — the content library's «تنزيل الملف» opened the file instead of downloading it.** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND.** The button reused the SAME presigned URL the previewer renders inline with (`fetchContentUrl` → `mintDownloadUrl` → `presignGetUrl`), minted with no `Content-Disposition` at all — a browser that can render the MIME type (a PDF, an image) simply displayed it again, exactly as the previewer intends for THAT surface. `presignGetUrl` gained an optional `attachmentFilename` parameter (sets `ResponseContentDisposition: attachment; filename="…"` on the presigned `GetObjectCommand`, using the storage key's own already-slugified filename segment — `buildStorageKey`'s output, so no new lookup); `mintDownloadUrl` gained a `disposition: 'inline' | 'attachment'` parameter forwarding it, exposed as `GET /content/{id}/download-url?disposition=attachment`. The download button now mints a SEPARATE, freshly-fetched `attachment` URL on click rather than reusing the preview's own `inline` one.

**Verified so far (§1, §5, §6, §7, §8):** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; frontend unit 1,354/1,354. New coverage: `grade.http.integration.test.ts` (§1b — `submitted` true only for a real, non-`in_progress` submission row, false for an unsubmitted student in the same audience, absent entirely on a physical sitting; a pre-existing teardown gap in this same file — `clear()` never cleared `StudentExamSubmission`/`StudentExamAnswer` before deleting the exams that RESTRICT against them — was found and fixed alongside, since this is the first test in the file to ever write one), `grade-sheet.test.ts` and `assessment-ui.test.ts` (§1a — the link and the redirect are gone; the removed table's own test coverage updated to say why rather than deleted silently), `register.test.tsx` (§5 — the option is absent from the rendered dropdown while `validate`/`buildPayload`'s own `self_managed` coverage, tested directly above it, proves the state machine is untouched), `content.integration.test.ts` (§8 — a real MinIO round trip: the default mint carries no `content-disposition` header, the `attachment` mint carries exactly `attachment; filename="…"`), `content-preview-dialog.download.test.tsx` (§8 — source-pinned: the click handler never reuses `load.url`, always calls `fetchContentUrl(..., 'attachment')`, and disables itself while the fetch is in flight), the existing `notification-list.test.tsx`/`notification-types.test.ts` (§7 — unaffected: neither test asserted the removed inline markup, and the type-safe `CATEGORY_ICON` map introduces no gap `notification-types.test.ts`'s own cross-check would have caught). §6/§7's own visual results each checked against a static harness built from the real compiled stylesheet (`scripts/dev/browser`'s own established harness pattern), not the Owner's populated local stack. Full disposable-stack integration re-run pending until the batch reaches a deploy boundary; see `docs/CHANGES.log` for the run this revision's completed sub-items actually shipped under.

**Revision 159 (Document Owner decisions — five reported fixes across نقاط الاختبارات, حصص الجدول, الجدولة's dialogs, the two main navs, and an exam's own access window, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND WHERE NEEDED.**

**§1 — a misleading sentence removed.** The question-freeze notice on بناء الاختبارات (`assessments.frozen`) said "أجابت مستفيدة واحدة على الأقل، فلم تعد الأسئلة قابلة للتعديل. هذا يحمي ما أجبن به." — the Owner asked for the second sentence removed; the first (the actual freeze rule, R124) is unchanged.

**§2 — حصص الجدول's «المؤطِّرات» column showed a bare count under a header that names people.** `listScheduleSessions` (`course-schedule.service.ts`) never joined `SessionStaff.user`, so `schedule-sessions.tsx` had nothing to render but `staff.length`. Fixed with the SAME `user: { select: { nameArabic: true } }` join and `user_name` DTO field `listCourseSchedules` already carries for its own staff list (Revision 155's own equivalent fix, applied here too — this list was missed at the time).

**§3 — الجدولة's occurrence-detail dialog, four fixes in one component.** (a) `التوقيت`'s value carried a stray `dir="ltr"` that put its own logical "start" margin on the wrong side of an RTL row, visually separating it from every other field's value — removed; a plain `HH:MM — HH:MM` string needs no direction override to read left-to-right. (b) `التاريخ`'s Hijri half rendered the raw ministry identifier (`"1448-03-22"`) with no separator from the Gregorian date beside it (the same stray-`dir` margin bug), reading as one run-together string — reformatted into the `day month year` label `day-events-dialog.tsx`'s own Hijri renderer already uses, with an explicit separator rather than a margin. (c) An exam occurrence named no one: `ExamStaff` (supervisor/assistant) was never surfaced at all, on the reasoning `instructors` means *who teaches this* and nobody teaches an exam (§4.6) — correct, and left unexpanded rather than reused; a NEW field, `supervisors`, carries them instead, in their own dialog row, for every kind (empty for a Session/Event). (d) Room and category were checked and found ALREADY shown for a Session and a holiday respectively — nothing to add there; reported rather than guessed at.

**§4 — the two main navs, reordered and reconciled.** بناء الاختبارات and نقاط الاختبارات (renamed from «نقاط الامتحانات» to match `/teacher/exams`'s own label exactly) move to the end of both the admin/super_admin sidebar and the teacher menu, after الجدولة and مكتبة المحتوى — the Owner's own stated order, identical in both dashboards now. **Two pre-existing §14.1 documentation gaps closed alongside this, found while updating the sitemap for the move**: `/admin/assessments` and `/teacher/assessments` (both live since R124) were never listed in §14.1's own sitemap text at all — the same "rule P" gap R105/R110/M4c's own entries in this document already record for other nodes that shipped without a sitemap update.

**§5 — an exam's «بدء الاختبار» button now respects its own scheduled end.** It was gated on `available_from` alone (R136 clause 16/17 — *is it open*) and never asked *is it still going on* — a sitting opened at 09:00 for a 09:00–10:00 window still offered "بدء الاختبار" at noon. Past the sitting's own `end_time` (local wall-clock, TD-11), the button is withdrawn for everyone, and a student/parent sees «عرض الاختبار» instead — the SAME `/dashboard/student/assessments?exam=` deep link, which `studentPaper`'s own contract already renders read-only once a submission exists (*"eligibility decides may I start, never may I see what I wrote"*, `assessment.service.ts`) — so this is a NEW route to an existing capability, not a new one.
