**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; frontend unit 1,348/1,348; new coverage in `event-details-dialog`'s own test suite (the ended-gate's three states: still open, past end with no active role, past end for a student/parent — proving the exact fixture regression this would have produced against the file's own pre-existing `date: '2026-06-15'` default, now overridden to 2099 in every unaffected test), `calendar.http.integration.test.ts`/`visibility-matrix.http.integration.test.ts` (an exam's own `supervisors` field, proved never folded into `instructors`), and `admin-modules.test.ts`/`teacher-modules.test.ts` (the new pinned order, in both dashboards). Full disposable-stack integration suite verified green (see the batch-level verification note below, shared across this Revision, 157 and 158). No migration for §1/§4/§5 (frontend/copy only); §2/§3 are additive backend reads, no schema change.

**Revision 158 (Document Owner decision — نقاط الاختبارات gets a per-student "view her responses and grade them" dialog, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, FRONTEND ONLY — no new endpoint.**

**§1 — the gap was never the data; it was that grading and reading were on two different screens.** بناء الاختبارات's own `OnePaper` already had a full submissions inbox with a per-student, read-only `SubmissionDialog` (`GET /assessments/{id}/submissions/{studentId}`, R148's supervisor-widened read); نقاط الاختبارات's own per-question inputs (Revision 153's own report/154's context) already existed, but blind — a table of bare number boxes with no question text and no answer beside them, and the one "عرض الإجابات" button on the page was a full-page redirect to the OTHER screen. The two capabilities existed; reaching both without leaving نقاط الاختبارات did not.

**§2 — one row, one dialog, reusing what already existed rather than a second reader.** Each row of نقاط الاختبارات (online exams only — a physical sitting has no submission to open) now carries its own «عرض الإجابات», opening a dialog that fetches the SAME submission read `OnePaper` uses and renders question+answer in the identical shape `SubmissionDialog` already established (prompt, chosen option(s), free text, justification) — not a second implementation of that view, an editable one. Each question with R137 points allocated gets its own grade input beside its answer, pre-seeded from whatever draft (or already-saved) score exists.

**§3 — two ways to mark her, both legal, on the SAME rule the table already enforced, now visible.** `entryPayload` (extracted from the bulk save, now shared by both) already decided: any filled per-question score sums to the total and wins; an empty set of them leaves the plain total field in charge. The dialog states this explicitly (`totalOverrideHint`) rather than leaving a marker to infer it, and adds the plain total field even when the exam uses points — the table itself only ever offered ONE or the other depending on whether points existed at all, never both at once.

**§4 — the dialog's own «حفظ» commits immediately, as a draft, for that student alone** — not a staged change waiting for the page's bulk «حفظ». `saveGradeDraft` (`grade.service.ts`) already upserts whichever entries a `PUT /exams/{id}/grades` call names and leaves every other student's row untouched; the dialog sends a one-entry array through the identical adapter call the bulk save uses, so nothing here is a second write path. Publishing stays the existing page-level action, applied to whatever the drafts (dialog-saved or bulk-saved) currently hold.

**§5 — the exam-wide link is kept, not removed, and now says what it actually does.** It still opens بناء الاختبارات's own submissions inbox for a bulk, side-by-side browse of every answer — a real capability the per-row dialog does not replace (§20 rule 16 cuts both ways) — relabelled «فتح في بناء الاختبارات» so it is never confused with the per-row «عرض الإجابات», which now means something different (grading, not merely reading).

**Verified:** frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; frontend unit 1,348/1,348, including new source-pinned coverage (`grade-sheet.test.ts`) proving: the shared `entryPayload` extraction changed no BR-7 behaviour (the empty-vs-zero rule, restated against its new location), the per-row button is physical-exam-aware, the dialog's own save sends exactly one entry built by the identical helper the bulk save uses, the manual-total path is offered explicitly, and the two links now carry deliberately different labels. No backend change and no migration — every read/write this dialog uses already existed.

**Revision 157 (Document Owner decision — SRS Revision 155 completed end to end: `this_and_future` splitting AND the admin scheduling form's own picker, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND.** Resolves Revision 155 §4 and §5, both explicitly deferred at the time as their own design questions rather than guessed at.

**§1 — `this_and_future` reaches a `multi_dimension` schedule, the same way the legacy three-arm identity change already did.** `splitCourseSchedule`'s own `multi_dimension` refusal (Revision 155 §4) is gone; in its place, `resolveTarget`'s existing `multi_dimension` case (unchanged) is reused for the successor exactly as the legacy modes already reuse it. Absent an explicit rename, the successor's dimensions are the predecessor's own, read once via `scheduleDimensions` and carried forward untouched — never zero join rows, which the deferred DB trigger would refuse. Named explicitly (`teaching_mode: 'multi_dimension'` + `dimensions`, the SAME exclusivity CREATE's own schema enforces, restated on the split schema), the successor gets the NEW dimensions instead, validated against every effective Level the new set implies (looped, not `[0]` alone — a multi-dimension class can name several).

**§2 — the admin scheduling form's own picker**, built where CREATE already accepted the capability but no screen offered it (rule P, the same shape R105/R110's own entries in this document record repeatedly). Five independent `MultiSelectField` controls — mirroring `ActivitySection`'s own dimension pattern, but never that component directly: its composition rule (pure OR, `scopeAllLevelsHint`) is the WRONG one here (AND-across-kind, a Teaching Circle unions instead — Revision 155 §2's own rule, restated as `multiDimensionHint` beside the picker). Administrative-group and circle OPTIONS are read UNSCOPED (`listAdministrativeGroups`/`listCircles`, both extended with an optional `pageSize`, called with 100 rather than the ordinary 25) — the chained `scope.options.groupId` `ActivitySection` itself uses needs a Level AND a branch already chosen before it answers anything (§4.4c: a group is a roster at a premises), which this dialog never sets for `multi_dimension`. A representative Level is synced into `scope.value.levelId` purely to keep the Subject picker sensible while the dialog is open — a form convenience, never the authority: the server still validates the Subject against every effective Level on save, exactly as §1 restates.

**§3 — locked (editing) states the fact plainly, on the SAME rule `ActivitySection`'s own locked scope already established.** §4.4 freezes a class's scope at creation; five empty, disabled pickers would look exactly like an audience nobody had chosen for a row that in fact has one — the identical `admin.calendar.scopeFixed` sentence Event's own locked scope renders replaces the pickers here too, rather than seeding five arrays from a row whose real dimensions this pass deliberately does not thread into edit-time state (matching R139's own "never on edit" rule for Events).

**§4 — a list-view display gap found and closed alongside this**: a `multi_dimension` row's `target_name` is `''` by construction (no single target exists to name) — the list's «الجهة المعنية» column rendered a blank cell rather than the empty-string check the `??` fallback already used for `null`. Fixed with the mode's own label as a plain, honest fallback; the real five-dimension breakdown is seen by opening the class.

**Verified:** backend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; frontend unit 1,348/1,348, including new coverage: `course-schedule.http.integration.test.ts` (§1 — carry-forward, explicit rename, a subject-not-taught refusal on rename, and the exclusivity rule extended to the split schema) and `class-section.multi-dimension.test.tsx` (§2/§3 — all five pickers render together, the legacy pair is unaffected for every other mode, the locked sentence replaces them on edit, and the submitted payload/validation/unscoped-read wiring, source-pinned against `scheduling.tsx` the same way `class-section.scope.test.tsx` already established for Events). Full disposable-stack integration suite: **2,631/2,649 passed** before this batch (Revisions 155–156's own last confirmed run); re-verified green after this batch and Revisions 158–159 together — see `docs/CHANGES.log` for the exact re-run figure. All 31 guards, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass. Migration: none new — §1 uses the existing `dimensions`/`courseScheduleDimensions` schema Revision 155 already shipped.

**Revision 156 (Document Owner decision — a `created_by`/`created_at` audit trail for anything a real person manually creates or deletes, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND ONLY. THIRTY-ONE NEW ADDITIVE COLUMNS, NO NEW TABLES.** Resolves the audit-trail item Revision 153's own report (above) deliberately left as "a separate, larger decision" pending explicit instruction — now given.

**§1 — every Prisma model classified MANUAL/AUTOMATIC/already-unambiguous before a single column was added**, rather than adding `created_by` uniformly. `deletedAt`/`deletedById` were already found (Revision 153's report) to be the near-universal convention (35/37 soft-deletable models); this revision's own audit found the inverse is true of `createdById` — genuinely absent everywhere except the two `Legal*` models. Three buckets, evidence-based (grep + read, not assumed): **(a) MANUAL** — a real person's deliberate action, no existing column already answers "who": the 31 models listed in §2 gained `created_by`. **(b) AUTOMATIC** — a system process with no independent lifecycle of its own creation (`Notification`, `Session`/`SessionStaff`'s materialization path, `EventBranch`/`EventCategory`/`EventLevel`/`EventAdministrativeGroup`'s pure side-effect joins of one parent transaction) — explicitly excluded by the Owner's own wording ("not notifications, as they get created automatically, not manually by a user") and generalised to every model sharing that same shape. **(c) already unambiguous** — a domain-specific actor column already names the "who" under its own name (`Attendance.markedById`, `QuranProgressLog.loggedById`, `ConsentRecord.grantedByUserId`), or the row's own foreign key already is the actor (`StudentExamSubmission`/`ChildApplication`/`SelfManagedClaim` are self-service — `studentId`/`parentId` already says who).

**§2 — the 31 models: `User`, `Partner`, `UserBranchRole`, `Branch`, `Room`, `Category`, `Level`, `Subject`, `LevelSubject`, `LevelSurah`, `AdministrativeGroup`, `TeachingGroup`, `StudentTeachingGroup`, `Enrollment`, `RecurringCourseSchedule`, `CourseScheduleStaff`, `SchedulingType`, `Event`, `Exam`, `ExamQuestion`, `ExamQuestionOption`, `AcademicPeriod`, `AcademicYear`, `EducationalContent`, `ExamStaff`, `EventStaff`, `HijriMonthStart`, `Grade`, `SessionContent`, `SessionStaff`, `FamilyLink`.** `FamilyLink` is the one non-obvious inclusion: `createLink`'s own comment already distinguishes a staff member acting on someone else's relationship from the family's own self-service request elsewhere, so `created_by` is real new information there, unlike e.g. `ChildApplication` where `parentId` already is the answer.

**§3 — `created_by` follows the SAME bare-scalar-no-FK convention `deleted_by` already established**, not a new one: 33 of 35 existing `deletedById` columns carry no Postgres foreign key at all (only `User.deletedBy`'s self-relation and `Trash.deletedBy` do), deliberately — `docs/architecture/security.md`'s stated principle against duplicating actor columns the audit log already reconstructs, and to avoid a `RESTRICT` blocking account erasure for any staff member who ever created or deleted anything. `created_by` is added identically: `ADD COLUMN "created_by" UUID` with no constraint, everywhere.

**§4 — a real, independent gap found and fixed while wiring this, not part of the ask itself:** `SessionStaff`'s one genuine MANUAL path — `session.service.ts`'s `replaceSessionStaff`, the per-occurrence override behind `PATCH /sessions/:id`'s `staff` field — never recorded `deletedById` on a dropped name (unlike every other soft-delete in the codebase, automatic materialization aside) and, once `created_by` existed to check against, was found to leave a REVIVED row's `created_by` at whatever it was before (`NULL`, for a row `Session`'s own automatic materialization had created) rather than crediting the admin who just re-added that person. Both are now set on their respective branch (`deletedById`/`createdById` on removal, `createdById` on revival — `deletedAt`/`deletedById` are still cleared on revival, unchanged); `replaceSessionStaff`'s signature widened to take the acting admin's id, both call sites (`overrideSession`, `regenerateOne`'s per-session block) updated to pass it.

**Verified:** backend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; full disposable-stack integration suite, 2,631/2,649 (18 pre-existing skips), all-table isolation intact; new coverage in `effective-staffing.http.integration.test.ts` (a second `PATCH /sessions/:id` override, proving the removed cover's row records who removed her and the replacement's row records who added her) and `course-schedule.http.integration.test.ts` (a `multi_dimension` create's row carries `created_by`). Not independently re-tested for all 31 models individually — the wiring is mechanically identical at every site (`data: { ...fields, createdById: actor.userId }` at each already-tested `.create()` call, verified file-by-file against `tsc --noEmit` as it was added) and every existing create-path test continues to pass unchanged, which is the same standard Revision 153's `deletedById` columns were held to when they were added. **The revival half of §4 was found BY the new test above, not before it**: written first assuming the revived row would already carry `created_by`, the test failed against a materialized `SessionStaff` row `Session`'s own automatic path had created with no creator recorded — exposing that the "revival" branch of `replaceSessionStaff` reset `deletedAt`/`deletedById` but never set `createdById`, fixed in the same pass. All 31 guards, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass. Migration: `20260916120000_r156_created_by_audit_trail` (additive only, no companion constraints file needed — no CHECK or trigger references `created_by`).

**Revision 155 (Document Owner decision — a class's multi-dimension targeting: branches, categories, levels, administrative groups and teaching circles, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND ONLY. ONE NEW ENUM VALUE, FIVE NEW ADDITIVE TABLES.** Resolves A3, the largest item this batch's own investigation (Revision 152 §2's context) had flagged as needing a concrete plan before any schema was written.

**§1 — a fourth, additive `teaching_mode` arm, mirroring §7's Event join tables exactly** (R24's own precedent, applied to a new entity rather than reinvented: an explicit join per dimension, never a generic polymorphic scope table). The three legacy single-target arms (`entire_level`/`administrative_group`/`teaching_group`) and their own CHECK constraint are completely untouched — proved by the full existing test suite passing unchanged. `multi_dimension` leaves all three legacy target columns `NULL` and resolves entirely through five new tables (`course_schedule_branch`/`_category`/`_level`/`_administrative_group`/`_teaching_group`), each Cascade-on-schedule-delete/Restrict-on-target-delete, matching `EventBranch`/`EventCategory`/`EventLevel`/`EventAdministrativeGroup`'s own shapes exactly. A deferred DB trigger backstops "at least one of the five" at commit time; the service layer separately requires at least one of level/group/circle specifically (`MULTI_DIMENSION_NEEDS_A_LEVEL`) — a class delivers a curriculum Subject to a real population, which branches/categories alone cannot name, the one place this diverges from Event's own looser "any combination, including none but Global" rule.

**§2 — the composition rule, extended by exactly one new case Event never needed.** `audienceWhere`'s new `multi_dimension` arm mirrors `eventAudienceWhere`'s own AND-across-kind/OR-within-kind composition for branches/categories/levels/administrative groups precisely. The one genuinely new rule: a named Teaching Circle **unions** with the rest rather than intersecting — a circle is already Level-locked (§4.4c), so "Level A and Circle C" reads as *these two populations together*, not their (frequently empty) overlap. Proved directly (`roster-resolution.integration.test.ts`) and again through the teacher-scope arm (`studentsTaughtBy`), which gained the identical fourth arm the three legacy modes already had.

**§3 — `branchId`/`roomId` stay exactly what they always were**: the class's PHYSICAL meeting place, unrelated to and unconstrained by the new audience dimensions. A `multi_dimension` class may reach an audience wider than the branch it physically meets at, by the Owner's own request — the old `administrative_group` mode's branch-match check is deliberately not reapplied here.

**§4 — deliberately not supported yet: R50 `this_and_future` splitting of a `multi_dimension` schedule**, on either side of the split. The predecessor's identity-carry-forward logic (`existing.levelId ?? existing.administrativeGroupId ?? existing.teachingGroupId` as a single fallback target) has no meaning once a schedule's real target is five join tables — extending it correctly is its own design question, not a guess grafted onto the legacy three-arm logic. Refused with a named reason (`MULTI_DIMENSION_SPLIT_NOT_SUPPORTED`) rather than silently mis-splitting; the full-series edit (`scope: 'all_sessions'`) is unaffected and remains available for a `multi_dimension` class's whole life.

**§5 — deliberately BACKEND ONLY in this pass, reported rather than assumed complete.** The wire contract is fully built and tested end-to-end (`POST`/`PATCH /admin/course-schedules`, the list and write DTOs both carry a `dimensions` field), so the capability is real and reachable by any caller of the API today. What is NOT built: the admin scheduling form's own multi-select picker for choosing `multi_dimension`'s five dimensions when creating or editing a class through the ordinary UI. `ActivitySection` (`class-section.tsx`) already has the closest precedent — a generic `MultiSelectField`-per-dimension renderer — but its combination rule is Event's OR-across-everything, not this revision's AND-across-kind/OR-within-kind-except-circles-union rule, and correctly wiring it needs its own pass through `scheduling.tsx`'s option-loading rather than a guess grafted onto a UI built for a different composition rule. Flagged explicitly rather than silently left half-usable.

**Verified:** backend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; full disposable-stack integration suite, 2,631/2,649 (18 pre-existing skips), all-table isolation intact; new coverage in `course-schedule.http.integration.test.ts` (create, the two refusals, the exclusivity rule, the Teacher self-service refusal, the list/roster read) and `roster-resolution.integration.test.ts` (the composition rule directly, and again through teacher scope) — all confirmed against the Document Owner's own worked fixture already in that file. All 31 guards, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass.

**Revision 154 (Document Owner decision — a Teacher may delete her own classes, events and exams, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** Reverses three separately-ratified "deletion stays Admin" decisions: Revision 140 §2 (class), Revision 43/72 and R71.3 (event), R70.4 (exam) — each reported back rather than assumed in Revision 152 §2, now resolved by explicit Document Owner instruction.

**§1 — "her own" is the SAME boundary each kind's edit grant already uses, never a wider one.** No new authorization concept and no schema change: `deleteCourseSchedule` now branches exactly as `updateCourseSchedule` does (`assertTeacherCurrentlyStaffs` for a self-service Teacher, `scope.assertCanActOnBranch` for a manager, unchanged); `deleteEvent`/the exported `assertMayDeleteEvent` role gate now admits a Teacher, who must additionally pass `assertMayEdit`'s existing per-event check (`responsible`, or her own group scope) before the specific row is touched — an Admin's own grant is UNCHANGED (still unconditional, no branch-scope narrowing introduced by this revision); `deleteExam` now calls `assertScope` (§4.4c) instead of a bare branch check, admitting a Teacher within her existing exam-edit scope. `Exam` still carries no `created_by` — R70.4's schema objection stands; the Document Owner's instruction supersedes the POLICY of who may act without needing the column HER decision found unnecessary to add for this.

**§2 — the two error shapes stay whatever each kind's edit already used**, not unified: a class or event outside her scope answers `NOT_FOUND`, exactly as an edit attempt already did; an exam outside her §4.4c scope answers `FORBIDDEN`/`EXAM_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, `assertExamInTeacherScope`'s own long-established shape (confirmed against its own `PATCH` test in the same file). A generic caller outside all three (student, parent, an unrelated teacher, `EventStaff: assistant`) still refuses at the coarse role gate before any per-row check runs.

**§3 — `notifyEventChange`'s post-delete "shall I notify" re-check (R82.5) extends the identical way**, on a closer look at why it exists at all: `assertMayDeleteEvent`'s ROLE gate widens to admit a Teacher, but the actual per-event guarantee — *this caller is the one who deleted THIS event* — was already, independently, enforced by `deletedEventRecipients`'s own `deletedById` match against the tombstoned row and its Trash entry. Widening the coarse gate does not weaken that specific check; a Teacher who did not delete a given event still gets `NOT_FOUND` there, unchanged.

**§4 — the teacher قائمة's حذف action reuses الجدولة's own three pieces of state and one function** (`deleting`/`deleteBlocked`/`notifying`, `confirmDelete`) rather than a second implementation — the identical `deleteSchedulingItem`/`classifyDeletion`/`deletionNotice`/`notifyEventChange` sequence Revision 152 §1 already reused for تعديل.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; frontend unit 1,329/1,329 (including a rewritten `schedules-edit.test.tsx` covering the delete wiring, confirmed to fail without the fix by stash/restore); full disposable-stack integration suite, 2,620/2,638 (18 pre-existing skips), all-table isolation intact, with new coverage in `course-schedule.http.integration.test.ts`, `event.http.integration.test.ts`, `teacher-exam-scope.http.integration.test.ts` — a Teacher deleting her own row of each kind, and being refused one outside her scope with the correct pre-existing error shape; all 31 guards, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass. **Two pre-existing tests corrected, not weakened**: `course-schedule.http.integration.test.ts`'s own Revision 140 test asserted "still cannot delete... even her own class" — updated to assert the new 200, with the unaffected conflicts-preview check reordered before the now-destructive delete call. **`grade.http.integration.test.ts`'s "a Teacher may NOT delete a sitting" used `teacherToken` against the file's own shared `examId` — and that teacher's fixture schedule exactly matches the exam's scope**, so the new grant let the delete actually SUCCEED and remove a fixture roughly a dozen later tests in the same file still depended on, cascading into 11 unrelated failures the first full-suite run surfaced. Split into two: a refusal test against `outsiderToken` (genuinely out of scope, asserting the correct `EXAM_OUT_OF_SCOPE`), and a new success test against a disposable exam created for that purpose alone, never the shared fixture. **One test-isolation gap found and fixed**: `teacher-exam-scope.http.integration.test.ts`'s `clear()` never deleted `trash` rows, harmless until this revision's own delete test created its file's first one — `trash.deleted_by_id` is `Restrict` against `user`, so `clear()`'s `user.deleteMany` began throwing and skipping every cleanup step after it (level/subject/room/branch) on every subsequent run; fixed by deleting matching `trash` rows before `user`, the same ordering `event.http.integration.test.ts`'s own `clear()` already carries. No migration.

**Revision 153 (Document Owner decision — اختباراتي and نقاطي merge into one table, ratified 2026-09-16):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.**

**§1 — one table, not two screens.** `/dashboard/student/grades` («نقاطي») is retired; its published-grades read (`readPublishedGrades`, untouched — §5.3's "published only" rule is unaffected) is now merged, client-side, by exam id, with `/me/assessments`'s existing online-exam read (`assessmentsForStudent`). Neither read was widened in what it is ALLOWED to return — only additive fields were added to each so the merged row has what it needs: `assessmentsForStudent` now also selects `level`/`subject` names (so an ungraded online exam still shows a subject), and `readPublishedGrades` now also selects `exam.mode` (so a published row says which attendance method it was, since نقاطي never needed to before).

**§2 — طريقة الحضور decides what a row offers, never whether it appears.** Every row — either mode, graded or not — states عن بُعد/حضوري. «مراجعة إجاباتي»/«فتح» and الحالة exist only for a remote row: a physical sitting has no in-platform interaction to report. النقطة applies to either mode once published, superseding the old boolean «نقطة منشورة» badge with the actual mark — نقاطي's own richer information, now on every row that has it.

**§3 — sortable on every column**, clicking any header (`sortRows`, the client-owned-list sort already used elsewhere — both reads are unpaginated, so this is not the pattern the same module warns against for a paginated collection).

**§4 — fixed alongside the merge, not sought out separately: a parent acting for a child could not open اختباراتي at all.** `myAssessments` never sent `X-Active-Child-ID`; `resolveActingStudent` refuses a parent-only actor with no header (§4.3) with a `400`. Both reads now carry the header identically — the same fix نقاطي's own read already had, extended to its merge partner.

**§14.1's sitemap and the permission-matrix's `/dashboard/student/grades` references are updated to the merged route and shape; §5's own Revision 151 text (which cited the old route by name) is left as it was, an accurate record of what was true when it was ratified.**

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; backend unit 342/342; frontend unit 1,327/1,327 (12 new — merge, mode-gating, sortability, the child-context fix); full disposable-stack integration 2,613/2,631 (18 pre-existing skips, all-table isolation intact); all 31 guards, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass. Three E2E browser scripts (`verify-portals.mjs`, `verify-journey.mjs`, `verify-grading.mjs`) updated to the merged route — not yet re-run live in this pass; flagged in `docs/TASKS.md`. No migration.

**Revision 152 (Document Owner decision — تقويمي's قائمة gains تعديل, reusing الجدولة's own edit dialog and the server grants it already holds, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, FRONTEND ONLY. NO MIGRATION.** Checkpoint 4 of the batch Revision 147 deferred — the smaller, unblocked half of B1; the حذف half is §2 below, explicitly NOT implemented and returned to the Document Owner rather than assumed.

**§1 — تعديل, a pure frontend-reuse task.** `PATCH /admin/course-schedules/{id}` already tolerates a Teacher who currently staffs the class (`assertTeacherCurrentlyStaffs`, Revision 140 §2); Event `assertMayEdit` already admits a Teacher in her own scope (own Administrative Groups, or named `responsible`, R71.2); Exam `updatePhysicalExam`'s `assertCanManage`/`assertScope` already admit a Teacher within her §4.4c scope. None of the three needed a server change — only `/teacher/schedules`' قائمة never offered a row action to reach any of them. The teacher page's قائمة now wires the identical `SchedulingDialog` الجدولة's own قائمة already opens in edit mode (`item={row}`), with the same `teachingContexts` derivation её own create-flow already built, so an exam or class she edits still resolves Level/Subject/Branch/Year from a class she teaches rather than from `/admin/levels`, which 403s for her.

**§2 — حذف is deliberately NOT added, on any of the three kinds, and the gap is reported rather than closed by inference.** Investigation confirmed all three row kinds' deletion stays server-refused for a Teacher, each by a separately dated, reasoned Document Owner decision: class deletion is Teacher ⊘ by Revision 140 §2's own text ("deletion is untouched... the ratified grant is creation and operational editing, not every write TD-2's row could theoretically cover"); Event deletion is Admin-only by Revision 43/72 and R71.3 (a `responsible` Teacher's grant was scoped to edit, never delete); Exam deletion is Admin-and-above by R70.4, which states in terms that `Exam` carries no `created_by` column, so "her own but not another's" cannot be expressed against this schema without a schema change. Wiring حذف into the teacher list unmodified would 403 at the server on every row it touched, and granting it for real would mean reversing three independently-ratified boundaries — exactly the class of decision §20 reserves for the Document Owner, not an implementing agent's inference from "reuse the same actions." **Open question returned to the Document Owner**: whether Event/Exam حذف should be granted to a Teacher for rows she is responsible for/staffs (and, for Exam specifically, on what scoping basis given the missing `created_by`), and whether B1 was intended to also reopen the already-once-ratified class-delete `⊘` (Revision 140 §2).

**Verified:** frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full frontend unit suite 1,319/1,319, including a new `schedules-edit.test.tsx` (the edit action opens `SchedulingDialog` on the clicked row; حذف is never wired — no `common.delete` label, no `setDeleting`; both dialogs reload the catalogue and the raw rows on save) confirmed to fail without the fix by a stash/restore round trip. No backend change, no migration — the server already granted تعديل.

**Revision 151 (Document Owner decision — per-question grading and publishing, wherever an exam's questions carry R137's optional points, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. ONE NEW TABLE, ADDITIVE.** Checkpoint 3 of the batch Revision 147 deferred — the harder remaining half of the "viewing responses and per-question grading" item; response-VIEWING's own reachability gap was Revision 150 §1.

**§1 — a new table, purely additive, never trigger-derived.** `grade_question_score` (migration `20260915180000_r151_grade_question_score`) holds one row per `(Grade, ExamQuestion)` a marker has actually entered a score for: `grade_id` (Cascade — the breakdown is part of the Grade, not independent), `question_id` (Restrict, matching every other reference to a question), `score NUMERIC(6,2)` with the same `>= 0` CHECK `grade.score` itself already carries, `@@unique(grade_id, question_id)`. `Grade.score` remains the single source of truth for the total; nothing here is trigger-maintained — the service computes the sum and writes both inside the same transaction `saveGradeDraft` already runs in, matching how every other write in this schema already works, since no "maintain an aggregate from child rows" mechanism exists anywhere to extend.

**§2 — the all-or-nothing points rule (R137) is re-checked defensively, never assumed.** R137's own consistency check (`assertQuestionPointsConsistent`, exported and reused rather than restated) runs only at a REMOTE exam's publish time; a PHYSICAL sitting's questions are never verified against it at all. `readGradeSheet`/`saveGradeDraft` both call a new `pointsBreakdown` helper that re-runs the check on every read/write and treats an inconsistent or unallocated configuration as *"this exam does not use points"* rather than surfacing a content problem on a marking screen — the correct place to discover such a problem is بناء الاختبارات, never نقاط الاختبارات.

**§3 — the wire contract.** `GradeSheet` gains an optional `questions: {id, prompt, points}[]`, present only when the exam qualifies; each `GradeSheetRow` gains a matching optional `question_scores`. `PUT /exams/{id}/grades`'s `GradeEntry` gains `question_scores`, which **replaces `score` entirely when sent** — the sum is computed server-side and `score` is ignored, never trusted from the caller. Refused (`VALIDATION_FAILED`) when the exam does not use points at all (`QUESTIONS_HAVE_NO_POINTS`), when a value exceeds THAT QUESTION's own points rather than the exam's maximum (`QUESTION_SCORE_OUT_OF_RANGE`), when a question id is repeated (`QUESTION_SCORE_DUPLICATE`), or alongside `absent: true` (a validator-level refusal, matching the identical existing rule for `score`+`absent`). **A partial breakdown — some questions scored, others not yet — is a legal draft**, exactly as an ordinary whole-exam save may leave students unmarked; the total is simply the running sum of whatever has been entered. **Replaced whole on every save**, on the same convention `staff`/every other whole-row field already follows: a question dropped from the payload no longer holds a score.

**§4 — the frontend.** `GradeSheetView` renders one column per question (labelled with its own points) when `sheet.questions` is present; the existing "mark" column becomes a read-only, live-computed sum instead of a second editable total. Sending an entirely empty breakdown as `[]` is deliberately avoided — that would tell the server *"her total is 0"*, exactly the *unmarked* vs *marked zero* distinction BR-7 exists to keep apart — so nothing entered yet still sends `score: null` with no `question_scores` key at all, on the identical path an ordinary unmarked row already takes.

**§5 — deliberately not extended: the student's own published-grades list** (`readPublishedGrades`, `/dashboard/student/grades`). Adding a per-question breakdown there would need a new per-exam drill-down screen a student has never had, not merely a list extension — a genuinely different, larger feature the Owner did not ask for ("per-question grading AND PUBLISHING" names the staff-facing half). Left for its own future request rather than built now on an inference.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full backend unit suite 342/342; full frontend unit suite 1,315/1,315; full disposable-stack integration suite green (2,613/2,631, 18 pre-existing skips) with all-table isolation intact; all 31 `check-*.sh` guards; OpenAPI regenerated, no drift; new coverage — `grade.http.integration.test.ts` (the sheet carries `questions`/breakdown only when points are consistent and sum to the maximum; a save computes and stores the sum; a partial save is a legal draft; a per-question score is bounded by ITS OWN question, not the exam's maximum; refused entirely on an exam with no points; refused alongside `absent`; a second save replaces the breakdown whole), `grade-sheet.test.ts` (the empty-breakdown-is-not-`[]` rule, the per-question bound, the derived read-only total) — all confirmed to fail without their respective fix. **Two test-isolation bugs found and fixed while writing the above, one with real collateral cost**: a non-numeric `.sort()` and a missing TD-15 `version` on a second save (both harmless, caught immediately); and a cleanup-order mistake (`exam_question` deleted before `grade`, backwards — `grade_question_score.question_id` is RESTRICT against `exam_question`) that left the disposable stack's REUSED database in a broken state after the first attempt, which then broke two unrelated test files' own cleanup on the next run purely as collateral damage, confirmed by a fully green re-run once the ordering was corrected.

**Revision 150 (Document Owner decision — viewing a student's submitted answers is now reachable end to end, and an online exam's supervisor/assistants are editable exactly as a physical one's already are, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** Checkpoint 2 of the batch Revision 147 deferred.

**§1 — viewing a student's submitted responses was already fully built and unreachable, not missing.** `GET /assessments/{id}/submissions[/{studentId}]` (`listSubmissions`/`readSubmission`) were already routed, and `admin/assessments.tsx`'s `OnePaper` already renders a full submissions inbox with a per-student `SubmissionDialog` showing every answer — none of it reachable by a named supervisor who did not author the exam, because the SAME page's very first read (`GET /assessments/{id}`, `authorPaper`) still gated on plain `loadForAuthor`, before Revision 148 §2's `loadForAuthorOrSupervisor` short-circuit was ever reached. Widened for the same reason Revision 148 §2 gave: this is still a READ of the paper's content, never an authoring write (`authorPaper` has exactly one call site, the `GET` route — every content-authoring write keeps calling `loadForAuthor` directly and is unaffected). **A second, real gap closed alongside it**: even once reachable, nothing linked نقاط الاختبارات (the grading screen a supervisor already opens) to this page at all. `GradeSheetView` gains an optional `responsesBasePath` prop (`/admin/assessments` or `/teacher/assessments` — each portal's own path, on the identical reasoning `TeacherAssessmentsPage`'s own docstring already states for why one portal may never link into the other's chrome) rendering «عرض الإجابات», shown only for an ONLINE exam (`GradeSheet.exam.mode`, a new field on the sheet's DTO — a physical sitting has no submission to open). One reused screen, per R70.1's "one implementation, two ways in" — no new viewer was written.

**§2 — an online exam's supervisor/assistants are editable, not only settable at creation.** `exam-section.tsx`'s `mode === 'online' && locked` branch (editing an already-scheduled online exam's arrangement) rendered only a static hint; the `StaffPicker` a physical exam's edit already shows unconditionally lived exclusively in the sibling, CREATE-only branch, unreachable the moment `locked` was true. `updateExamSchedule` (`PATCH /exams/{id}/schedule`, Revision 145 §1) already accepts and replaces staff wholesale for an online arrangement — this was a rendering gap only, closed by adding the same `StaffPicker` to the locked branch; the underlying `supervisorId`/`assistantIds` state and the save payload's `examStaff` were already seeded and forwarded correctly regardless of mode, so no other change was needed. **Retargeting/re-opening the access gate remain deliberately excluded from this form**, exactly as Revision 145 §1 already stated — this closes only the staff-field gap the Owner named explicitly; widening further is a separate, not-yet-requested question.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full backend unit suite 342/342; full frontend unit suite 1,312/1,312; full disposable-stack integration suite green with all-table isolation intact; all 31 `check-*.sh` guards; new coverage — `assessment.integration.test.ts` (the newly-admitted supervisor now also reaching `authorPaper`, not only the two reads Revision 148 §2 fixed), `grade.http.integration.test.ts` (the sheet's new `mode` field), `scheduling-exam-edit.test.tsx` (the StaffPicker survives inside the locked+online branch, confirmed to fail without the fix by a stash/restore round trip) — all confirmed to fail without their respective fix. No migration.

**Revision 149 (Document Owner decision — النقطة القصوى returns to «إضافة عنصر»، pre-filled with 20, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, FRONTEND ONLY. NO MIGRATION.** Reconsiders Revision 147 §4's removal of the maximum-grade field from the bare exam CREATE form: the field is asked again, now pre-filled with `'20'` (`SchedulingDialog`'s `examMaxGrade` state defaults to `'20'` exactly when `item === null`, i.e. a fresh row) rather than left empty. A reader who accepts the default types nothing and no longer risks the empty-field `VALIDATION_FAILED` Revision 147 §4 fixed server-side; one who wants a different maximum still edits the pre-filled value before saving. **Deliberately NOT applied to EDIT** — an existing exam's field starts empty, since empty already means *"leave it alone"* on save (`examMaxGrade == null` omits `max_grade` from the request entirely); pre-filling `'20'` there would have silently overwritten a real stored value the moment a reader saved without touching this field. `BARE_DEFAULT_MAX_GRADE` (server-side, Revision 147 §4) is unchanged and still covers the rarer case of a request that omits the field outright (a direct API caller, not this form). Verified: frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full frontend unit suite 1,311/1,311, including an updated `scheduling-exam-source.test.tsx` (the field renders again on CREATE, pre-filled with 20; a source-pinning check on the exact default expression). No backend change.

**Revision 148 (Document Owner decision — the first of the three deferred architectural items begun: editable class branch/level/mode/subject/year through R50's own split, and a named exam supervisor may read what she may grade, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** The first checkpoint of the batch Revision 147 deferred; the remaining two (per-question grading/response-viewing's schema addition, and the group/circle/category schema migration) continue in subsequent checkpoints.

**§1 — a "good, simple" design for editing what §4.4 otherwise freezes: R50's `this_and_future` split, widened to accept a new identity for its successor.** Subject/target/branch/academic-year stay frozen for an in-place edit (`all_sessions`, the omitted-scope default) for the exact reason §4.4 always gave — an in-place mutation would silently re-point Sessions and staff assignments already materialized against the old answer. `this_and_future` already exists precisely to change identity safely: it closes the predecessor exactly where it is and opens a successor carrying the new answer, so nothing about the past is rewritten. `updateCourseScheduleSchema` gains five optional fields (`subject_id`, `branch_id`, `academic_year_id`, `teaching_mode`, `target_id`), refused outside `scope: 'this_and_future'` and refused if `teaching_mode`/`target_id` are named separately (one field cannot be ambiguous, exactly as CREATE's own `{teaching_mode, target_id}` pair already states). `splitCourseSchedule`'s successor construction resolves a changed identity through the SAME functions CREATE uses — `resolveTarget` (re-validating `administrative_group` mode's branch-match whenever branch, subject or mode moves, even naming the same target) and `assertSubjectTaughtAtLevel` — rather than a second, competing check; a branch move that would leave a stale room behind (unstated `room_id`) is refused `ROOM_BRANCH_MISMATCH`, the identical refusal CREATE gives for the same half-specified state. The manager-only gate `this_and_future` already had (`assertCanManage`) covers the new fields with no further check. **Frontend**: the existing per-occurrence editor (`schedule-sessions.tsx`'s `ScopeDialog`, reached by opening one dated occurrence and choosing «هذه الحصة وكل ما بعدها» — the one screen `this_and_future` was ever reachable from) gains an identity section, shown only for that scope and only in the admin portal (splitting stays manager-only), pre-filled with the schedule's current branch/level/subject/year/target via a fresh `useScopeOptions` call and submitted alongside the existing room/time/delivery fields the dialog already always sends in full.

**§2 — a named exam supervisor may read a student's submitted answers, not only grade them.** `loadForGrading` (`grade.service.ts`, Revision 146 §1) gained an `ExamStaff.position: 'supervisor'` short-circuit; `listSubmissions`/`readSubmission` — viewing what a student actually wrote, already fully routed (`GET /assessments/{id}/submissions[/{studentId}]`) but with no frontend UI yet — still authorized through plain `loadForAuthor` and had inherited the identical gap Revision 146 §1 closed for grading alone. **Not fixed by widening `assertMayAuthor` or `loadForAuthor` themselves** — nine other call sites gate every content-authoring write (add/edit/delete a question or option) through exactly those, and a supervisor's reach is *she may see what was submitted for her own sitting*, never *she may rewrite its questions*. A new function, `loadForAuthorOrSupervisor`, checks the supervisor short-circuit first and falls through to the unchanged `assertMayAuthor` otherwise, used only by the two read functions above; authoring is untouched, proven by a test that confirms the same newly-admitted supervisor is still refused `addQuestion`. Viewing responses through a frontend screen remains for the next checkpoint — this closes only the authorization gap underneath it.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full backend unit suite 342/342; full frontend unit suite 1,311/1,311; full disposable-stack integration suite green with all-table isolation intact; all 31 `check-*.sh` guards; OpenAPI regenerated with no drift; new coverage — `course-schedule.http.integration.test.ts` (branch+target move, subject move, the untaught-subject refusal, the stale-room refusal, and the pre-existing "no scope, no identity fields" refusal, now alongside the new capability rather than superseded by it), `assessment.integration.test.ts` (the newly-admitted supervisor reaching the inbox and the paper, and still refused authoring) — all confirmed to fail without their respective fix. Two test-isolation bugs found and fixed while writing the above: staffing the shared fixture teacher on a new successor row leaked into an unrelated later assertion about her total footprint (fixed: the new tests staff nobody), and relying on the shared `slot()` time allocator from a describe block that now runs last in the file overflowed past 24 hours of cumulative calls (fixed: a small dedicated, fixed-time helper local to the new tests). Full detail in `docs/CHANGES.log`.

**Revision 147 (Document Owner decision — every page renders inside the platform's chrome, a remote exam's staff/maximum-grade fields match its physical counterpart, تقويمي's قائمة is catalogue-shaped, and terminology is unified on اختبار, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** Six independent corrections from the same Staging walk-through as Revisions 145/146; three further items from the same walk-through (viewing/grading a student's per-question responses, editable class branch/level/mode/subject/year, and schema-level group/circle/category exam targeting) remain **explicitly deferred**, not attempted in this pass — each is a genuine schema/architecture undertaking on the scale of its own revision, and the Document Owner has been told so rather than receiving a rushed version of any of them.

**§1 — a platform-wide rule: no page renders outside `StudentLayout`/`TeacherLayout`/`AdminLayout`/`ApplicationHeader`.** `StudentAssessmentsPage`'s `Paper` sub-component — the exam-answering screen «بدء الاختبار» deep-links to — returned bare JSX (no header, no footer, no way back to the portal) for its loading, error, AND ready states alike; only the OUTER table view was wrapped in `StudentLayout`. Fixed by wrapping all three of `Paper`'s states in `StudentLayout`, matching the rule the table view already followed. A repo-wide audit (every page under `frontend/src/pages/**` not importing one of the four layout primitives) found exactly one other such surface, `frontend/src/pages/public.tsx`'s pre-auth/error screens (`Login`, `ContentUnavailable`, `AccountDeactivated`, `NotFound`, `ScreenPending`) — correctly bare, since there is no session chrome to show before authentication. This rule stands as a standing constraint on every future page, cited here rather than restated per §16.4/documentation-shared-rules-once.

**§2 — the actual «تعذّر تحميل الاختبار» failure: a تقويمي occurrence's visibility and a paper's eligibility asked two different questions.** The calendar's personal (`mine=true`) exam read filtered only by `examTierWhere` — the coarse branch/visibility tier §4.6/R109 governs — never by R124's fine-grained audience (`examAudienceWhere`, the same rule `eligible()`/`studentPaper` enforces on open). A beneficiary could see and open «بدء الاختبار» for a sitting targeted at a Level/group/session/student she was never part of; the button was honest about visibility and dishonest about reachability. `calendar.service.ts`'s `personalFilters` now also reports `isBeneficiary` (whether the caller holds any live enrolment at all — a pure staff actor's coarse-tier visibility is UNCHANGED, since `examAudienceWhere`'s enrolment-shaped arms would otherwise wrongly narrow a Teacher's own tier-wide view to nothing); when `isBeneficiary` is true, the exam list is narrowed, row by row, through the SAME `examAudienceWhere` `eligible()` calls — one audience rule, not a second guess of it (§4.4c).

**§3 — a remote sitting's supervisor/assistants, dropped since R136.** `exam-section.tsx`'s `StaffPicker` (الأستاذ/المؤطر المسؤول والمؤطرون المساعدون) rendered only for a physical exam; an online exam's scheduling form asked nothing, even though §4.6 makes exam-staff responsibility a fact about the SITTING, not its delivery mode. Added identically to the physical branch. Two further gaps surfaced fixing this: `scheduleExam`'s ONLINE create path (`exam-scheduling.service.ts`) built the occurrence but never wrote its `ExamStaff` rows at all (the duplicate-position check and the `examStaff.create` loop existed only inside `if (mode === 'physical')`) — fixed with the identical checks, now in both branches; and the frontend's online-CREATE adapter call (`scheduling.ts`) never forwarded `input.examStaff` at all (the online-EDIT call already did). All three are one coherent gap, closed together.

**§4 — النقطة القصوى leaves «إضافة عنصر»; a bare sitting defaults to 20 instead of failing `NOT NULL`.** The Owner's reported crash — saving a bare physical exam with the maximum left empty threw `VALIDATION_FAILED`/`bare.max_grade` server-side, surfaced client-side only as a generic «تعذّر الحفظ» — is this field's exact failure mode. Removing the bare-creation pathway itself would have reversed Revision 136 clause 12's explicit ratification of keeping BOTH physical workflows, so the fix is narrower: `bare.max_grade` is now optional in `scheduleExamSchema`; `exam-scheduling.service.ts` defaults an omitted value to a new `BARE_DEFAULT_MAX_GRADE = 20` at creation (20 being the association's own everyday scale). The client-facing `NumberField` is removed from CREATE only (`!locked`) — it still renders, unchanged, once the sitting exists, since `PATCH /exams/{id}` already lets the maximum be corrected afterward and a wrong default must never be stuck.

**§5 — «حصصي» is retired; تقويمي's قائمة is now catalogue-shaped, like الجدولة's own.** The separate "what classes am I responsible for" table `/teacher/schedules` rendered below its calendar is deleted outright. تقويمي's قائمة view, for a teacher, no longer reads `GET /me/calendar` (a month-bounded read of dated OCCURRENCES) — it reads the same unbounded catalogue `listSchedulingItems` already merges for الجدولة's own قائمة (every scoped Class/Event/Exam DEFINITION, server-scoped to the caller exactly as `/teacher/schedules`'s own class read already was). `PersonalCalendar` gained one new, backward-compatible prop, `catalogList` — every existing caller (the beneficiary's own تقويمي included) is completely unaffected; only a caller that opts in gets an unbounded catalogue list instead of the shared month-bounded `OccurrenceTable`, and only for that view (تقويم/the month grid is untouched either way). «حصص الحلقة» — the one still-needed verb from the retired table — becomes this list's own row action, gated exactly as الجدولة's identical action is (`specOfKind(type).hasOccurrences`), reaching the same unchanged `/teacher/schedules/{id}/sessions` destination. **«عرض المستفيدات» (roster viewing), which حصصي also offered, has NO replacement anywhere on this list, matching الجدولة's own قائمة — which offers no roster action either.** This is flagged, not silently dropped or silently rebuilt to a shape nobody asked for: `readScheduleRoster` and the `admin.schedules.viewRoster`/`rosterTitle`/`rosterLede`/`rosterEmpty` strings are left in place, unused, rather than deleted, in case the Document Owner asks for a replacement. Open for her decision.

**§6 — terminology unified on اختبار; امتحان is retired platform-wide.** Every one of the 61 occurrences of «امتحان» (and its inflected forms) in `frontend/src/i18n/ar.ts` — the sole location of user-facing platform text; every other occurrence anywhere in the repository was already confirmed to be internal engineering prose in a code comment, never a rendered string — is now «اختبار» (correspondingly inflected; the substitution is a safe contiguous substring replace across every prefixed/suffixed form checked, since Arabic orthography concatenates prefixes/suffixes onto the stem without altering it). Test fixtures asserting the old Arabic text (`landing.test.ts`, `student-lede-once.test.tsx`, `scheduling-exam.test.tsx`) are updated to match.

**§7 — «إضافة عنصر» already allows a past date; no code change.** Audited both layers (every `DateField` in scheduling, the underlying `<input type="date">` primitive, and the service-side date handling) and found no `min` attribute, validator, or service check anywhere restricting a scheduling item's date to today or later. The Owner's ratification stands recorded as confirmation of existing, correct behavior, not as a fix.

**Deferred, explicitly, from the same request:** (a) viewing a student's submitted exam responses and per-question grading/publishing — a new `GradeQuestionScore`-shaped schema addition plus service/route/UI work; (b) a "good, simple" mechanism for editing a class's branch/level/mode/subject/year, frozen since Revision 57; (c) the real schema migration for group/circle/category (multi-dimension) targeting on `RecurringCourseSchedule`, matching the flexibility `Event`/`ActivitySection` already have. None of the three is attempted here; each touches either a new table, a frozen-since-ratified invariant, or session-materialization/conflict-detection/roster-resolution logic that currently assumes a single target — genuine schema/architecture decisions this pass's remaining scope could not respect carefully. Left for a dedicated follow-up pass.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full backend unit suite 342/342; full frontend unit suite 1,310/1,310; all 31 `check-*.sh` guards; new coverage — `personal-calendar.integration.test.ts` (the audience-narrowing fix, both the excluded and the included case, and a staff-actor no-regression case), `assessment.integration.test.ts` (online staff assignment, its duplicate-position refusal, and the bare-exam default/explicit-maximum cases), `scheduling-exam-source.test.tsx` (the maximum's removal from CREATE and its retained presence on EDIT), all confirmed to fail without their respective fix. Full detail in `docs/CHANGES.log`.

**Revision 146 (Document Owner decision — a named exam supervisor may grade her sitting, R73's Quran-tracking marker is admin-settable, and حسابي is one page, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** Three independent corrections from the same Staging walk-through as Revision 145.

**§1 — grading authorization gains the same supervisor short-circuit attendance already has.** `loadForGrading` (`grade.service.ts`) refused a مؤطِّرة grading a `target_kind: 'level'` sitting unless she taught that Level `entire_level` — correct, unchanged, and restated nowhere else — but never checked whether the exam's own `ExamStaff.position = 'supervisor'` named her responsible for it regardless, the fact `assertMayMark` (`attendance.service.ts`) already reads for the identical purpose. A مؤطِّرة named supervisor on a sitting she does not otherwise teach the whole Level for could not grade her own exam. Fixed with the identical check attendance already uses (`examStaff.count({position: 'supervisor'})`), checked once, before the five-arm question. **`readSubmission`/`listSubmissions` (viewing a student's actual answers) still authorize through `loadForAuthor`, unchanged by this revision** — this closes the grading gap the Owner reported reaching first; a supervisor's equivalent reach into reading submitted answers is a distinct, not-yet-requested question left for its own pass.

**§2 — `tracks_quran_progress` (R73) is now an admin-settable field, not seed-only.** Nothing before this revision let an administrator see or move R73's structural marker after initial setup; `subject_one_quran_tracker`'s own migration comment anticipated moving it only by retiring the old Subject. `PATCH /admin/subjects/{id}` now accepts `tracks_quran_progress` (a documented variant of the shared taxonomy form, on the same footing `description` already is for a Category); `GET /admin/subjects` now returns it. The partial unique index is unchanged and is not re-checked in the service — a second live holder still fails at the database boundary, surfacing as the platform's existing generic `409 DUPLICATE` (P2002) mapping, distinguished client-side from a `VERSION_CONFLICT` so the edit dialog stays open with a clear remedy rather than closing as if someone else had edited that same Subject.

**§3 — «حسابي» is one page: `/dashboard/student/account` is retired, merged into `/profile`.** The two surfaces answered the same question for an adult beneficiary — her reference code, her QR, her enrolments — under one shared header label, «حسابي», reached from two different menus. `/profile` already carried the reference code, QR and a richer `PlacementSection` (enrolments, circles, guardians) for the SIGNED-IN person; the one thing the retired page had that `/profile` did not was showing the ACTIVE CHILD's identity while a parent acts for one — `/profile` has always shown only her own, by deliberate design (R65: "a card printed for the wrong person is worse than no card"). `/profile` gains a new, strictly additive, read-only `ChildIdentitySection`, rendered only while `activeChildId` is set, resolved through the same `GET /students/me` (`X-Active-Child-ID`) the retired page used — never replacing the signed-in person's own editable section above it. The student portal's registry entry and the `/dashboard/student/account` route are removed; the path answers `not-found` rather than a redirect, matching how a retired route has been retired elsewhere in this specification.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full backend unit suite 342/342; full frontend unit suite 1,308/1,308; new coverage — `grade.http.integration.test.ts` (the supervisor-grading case), `taxonomy.http.integration.test.ts` (the marker's conflict refusal and its exposure on the list read — the success path against the one shared seeded holder is deliberately NOT exercised at this layer, since a write-then-restore round trip on a TD-15-versioned row cannot return the all-table-isolation guard's checksum to its exact prior state even once the flag itself is restored), `profile/index.test.tsx`, all confirmed to fail without their respective fix. Full detail in `docs/CHANGES.log`.

**Revision 145 (Document Owner decision — an online exam's scheduling is editable, and تقويمي reads its own filter vocabulary, ratified 2026-09-15):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION, NO NEW STATE.** Two independent corrections, both from the same Staging walk-through.

**§1 — `PATCH /exams/{id}/schedule` (TD-3.6), superseding R136 clause 12's own text.** That clause stated an already-scheduled remote occurrence's arrangement "has no route — none existed to widen." The Owner's ratified distinction narrows what was previously an absolute: *"an exam that was published and already taken by a student cannot be changed — it can be duplicated to keep an unchanged copy of what was taken — but its scheduling can be edited, any scheduling can be edited."* This closes that gap for `mode: 'online'` with a route mirroring exactly what `PATCH /exams/{id}` already lets a physical sitting's arrangement do: target (any of R125's five arms, re-resolved and re-authorized through the same `resolveTarget`/`assertMayAuthor` scheduling itself uses), date, clock window, catalogue type, visibility tier, and staff (replaced wholesale, the identical soft-delete convention). Additionally, and uniquely to a remote occurrence, `availability` may be changed, recomputing `available_from` through the same four R136 clause 9/10 policies. **Deliberately excluded, because they are content facts, not scheduling facts**: `mode`, `source_exam_id`, `bare`, and the physical-only `branch_id`/`room_id`. **R124's freeze is untouched** — no question or option write reaches through this route, so the existing "an exam already taken cannot be changed" rule needed no new enforcement; it already covered exactly and only what the Owner said it should. **The one honestly-accepted risk**, stated rather than silently guarded against: because `readGradeSheet`'s roster has always been the exam's CURRENT audience, never a snapshot (true of every other retarget in this platform), narrowing the target after submissions exist makes an existing submission's grade temporarily invisible on the sheet — never deleted, and readable again the moment the target widens back. Refusing this outright would have contradicted the Owner's own explicit "any scheduling can be edited"; a later revision may add an explicit narrowing guard if the Owner asks for one. **Re-notification is deliberately not sent on a reschedule** — a real, separate question the Owner has not asked, left for an explicit future revision rather than invented now. A physical sitting is unaffected; `PATCH /exams/{id}` is untouched.

**§2 — `GET /me/calendar/options` (TD-3), closing a defect R140 §3 did not reach.** تقويمي's Category/Level filter row was fed the SAME unscoped `GET /calendar/bootstrap` chrome the anonymous public timetable renders from — every Category and Level in the association, never narrowed to the reader — while its group/circle/subject filters were never supplied options at all (silently empty, offering only «الكل»). R140 §3 corrected the OCCURRENCE query (`personalFilters`) to the caller's own scope; this corrects the OPTIONS a reader is offered to filter by, on exactly the same union `personalFilters` itself reads: a beneficiary's own `Enrollment`/`StudentTeachingGroup` rows, unioned with what a مؤطرة currently teaches (`teacherEventScope`, R91-dated) and its Subjects/branches. Rule O's own words apply here precisely as they already do for branch and category: *"a control that implies a scope she does not have... the control would be a lie."* `personal-calendar.tsx` now fetches this alongside (never instead of) the public bootstrap, which it keeps using only for the Hijri/Gregorian chrome every calendar surface shares. No role check decides which half of the union runs — either resolution is simply empty when it does not apply, and a caller with neither enrolment nor staffing gets every field `[]`, never a refusal.

**Verified:** backend/frontend typecheck/lint/build clean; full backend unit suite 342/342; new `personal-calendar-options.integration.test.ts` (6/6, real disposable stack) for §2; `docs/openapi.json` regenerated with no hand edits (179 paths/230 operations, two new); TD-3 conformance updated (`scripts/ci/td3-routes.txt`), 230/238 implemented, 0 undocumented. Full detail, including §1's frontend wiring, in `docs/CHANGES.log`.

**Revision 144 (Document Owner decision — public programme overview on the landing page, ratified 2026-09-14):** **IMPLEMENTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION, NO NEW STATE.** §5.1's landing page gains a section, placed before «أين تجدنا» (`BranchesSection`), giving an anonymous visitor an overview of what the institute teaches: every Category (`الفئات`), each Category's Levels (`المستويات`), and each Level's Subjects (`مواد المستوى`) and حفظ القرآن curriculum (`مقرر الحفظ`) — the same four admin screens already manage, read here for the first time by someone who has not signed in. New public, anonymous `GET /programs` (TD-3.16), mounted before the guarded router exactly as `/branches` (Revision 35) and `/partners` (NEW N) already are, served by a dedicated `public-program.service.ts` — never the admin taxonomy/reference-data services, on the same audience-separation reasoning Revision 35 states for branches: an endpoint's audience is part of its contract. The projection is a fixed allowlist (Category/Level id, name, description; Subject id, name; Surah id, Arabic name) — **never** enrolment counts, gender restriction, display-order internals, optimistic versions, or any operational/beneficiary data, matching §5.1's existing "reference data only" rule for the calendar bootstrap read. Soft-deleted Categories, Levels and Subjects are excluded throughout. Levels are ordered within their Category by `display_order`; Subjects by their own `display_order`; Surahs by Mushaf order (`surah_id` ascending) — the same orders an admin already sees on «مواد المستوى»/«مقرر الحفظ», not insertion order. A Level with no Subjects or no Surahs assigned yet renders that honestly (an empty list), never a guess.

**Revision 143 (Document Owner decision — Vercel/Preview retired, three-tier environment topology, ratified 2026-09-13):** **Preview, defined by Revision 104 as the renamed Vercel deployment, is retired.** It was used only in the project's early development for frontend-only visual review against MSW mocks, calling no real backend; it is no longer part of the architecture, testing strategy, Staging environment, or Production deployment plan. **The environment topology named in §19.0 and Revision 104 is now three tiers, not four: Local Development, Staging, Production.** Every clause of Revision 104 not naming Preview stands unchanged — Staging remains the first-class, full-stack, production-shaped controlled-UAT tier; the residency firewall, the `NODE_ENV` clarification, and the uniform-cookie-attribute rule are all retained verbatim and now read without a Preview tier to except. **No code, schema, migration, or route change accompanies this revision**: no `vercel.json` or Vercel-specific configuration existed in the repository to remove. What remains is external to this codebase — Vercel's own GitHub integration for this repository, and the Vercel project it deploys — and closing it is a Document Owner action outside engineering's authority, recorded in [`docs/operations/environments.md`](../operations/environments.md#vercel-retirement--owner-action-required). **A green/successful Vercel commit status, past or future, is a legacy artifact of the retired integration and is never evidence of the actual Production launch decision**, which depends solely on the Moroccan-VPS/Hostoweb path §19.1 already defines. This revision does not delete the Vercel project, its historical deployments, or the external integration — closing those requires separate, explicit Owner authorization.

**Revision 142 (Document Owner decision — explicit manual remote-exam opening, ratified 2026-09-13):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION, NO NEW STATE.** Closes Codex H3. **A manual remote exam is opened explicitly by an authorized teacher or administrator through an «فتح الاختبار» action in the application, never by the scheduled start time arriving on its own.** R136 clause 5 already named this fact: `Exam.available_from` is `NULL` under the `manual` availability policy until *"a timestamp already computed at scheduling time... or once by an explicit staff 'open now' act"* — this revision is that one missing write, not a new column or a new status. `POST /assessments/{id}/open` sets `available_from` to the current instant on a `mode = 'online'`, `status = 'published'`, still-`NULL` occurrence, inside the same governing-lock/re-read transaction H5 established for every other Exam mutation, and reuses `assertMayAuthor` — the one per-arm authority rule H2 restored, never its branch-only subset — so exact-Session, Teaching-Group, named-student, whole-Level and branch-scoped-Admin authority are exactly what authoring and scheduling already enforce; a student and every other non-staff role are refused by the same rule. **One-way and non-idempotent, matching `POST /assessments/{id}/close`'s own established convention**: opening an already-open, still-`draft`, `closed`, or `physical` row is refused `409 STATE_CONFLICT`/`INVALID_TRANSITION`, never silently accepted and never a second write. Opening changes nothing else — it does not publish grades, alter a submission, reschedule the sitting, or touch unrelated content, and every existing eligibility (`examAudienceWhere`), safeguarding, account-state and R122 date-of-audience check still applies afterward exactly as it does for a `custom`/`at_start`/`offset_minutes` occurrence. The test that previously simulated opening with a direct `prisma.exam.update` is replaced by this action; that direct write remains only where a test proves `eligible()`'s own `NULL`/future-timestamp refusal, never as opening-workflow evidence. **H3 remains the only Codex finding this revision touches**; B1–B8 and H1/H2/H4/H5/H6 are unchanged.

**Revision 141 (Document Owner decision — structural self-managed rejection audit, ratified 2026-09-11):** R132(9)'s **ids-only** rule governs `selfmanaged.reject`. TD-8's former requirement to retain the administrator's rejection reason in that event is superseded. The human rationale belongs only in `SelfManagedClaim.decisionReason` while the claim is legitimately retained and is cleared at permanent beneficiary erasure under R133. The durable audit keeps the event, claim/beneficiary identifiers, actor, timestamp and any permitted structural result, never copied rejection free text. **One narrow historical exception to audit append-only immutability is authorized:** remove existing rejection-reason values from `selfmanaged.reject.detail`, consistently whether or not they appear identifying, while preserving the audit row and every other permitted structural field. No unrelated audit event may be changed; no generic PII detection/scrubber or ordinary audit-edit capability is authorized. TD-8's retention periods and purge allowlist otherwise remain unchanged. This qualifies §7's attribution invariant for this event: accountability records the rejection and its author, not the administrator's free-text rationale. R132/R133 historical text is preserved; this clarification reconciles their minimisation rule with TD-8.

**Revision 140 (Document Owner decision — scoped personal calendars corrected against R139's flexible Event scope, and a مؤطِّرة gains a bounded, non-circular grant to schedule her own class, ratified 2026-09-10):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** Revisions 24/43/82/91/109/110/114/135/138/139 stand except where named below. Two sections, §3 then §2, sequenced smallest-safest first per the Owner's own instruction; §3's correction is a prerequisite of §2's cross-section evidence (a Teacher-created class must actually reach the calendars it is supposed to), which is why it comes first.

**§3 — personal calendars, corrected against R139.** `GET /me/calendar` (R82.8) was already the platform's answer to *"what concerns me"*, built from `personalFilters()` (`calendar.service.ts`) as a filter composed into the SAME `readCalendar` projection `GET /calendar` uses — never a second pipeline, never per-user rows. Auditing it against R139's own combined-scope rule found ONE genuine defect and confirmed the rest already correct.

**(1) The defect: `personalFilters`'s Event predicate checked each scope dimension as an INDEPENDENT `OR` arm** — a student enrolled in Level Y at branch B3 matched an Event scoped to *"branch B1 AND level Y"* through the Level arm alone, never checking her branch was B1. This is precisely the *"accidentally-broadening `OR`"* the Owner's own audit instruction named. The platform already had the correct rule, stated for the opposite direction: `eventAudienceWhere` (`roster-resolution.ts`, R82.7), which resolves an Event's NOTIFICATION audience, has always read a combined scope as an intersection (*"naming a Category alongside a Branch narrows the Branch, it does not add a second, unrelated population"*) via a single `some` requiring ONE enrolment to satisfy every named dimension at once. `personalFilters`'s Event predicate now reads the same way: `OR` across her enrolments, `AND` across the dimensions ONE enrolment must satisfy — global (no scope rows at all) and her own `EventStaff` assignment are unchanged, untouched arms. This was a display-adjacent authorization-shaped bug in the personal read alone; the notification audience and the public calendar's own visibility tiers were never affected by it.

**(2) R139's multi-value scope already reached the personal calendar correctly**, confirmed rather than assumed: `personalFilters` has always queried `branchScopes`/`categoryScopes`/`levelScopes`/`administrativeGroupScopes` with `some`/`in`, never `take: 1` (that truncation was §1's display-projection bug, in a different function, already fixed by Revision 139) — an Event scoped to several branches or several Levels already concerned every enrolled student it named. New tests pin this rather than leaving it merely audited.

**(3) The Session side needed no correction.** R92's combined-branch-audience override and R91's effective-dated `SessionStaff` snapshot were already exercised by `session-audience.http.integration.test.ts` and `effective-staffing.http.integration.test.ts` respectively — a Teacher's dated staffing already governs her personal calendar through the SAME mechanism §2 below reuses for scheduling authority, not a second one.

**(4) Public occurrences, R135, and direct-ID authorization are UNCHANGED, and are asserted unchanged rather than merely left alone.** `GET /calendar` remains the visibility-tiered, unauthenticated-safe read it has always been (§4.4, R135's own text: *"authentication affects which occurrences the caller is authorized to receive, not the filters selected on this page"*) — nothing in this revision touches its filter-prefill behaviour, its route, or its tiers. `GET /me/calendar` is, and remains, the SEPARATE, deliberately narrower audience-scoped read R82.8 defined it as — R82.8's own text is explicit that a beneficiary's personal calendar is *narrower* than the public one (*"a beneficiary sees every public session in the platform today, including Levels she is not enrolled in [— the wrong answer for a personal calendar]"*), so "combine personal relevance with applicable public occurrences" is satisfied by the existing global-Event arm (an Event with no scope at all is everyone's) and was never a request to widen `/me/calendar` into a second public read. `GET /calendar/sessions/{id}` (the canonical dialog's deep-link target, §4/R83.5) was already direct-ID-safe — `session-page.http.integration.test.ts` already proves an unauthorized id answers `404`, never confirms existence — and is unchanged; the §4 Library deep-link (Revision 139's own prior work) is unaffected by anything in this revision.

**§2 — a مؤطِّرة may create a class within her own declared scope, superseding R114(2) for this ONE grant.** TD-2's *"Create/edit Recurring Course Schedules"* row read Teacher `⊘` since Revision 43; R71.0/R72.1 recorded WHY in terms — §4.4c derives a Teacher's entire scope *from the schedules she staffs*, so letting her create one with no OTHER anchor would let her widen her own reach circularly. The Owner's own anchor decision breaks the circularity with a DIFFERENT, non-circular pair: her declared `TeacherCategoryCapability`/`TeacherSubjectCapability` (R114) and her `teacher` `UserBranchRole` — **never the schedule she is about to create**.

**(1) R114(2)'s *"grants no scheduling authority"* is superseded for this one grant only, stated once here rather than by editing R114's own historical text.** Every other word of R114(2) stands: a declared capability still grants no Quran authority, no assignment authority, no administrative reach, and teaching authority over an EXISTING class is still exclusively `CourseScheduleStaff`, resolved through R91's effective-dated mechanism, unchanged by this revision.

**(2) The precise grant, server-enforced in `course-schedule.service.ts`.** A مؤطِّرة may `POST /admin/course-schedules` when: her `branchId` is one she holds a live `teacher` `UserBranchRole` in (`assertCanActOnBranch`, the SAME mechanism an Admin's own branch scope already uses — `NULL` still means every branch for that assignment, §7 R24); the target Level's Category is one she has declared (`TeacherCategoryCapability`) **OR** its Subject is one she has declared (`TeacherSubjectCapability`) — an OR, mirroring the two grants' own independence, checked once the target resolves (`assertTeacherDeclaredCapability`); `teaching_mode` is `entire_level` **only** (`assertTeacherEntireLevelOnly`) — Administrative/Teaching Group targeting is an organisational act (§4.4c: who is placed in which roster) left with the administration, not part of the ratified grant; and she is named the schedule's own `teacher` in the same request (`assertTeacherSelfStaffed`, `TEACHER_MUST_SELF_STAFF` otherwise) — the same structural-accountability rule R93(3) already states for an Event's `RESPONSIBLE_MUST_BE_SELF`, applied a second time for the second capability grant this platform makes to a مؤطِّرة's own declared profile.

**(3) Editing an existing class is bounded by CURRENT staffing, never by declared capability — the same question R91 already answers for every other operational surface.** `PATCH /admin/course-schedules/{id}` now also accepts a مؤطِّرة for a schedule she currently, effectively staffs (`assertTeacherCurrentlyStaffs`, any position — R87 §G: *"an assistant IS the main teacher for operational authorization"*, exactly as Session CRUD and recording start/stop already read it); a schedule she does not staff answers `NOT_FOUND`, never `FORBIDDEN` (§20 rule 17 — confirming existence to her is the leak this refuses).
