A `staff` patch that would remove her own `teacher` position is refused (`TEACHER_MUST_REMAIN_STAFFED`) — she may not edit herself out of her own class's accountability. **`this_and_future` splitting (R50) stays manager-only even on her own class** — it creates a successor schedule and closes the predecessor, a wider act than the operational edit this grant covers, and deletion is untouched (Teacher `⊘`, unchanged): the ratified grant is creation and operational editing, not every write TD-2's row could theoretically cover.

**(4) `GET /me/course-schedule-options` — a new, narrow, single-purpose read, on R93.4's own established precedent (*"a narrower question, never a wider permission"*), never a flag on the existing `/me/scope-options`.** An Admin reads that SAME endpoint for the SAME `ClassSection` scope chain, and an Admin's own class-creation authority is not bounded by declared capability at all — a mode flag on one shared endpoint risks one branch of a conditional quietly narrowing or widening the OTHER caller, which is exactly the class of defect a second, single-caller endpoint cannot produce. It returns the identical `{ categories, levels, subjects, academic_years, branches }` shape, narrowed by the SERVER: branches from her `teacher` `UserBranchRole`; a Level whose Category she has declared offers every Subject it teaches (the broader grant, not narrowed further); a Level reached only through a declared Subject offers just that Subject; a Level reached by neither is absent. **An option is not authorization**: `POST /admin/course-schedules` re-derives the identical check independently from the request it actually receives, exactly as `/me/scope-options`'s own established reasoning already states for the library.

**(5) The frontend reuses the canonical scheduling surface — no duplicate scheduler.** `/teacher/schedules` already opened `SchedulingDialog` (the SAME component `/admin/schedules` uses) for `activity`/`exam`; `class` joins `TEACHER_TYPES`. `ClassSection` (also shared, unchanged for Admin) gains `staffLocked` — a static *"you are this class's responsible مؤطِّرة"* statement replacing the multi-row `StaffingPeriods` editor a مؤطِّرة is server-refused from using any other way, exactly as `ActivitySection` already replaces a picker with a fixed fact via `responsibleLocked` — and `modes` is restricted to `['entire_level']` for her, never offered a choice the server always refuses. `useScopeOptions` gains `restrictToOwnCapability`, read only at the ONE call site `ClassSection`'s own scope chain uses, selected by the SAME `canAssignStaff` flag that already distinguishes Admin from Teacher throughout this shared dialog — every other item type and every Admin caller of this hook is provably unaffected, since nothing else reads `restrictToOwnCapability: true`.

**Verified:** backend lint/typecheck/build clean, 38 files/334 unit tests (unchanged — this revision is integration-tested); frontend lint/typecheck/build clean, 105 files/1,248 unit tests (unchanged code paths; existing suite green throughout); full disposable-stack integration suite, all-table isolation intact, with new coverage: `personal-calendar.integration.test.ts` (9 tests — the intersection correction, R139's multi-branch/multi-Level scope reaching the personal calendar, the global-Event arm, and a مؤطِّرة's own staffed-regardless-of-scope arm), `course-schedule-teacher.integration.test.ts` (14 tests — the full positive/negative authorization matrix: Category-alone and Subject-alone grants, wrong branch, undeclared Level/Category combination via direct target substitution, non-`entire_level` targeting, missing/wrong self-staffing, a bare Teacher role with neither anchor, edit bounded by current staffing with the NOT_FOUND-not-FORBIDDEN shape, the remain-staffed guard, `this_and_future` refusal, Admin/Super Admin behaviour unchanged, and the cross-section chain — a Teacher-created class's materialized Session reaching both the enrolled student's and her own personal calendar), and 7 new cases in `scope-options.http.integration.test.ts` for `/me/course-schedule-options`. `docs/openapi.json` regenerated with no hand-edits (175 paths/226 operations, one new route); TD-3 conformance updated (`scripts/ci/td3-routes.txt`), 226/234 implemented, 0 undocumented. All `scripts/ci/check-*.sh` guards, doc-links and `git diff --check` pass. **No migration** — every table this revision reads or writes (`TeacherCategoryCapability`, `TeacherSubjectCapability`, `UserBranchRole`, `CourseScheduleStaff`, the Event scope joins) already existed; only authorization logic, one new read endpoint, and the frontend's reuse of already-shared components changed. No Staging or Production action was taken. `docs/TASKS.md` is updated to match, ticked with this same evidence cited rather than restated.

**Revision 139 (Document Owner decision — event scope is confirmed a five-tier, explicit taxonomy the platform already carried; the read projection and the creation picker are corrected to actually reach it, ratified 2026-09-10):** **IMPLEMENTED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND. NO MIGRATION.** Revisions 24/43/110/138 stand except where named below.

**The taxonomy is formalised, not invented.** §7's `EventBranch`/`EventCategory`/`EventLevel`/`EventAdministrativeGroup` join tables and Revision 24's explicit rejection of a polymorphic `scope_type`/`scope_id` framework already gave an Event exactly the five tiers this revision was asked to confirm distinguishable: **platform-wide** (`global: true`, no `branchIds` — every already-operational branch); **all branches the actor may reach** (the same `global: true`, narrowed server-side to the actor's own reachable branches for anyone short of Super Admin — never a client-supplied list pretending to be "all"); **selected branches** (`branchIds`, one or more); **all Levels within the selected/permitted branches** (branches named, no `levelIds` — the absence of a row on a dimension has always meant "unrestricted on that dimension," confirmed at the audience-matching read); and **selected Levels** (`levelIds`, one or more, independently of how many branches). A sixth case, **no Level at all**, was already enforced for genuinely general events: `assertHolidayShape` (`event.service.ts`) refuses `levelIds`/`groupIds` outright for a `holiday`-structural-kind event, so a vacation cannot silently acquire a Level nobody asked it to carry. Every one of these was already server-enforced, already covered create/edit/recurrence/series-split/conflict-detection/candidate-selection/visibility/deletion identically (they all read the SAME `EventScopes` shape and the SAME join rows), and needed no new column, no new enum, and no migration — auditing this against the schema, the authorization code and the existing test suite is what let this revision stay this small.

**Two genuine gaps, both display/input-layer, neither authorization or data-model.** (1) `calendar.service.ts`'s `GET /calendar` projection read each event's `branchScopes`/`categoryScopes`/`levelScopes` with Prisma `take: 1`, so a genuinely multi-branch or multi-Level event silently showed only its first attached branch/Level to a READER — the join rows themselves, and every authorization/audience-matching query over them, were always complete and correct; only what a client could see of an already-correct scope was truncated. (2) `ActivitySection`'s scope-target picker (`class-section.tsx`, used by نشاط/حفل/عطلة creation on `/admin/scheduling`) accepted exactly one id per dimension, even though `EventScopes.branchIds`/`categoryIds`/`levelIds`/`groupIds` have always been arrays on the wire — an administrator could ask for one branch or one Level, never "these two," not because the platform refused it but because the form never offered it.

**Fix (1) — `Occurrence` gains plural fields, additive beside the singular ones.** `branch_ids`/`branch_names`, `category_ids`/`category_names` and `level_ids`/`level_names` (`GET /calendar`, TD-3.4) carry the WHOLE scope; the existing singular `branch_id`/`branch_name`/`category_id`/`category_name`/`level_id`/`level_name` (Revision 36, §13) are UNCHANGED and stay the first entry of the same list, for a reader who only ever asked "which one." A Session or an Exam always reports a single-element array on every one of these — §4.4c's exactly-one-target invariant for `RecurringCourseSchedule` and an Exam's own single branch/Level are unaffected and unchanged by this revision — only an Event's arrays can genuinely hold more than one entry. No new endpoint and no route-shape change; TD-3 conformance is unchanged (225/233 implemented, 0 undocumented) and `docs/openapi.json` is regenerated with no hand edits.

**Fix (2) — the picker becomes multi-valued, on the SAME arrays the backend already accepted.** `ActivitySection` replaces its single-select scope target with `MultiSelectField` (an existing, reused component — no new control invented) bound to `scopeIds: string[]`; `/admin/scheduling` submits the full chosen set (`{ branchIds: scopeIds }` etc.) rather than a single-element array, with explicit Arabic hints distinguishing "no Level chosen → every Level in the selected branch(es)" from the platform/all-branches global case, so the five tiers read as five tiers on screen, not one nullable field with undocumented meanings. **No backend contract change was required for this half** — `createEvent`/`updateEvent` already accepted arrays; only the form was artificially single-valued.

**Two incidental defects, found while auditing the exact code this revision touched, corrected in the same commit rather than deferred.** (a) The activity/holiday scope-required validation on `/admin/scheduling` lacked the `!editing` guard its sibling checks already carry, so a Teacher editing her own existing event — whose scope picker is locked and hidden on edit, and therefore never repopulates `scopeIds` — could be incorrectly blocked from saving an unrelated field change. (b) Switching `scopeKind` (branch ↔ category ↔ level ↔ group) did not clear the previous dimension's `scopeIds`, which could submit a branch id as a `levelId` after a dimension switch made without first deselecting. Neither is a new capability; both are corrections to code this revision was already modifying.

**Verified:** backend lint/typecheck/build clean, 38 files/334 unit tests; frontend lint/typecheck/build clean, 105 files/1,248 unit tests (up from 1,230 — 18 new, covering the multi-scope display and the picker's count/empty-state/hints/locked-state/source-pinned payload shape); full disposable-stack integration suite green with all-table isolation intact, including new coverage in `calendar.integration.test.ts` (the whole scope is read back, not only the first branch/Level/category; a Level-less event reports empty arrays, not null; a Session always reports exactly one) and `calendar.http.integration.test.ts` (the plural fields are real arrays over real HTTP, each containing the singular field's own value). OpenAPI regenerated with no hand-edits (174 paths, 225 operations, unchanged route count — this revision added wire fields, not routes) and TD-3 conformance unchanged in shape. All `scripts/ci/check-*.sh` guards, the documentation-links guard and `git diff --check` pass. **No migration**: the join tables already stored multiple rows per event since their original introduction (§7); nothing about the data model changed, only the read projection and the creation form. `RecurringCourseSchedule` — including محاضرة, an explicit 2026-08-28 Owner ruling that a lecture is taught and therefore class-shaped — stays single-target by §4.4c's own CHECK constraint, untouched by this revision; §1's "no mandatory Level" applies to genuinely general Event-kind activities only, never to a taught class. No Staging or Production action was taken. `docs/TASKS.md` is updated to match, ticked with this same evidence cited rather than restated.

**Revision 138 (Document Owner decision — scheduling consistency, Session editing, responsive portal navigation, public site/legal content, and mobile calendar, ratified 2026-09-09):** **IMPLEMENTED, MIGRATED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND.** Revisions 43/50/57/91/97/105/106/109/110/119/133/136/137 stand except where named below. This revision is nine independent areas, each additive to already-ratified surfaces; none retire or restructure R137's academic-year/exam work, which stands unchanged throughout.

**(1) `recurrence=none` teaching candidates.** `GET /admin/teaching-candidates` refused every one-time (`recurrence: 'none'`) class with `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, because R137(11) made `none` a first-class, DEFAULT recurrence for حصة دراسية/محاضرة without updating this endpoint's own، separately-declared enum — three other places already enumerating recurrence (`course-schedule.validators.ts`, `lib/recurrence.ts`'s `matches()`) were already correct; this was the one miss. `teachingCandidatesQuerySchema` now accepts `none`, requiring a new `date` field (the one-time class's own calendar date) precisely when `recurrence=none` and refusing it otherwise. Conflict-checking for a one-time class is not weakened toward `availability_indeterminate` — `weekdayOf(date)` (new, `policies/teaching-profile.ts`) derives the single weekday the class's real date falls on and feeds the SAME weekday+time-overlap machinery every recurring pattern already uses, symmetrically checking a colleague's own one-time class via its already-selected `anchorDate`. The default الجدولة → ＋ إضافة عنصر dialog (مرة واحدة, no date chosen yet) now produces no request at all until a date is entered, so the reported 400 cannot recur even transiently.

**(2)/(3) Session editing gains the series form's own editable fields, and an explicit, Session-level preserve-vs-overwrite choice for manually edited Sessions.** `Session` gains `title`/`description` (`VarChar(120)` default `'حصة'`, `VarChar(2000)` nullable — migration `20260909120000_r138_session_title`, expand-migrate-contract per TD-6b), on **exactly the same footing** `delivery_mode`/`visibility` already have: snapshotted at materialization, editable per-occurrence (تعديل الحصة → هذه الحصة وحدها, marking the Session `overridden`), and editable at the rule level through either wider scope (هذه الحصة وكل ما بعدها / كل حصص هذا الجدول), which resyncs future un-protected occurrences exactly as delivery/visibility already do. **Deliberately excluded, and documented why rather than silently omitted**: subject/teaching-mode/target/branch/academic-year are creation-time-immutable even at the SERIES level already (a pre-existing, R138-unrelated `.strict()` constraint on `updateCourseScheduleSchema`), so widening them to the occurrence would be a genuinely new capability, not parity; scheduling-type and attendance-marking are not exposed per-occurrence because per-date policy switching is not a coherent concept and was not specifically requested.

**The preserve-vs-overwrite choice — Session-level, never field-level, and NOT a family of per-field override columns.** The Owner's own instruction was explicit that the existing single `overridden` boolean plus an explicit overwrite decision must be preferred over inventing `title_override`/`description_override`/etc., one column per editable property — and it is: the SAME `overridden` flag that already protected `delivery_mode`/`visibility`/`room_id` now also protects `title`/`description`, one flag governing seven mirrored fields rather than seven flags governing seven fields. `materializeSchedule` (`session-materialize.service.ts`) gains a fifth parameter, `overwriteManuallyEdited` (default `false`): sessions whose protection reasons are **exactly** `['OVERRIDDEN']` become eligible for a forced resync when the flag is set; a session ALSO protected by `HAS_CONTENT`/`HAS_ATTENDANCE`/`LIFECYCLE` is never overwritten regardless of the flag — historical work is never silently destroyed by this choice. `updateCourseSchedule`/`splitCourseSchedule` accept `overwrite_manually_edited` on the wire (`PATCH /admin/course-schedules/{id}`), threading the same flag through both the whole-schedule resync path and the this-and-future split's successor-migration path; `MaterializationDto` gains `overwritten: number`, counted apart from `resynced` so a response can say what it changed *because it was asked to*, distinct from what it changed because nothing protected it. **The choice is asked, not assumed, and only when it matters**: the frontend computes which already-loaded Sessions are eligible (`protected_reasons` strictly `['OVERRIDDEN']`, filtered to the edit's own date range) and shows the Owner's own two-option Arabic wording (*"هناك حصص معدّلة يدويًا ضمن هذا الجدول. كيف تريدين تطبيق التغييرات؟"* — تطبيق التغييرات على الجميع / الإبقاء على المعدَّلة كما هي) **only when at least one eligible Session exists in range** — asking every time would train an administrator to click through unread, the exact failure §14.4 exists to prevent. One shared component (`ManualEditsDialog`, `components/scheduling/manual-edits-dialog.tsx`) and one shared eligibility filter (`sessionsEligibleForOverwrite`) serve BOTH callers — schedule-sessions.tsx's وwider occurrence-screen scopes and scheduling.tsx's own series editor (تعديل العنصر) — because item's own instruction is explicit: *"do not maintain two competing implementations for 'all Sessions.'"* The series editor's own save now performs the identical pre-flight check (fetching the schedule's Sessions, computing eligibility, asking if needed) before every existing-schedule save, not only from the occurrence screen.

**(4) Portal navigation collapses on mobile and opens as a bounded overlay, never an expanding layout column, on any width.** All three portal shells (Admin/Teacher/Student) already share `PortalShell`; the fix lives there once. Below the header's own `60rem` breakpoint (the SAME number `ApplicationHeader`'s burger already uses — one coherent responsive system, not a second breakpoint) the sidebar is collapsed by DEFAULT, decided entirely by a CSS media query rather than a JS guess that would be wrong for the first frame of every full-page navigation. A new `.admin-nav-toggle` control, present at every width, opens/closes it; open/closed is announced through the SAME `nav.openMenu`/`nav.closeMenu` words `ApplicationHeader`'s own burger already uses.

**A desktop defect the Owner found by hand after the first cut, and corrected in this same revision rather than deferred.** The first implementation let a desktop press of القائمة RESTORE the sidebar as the ordinary `16rem` grid column it already defaults to — which read as "opening" only until the reader noticed the grid itself changed shape under her, exactly the *"pushes the page sideways"* complaint the Owner's screenshot showed. `override` now has exactly two explicit states, `collapsed` and `overlay`, and **`overlay` never touches `grid-template-columns`, at either width**: it renders `.admin-nav` as `position: fixed`, stacked on top of the page, right-side in RTL (`inset-inline-start: 0`), at a bounded width (mobile: `min(85vw, 20rem)`, unchanged from the Owner-confirmed-good mobile behaviour; desktop/tablet: `16rem`, the sidebar's own existing reading width, never the whole viewport) — collapsing is the ONLY state that changes the grid, and it only ever reclaims the column (`1fr`, more room), never grows it back toward the sidebar. The untouched resting default (nothing pressed) is exactly what it was before this correction: desktop still shows the inline column with no click required, per the Owner's own original instruction that desktop may keep its expanded default. Escape and a backdrop tap close the overlay at both widths; following any nav link (a plain full-page `<a href>`, not client routing) closes it by leaving the page, so the destination is on screen immediately with nothing left over to dismiss.

**(5) The authenticated homepage hero shows no call-to-action at all.** It previously showed its own «لوحة التحكم» button — a second control repeating exactly what `ApplicationHeader`, one screen-length above, already states and already offers. `Hero` now renders no `hero__actions` block whatsoever once `useNavigation().isAuthenticated`; the logged-out «تسجيل الدخول» CTA is unchanged. **مسالك التعليم and كيف تنضمّين are removed entirely, not replaced** — their only components (`Card`/`Step`, `components/ui/card.tsx`) had no other caller anywhere in the frontend once removed, so the file and its component-specific CSS (`.card__icon`/`.card__meta`/`.steps`/`.step`/`.step__title`) are deleted with them; `.card__title` stays, since `BranchCard` uses it directly on an unrelated footing. The `landing.stages*`/`landing.how*`/`landing.step*` i18n strings stay in the catalogue, unused, on the same precedent already established for the earlier-removed mission section — there is no reverse "unused key" guard in this codebase (`i18n/resolves.test.ts` records why one was tried and withdrawn). **أين تجدنا and شركاؤنا are redesigned, not merely left as-is**: a lone branch no longer sits stranded in a two-column grid with visible empty space beside it (`.grid--2` moved from a fixed `repeat(2, 1fr)` to `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 20rem), 1fr))`, which also still shows two/three-abreast once there is room); each partner now renders as its own bordered surface (reusing `.card`'s own surface/border tokens) rather than bare bold text loose in a row. Both keep their existing zero/one/multiple handling and every field is still data-driven — no invented branch or partner content.

**(6)/(7) The public Privacy Policy and Terms of Use are versioned and Super-Admin-managed, on the SAME pattern §2.3/R119's `LegalConsentText` already proved, not a second mechanism.** A new model, `LegalDocument` (migration `20260909132600_r138_legal_documents`), carries `kind` (`privacy_policy` | `terms_of_use`) as the one genuine difference from `LegalConsentText`: that model enforces exactly one active row GLOBALLY (right for one shared consent wording), which is wrong here — the two public documents must each have their own current version in force at once, so the partial unique index enforcing *"at most one active row"* is scoped `(kind) WHERE status = 'active'` rather than bare `(status)`. Every other invariant is identical in kind and reasoning to `LegalConsentText`'s own: immutable once activated (`assertEditable`, `LEGAL_DOCUMENT_IMMUTABLE`); the one-active-per-kind invariant is a DATABASE constraint, not a service-level check, so two concurrent activations of the same kind cannot both win; nothing is ever deleted — superseding is a status change, and a version the public page once showed stays readable forever, because the platform must be able to answer *"what did this document say on date X"* from its own data. **No FK from `ConsentRecord`** — unlike the registration consent wording, nothing records *"which Privacy Policy version a reader saw"*; the public page always shows whichever version is active NOW, and historical readability (never the destruction of a shown version) is what keeps a past version answerable without a record needing to name it. `GET /legal-documents/{kind}` is anonymous (Google's OAuth policy requires the policy reachable without signing in, the identical reasoning `GET /registration/consent-text` is anonymous for); `GET/POST/PATCH /admin/legal-documents(/{id}(/activate))` are Super Admin only, `assertFreshActive`-gated against LIVE role rows exactly like every other §5.6 setting. Audited as `legaldocument.create`/`.update`/`.activate`, carrying `kind` and, on activation, the superseded version's id — never the body, never a copied label (`assertMinimizedDetail`'s own forbidden-key rule already refuses `title`/`label`-shaped keys, the identical trap caught during this same revision's Session-title audit-detail work in (2)/(3)). A new `/superadmin/settings` section, `LegalDocumentsSection`, is mounted twice (once per kind) — structured identically to the existing `ConsentTextsSection` it is modelled on: the active version in full, drafts with تعديل/اعتماد, superseded versions visible without a click (a compliance reader asks *"what did this say in March"*, and an accordion would hide the answer behind a click nobody knows to make).

**`pages/legal.tsx` is dynamic now, not hardcoded JSX** — the identical defect R119 already fixed for the registration consent wording (a version string with no technical relationship to the deployed text) existed here too, silently, because nothing had touched it since. The page fetches `GET /legal-documents/{kind}` and renders exactly the active `body_arabic` (`white-space: pre-line`, paragraph breaks preserved as approved), or an honest, non-alarming *"not yet published"* state (§14.4 — a declared state, never a blank page) when nothing has been activated for that kind. **Nothing is auto-seeded in production, on the SAME precedent `LegalConsentText` and `PARTNERS` (`production.ts`) already establish**: a plausible-looking legal document fabricated by a deploy script is worse than an honestly absent one, and §2.3 already makes approving legal wording an Owner/legal-review task, not a code decision. The former static content (`ar.legal.privacy*`/`ar.legal.terms*`) is kept in the catalogue, unused by the page now, as a **reviewed, updated recommended DRAFT** a Super Admin can paste into the new editor — it already states, correctly and specifically for this platform: the exact two Google scopes actually requested (`openid`/`email`, and explicitly NOT `profile`, corrected once already at R121 and re-verified here against `oauth.ts`); what R133's ONE deletion lifecycle actually deletes versus retains (the educational/safeguarding/consent/audit record, named explicitly, survives — never silently promised away); no sale, no advertising, no third-party sharing. **What remains genuinely unresolved is marked, not fabricated**: the retention PERIOD for records R133 keeps, the association's registration/CNDP-facing legal-entity detail, and governing law are each a visible ⚠ marker (`legal.ownerInput`/`legal.privacyRetentionPending`) rather than an invented number or name — this is operational legal-content preparation, and Owner/legal review remains appropriate before any version drafted from it is activated in production. `legal-consent-text.integration.test.ts`'s own five invariant categories are mirrored in `legal-document.integration.test.ts` (14 tests) plus a genuinely new one this model specifically needs — activating one kind never supersedes or blocks the other — and a real-HTTP suite (`legal-document.http.integration.test.ts`, 5 tests, through Nginx/API) proves the actual wire: draft → activate → the public read is genuinely anonymous and shows the exact text with no status/provenance leaking; an Admin (not Super Admin) is refused every write verb; an unknown `kind` in the URL is a coded `400`, never a crash; a second activation supersedes the first, whose exact wording stays listed and readable; editing anything ever active is refused (`LEGAL_DOCUMENT_IMMUTABLE`).

**(8) Mobile month-grid event chips wrap, at up to three lines, instead of ellipsis-truncating to a handful of characters.** «حصة تجويد القرآن» read as «حـ…» under the existing `white-space: nowrap` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` on `.event-chip__title`; inside the SAME established mobile breakpoint (`@media (max-width: 44rem)`, tested since R91 at 360px — no new, overlapping breakpoint introduced) the title now wraps naturally (`white-space: normal`, `overflow-wrap: break-word`, `-webkit-line-clamp: 3` — the identical idiom `.content-card__description` already uses for the same shape of problem, supported in every engine this project targets despite the vendor prefix), using nearly the full cell width the breakpoint already frees by hiding `__time`/`__delivery`/`__tag`. Chip padding is reduced further and `align-items: center` (which fought multi-line text by vertically centering a box that no longer has a fixed single-line height) is dropped in favour of `flex-start`; `min-height: var(--space-10)` (40px) stays as a floor so a short, single-line title is still a real ≥32px tap target (`calendar-geometry.mjs`'s own `tappable` check), while a longer title simply grows the box past that floor via the wrap rule. **A thin kind-indicator bar, costing no title width**, extends the exam-only pattern (R58's `box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 var(--color-exam)`) to the session and event kinds, in the BASE rule rather than only inside the mobile query — mobile hides delivery/tag/time and leaves colour plus this bar as the only kind signal left, and desktop had no reason to lack the same distinction §14.4 already requires elsewhere ("colour alone is not the signal"). **The 7-column real month grid is unchanged in every particular** — no agenda/list/day/card view was introduced, matching the Owner's own explicit instruction; every occurrence in a day still renders (no cap, the list itself scrolls, per the existing, unmodified `.cal-day__events` behaviour) and multiple events per day stack exactly as before. Verified against the existing, unmodified `verify-public-reader.mjs`/`calendar-geometry.mjs` real-browser suite (seven equal RTL columns, populated tappable cells, readable dual dates, no document overflow, at 320/360/375/390/412/430/768/1280px) as part of the disposable-stack integration pass this revision's evidence cites below — every one of those pre-existing assertions still passes against the modified stylesheet.

**Verified:** backend lint/typecheck/build clean, 38 files/334 unit tests; frontend lint/typecheck/build clean, 103 files/1,211 unit tests; full disposable-stack integration suite — 107 files/2,446 tests (17 pre-existing, unrelated skips) — passes with full all-table isolation, including new coverage for clause (1) (recurrence=none conflict-checking, 9 new tests), (2)/(3) (Session title/description snapshot-and-resync, the explicit preserve-vs-overwrite choice including the worked "protected by two reasons is never overwritten" case, 4 new tests, plus HTTP-level coverage of the new fields and `overwrite_manually_edited`), and (6)/(7) (`legal-document.integration.test.ts`, 14 tests; `legal-document.http.integration.test.ts`, 5 tests over real HTTP). OpenAPI regenerated with no hand-edits (174 paths/225 operations, up from 169/220 — five new legal-document routes) and TD-3 conformance unchanged in shape (225/233 implemented, 8 pending, 0 undocumented). All `scripts/ci/check-*.sh` guards, the documentation-links guard and `git diff --check` pass. **Real-browser evidence, targeted at the two areas with genuine, previously-unproven layout/wire risk**: a new, standalone, backend-free CSS-geometry harness (`nav-toggle-harness.html` / `verify-nav-toggle-geometry.mjs`, 18/18 checks) proves the navigation correction specifically — at 390/1280/1440px, that opening never moves or resizes `.admin__main`, that the overlay is `position: fixed` with a bounded width at every width, and that Escape/backdrop/nav-link-click all close it — since a layout-shift regression of exactly this shape is precisely what no source-level or DOM-structure test can see (the same reasoning `measure-page-header.mjs` already established for the page-header invariant); the legal-document HTTP suite above is the real-HTTP proof for (6)/(7)'s new wire contract. Items (4)'s mobile behaviour, (5)'s CSS-only redesign, and (8)'s chip wrapping are covered by the existing, unmodified real-browser calendar suite plus targeted frontend unit/source-pinning tests and manual verification. **Items (1) and (2)/(3) are NOT walked in a real browser in this revision** — they are backed instead by the disposable-stack integration suite cited above (9 new tests for (1); 4 new service-level tests plus HTTP-level coverage for (2)/(3)) plus frontend unit/typecheck/lint/build, the same real-DB-but-not-browser-driven evidence tier Revision 137 already used for its own comparatively thin extensions of existing infrastructure — named explicitly here rather than left for a reader to assume full browser coverage across every clause. `docs/TASKS.md` is updated to match, ticked with this same evidence cited rather than restated.

**Deliberately not built as a full from-scratch browser walk, and named so a future reader does not mistake the omission for an oversight:** a dedicated CDP script clicking through the Super Admin legal-document editor's own UI (draft → edit → preview → activate) end to end. The equivalent property — the real HTTP wire contract, the authorization boundary, immutability, and supersession — is proven at the HTTP layer instead (`legal-document.http.integration.test.ts`, through real Nginx/API), and the admin screen's own composition is the SAME `FormDialog`/`ConfirmDialog`/`VersionCard`-shaped pattern `ConsentTextsSection` already uses and has its own established browser coverage for; a second, UI-level walk of an already-proven interaction pattern was judged lower marginal value than the navigation-geometry harness above, which proves a genuinely new, previously-unverified layout property. No Staging or Production action was taken at any point in this revision.

**Revision 137 (Document Owner decision — academic-year administration, session-linked assessments, question/option CRUD, holiday/attendance/dropdown/layout UX fixes, self-managed-claims consolidation, and beneficiary profile completeness, ratified 2026-09-09):** **IMPLEMENTED, MIGRATED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND.** Revisions 43/56/58/62/79/90/91/110/119/123/124/125/132/136 stand except where named below. This revision is fourteen independent, additive fixes to already-ratified surfaces plus two genuine defects real-browser testing found while verifying clause (1) — none retire or restructure R136's exam architecture, which clause (14) restates unchanged.

**(1) Academic Year gains full administration, co-located with its semesters.** `AcademicYear` (§4.10(6), §7) previously had no write path at all — §14's own "System Settings (`/superadmin/settings`) … AcademicYear management" line described a screen that was never built, and this clause **supersedes** it: management lives at الفصول الدراسية (`/admin/academic-periods`), beside the semesters a year contains, not on a general settings page, because a year and its semesters are one coherent administrative surface, not two. `POST/PATCH/DELETE /admin/academic-years` (TD-5 soft delete, TD-15 optimistic locking, Super Admin only, audited as `academicyear.create/update/delete`) are new TD-3 routes. Deleting the year currently marked `is_current` is refused outright (`STATE_CONFLICT`, `ACADEMIC_YEAR_IS_CURRENT`) — `is_current`'s own partial unique index carries no `deleted_at` term, so soft-deleting it would neither free the flag nor make any other year current automatically; an administrator marks a different year current first, an ordinary edit. الفصول الدراسية's semester form now offers **every** academic year, not only the current one, closing the actual gap behind *"the semester form only ever offers one year"* — there was no way to create a second year for it to offer. **Two defects, found only because this clause was proven against a real browser rather than trusted from integration coverage alone, are corrected in the same migration/deploy unit:** the write schema accepted `isCurrent` (camelCase) under `.strict()` validation while the real frontend — and this endpoint's own already-published OpenAPI prose — always sent `is_current` (TD-3's ordinary wire convention), so every existing test happened to send the same wrong key the buggy schema accepted and the create/edit dialog was completely non-functional through the actual UI; and `academic_year.label`'s unique index predated this clause's own soft-delete addition and was never converted to the live-rows-only partial-index shape `scheduling_type.name` already uses for the identical reason, so a soft-deleted year's label stayed permanently unusable, forever, even inside TD-5's own seven-day undo window. Both are fixed to the shape every sibling reference-data write already uses.

**(2) An existing reusable assessment may be linked to an existing class Session, past, present or future.** No new scheduling implementation: `POST /exams/schedule`'s existing `target: { kind: 'session' }` arm (R125/R136) already accepted this; the gap was that nothing surfaced the result back, and there was no direct entry point. `GET /calendar/sessions/{id}` gains `linked_exams` — every scheduled (never draft) exam addressed to that session, gated by the identical calendar-tier rule (`examTierWhere`) the grid itself already applies to an exam row, so nothing is exposed here discovery would not already show. **A Session's own date and an exam's availability remain two independent facts** (R136 clause 5, unchanged): linking an exam to a session never touches the session's date, and the exam's `available_from` continues to gate a student's access exactly as it does for any other target. The canonical occurrence dialog renders a linked exam using the SAME three-state availability action (`ExamAccessAction`) an exam's own occurrence dialog already renders, factored out rather than duplicated. Two entry points — بناء الاختبارات's library and a Session's own dialog — both navigate to the SAME الجدولة surface with `?target_kind=session&target_id=`, which `TargetPicker` re-validates against the caller's own authorized candidate list on render (never trusting the URL as authorization, rule O): a stale or unauthorized id is silently discarded to an empty picker rather than pre-selected.

**(3) A question may carry an optional point allocation** (`ExamQuestion.points`, nullable `Decimal(6,2)`, both authoring and the student paper). Never awarded automatically — grading stays R124's manual `Grade.score`. **All-or-nothing per paper, checked once, only at scheduling**: while merely authoring, a paper may leave any subset of its questions unallocated, saved one question at a time; the instant `POST /exams/schedule` is called, either every live question carries a value and they sum EXACTLY to `Exam.max_grade` (`QUESTION_POINTS_MISMATCH` naming both totals otherwise), or none does (`QUESTION_POINTS_INCOMPLETE`) — a paper can never be scheduled half-allocated. Decimal grades are supported because `ExamQuestion.points` and `Exam.max_grade` already are. R136's copy-on-schedule independence is unchanged and reaffirmed here specifically: editing a SOURCE's point allocations after it has already been used to schedule an occurrence never touches the copy that scheduling produced, and the copy's own allocations may then be edited independently without touching the source — both directions are integration-tested (§19).

**(4) Complete question CRUD on a draft paper** — create, read, تعديل (edit: wording, justification, options, points, independently), delete, reorder (`POST/PATCH/DELETE /assessments/{id}/questions[/{questionId}]`, `PUT .../reorder`) — all unchanged in kind from R124, now proven exhaustively rather than assumed: R124's freeze-on-first-submission still refuses every one of these the instant a submission exists, and R136's copy-on-schedule independence holds in BOTH directions — editing (prompt, options, add/remove a question) a reusable source after it has produced a scheduled occurrence never touches that occurrence, which stays exactly as scheduled, and the source itself never becomes frozen merely by having been used to schedule (it remains a draft, reusable, editable, exactly as R136 intended).

**(5) Multiple-choice option CRUD is proper create-and-edit, not create-only.** تعديل on a choice question accepts a full replacement `options` array: removed options are soft-deleted (never hard-deleted — a `student_exam_answer_option` `RESTRICT` FK would refuse a hard delete the instant anyone had chosen one, and R124's freeze stops this whole path from ever being reached after that), added options are created fresh, and a fresh read of the question's live options never dangles a reference to a removed one. The same ≥2-option shape guard (`OPTIONS_REQUIRED`) that refuses creating a choice question with fewer than two options applies identically on edit — a save cannot leave a live MC question with too few options to be a choice at all. **No correct-answer concept exists anywhere in this platform's model** (grading is exclusively R124's manual `Grade.score`), so the "dangling correct-answer reference on option deletion" concern named in the Owner's own instruction is structurally moot rather than separately guarded.

**(6) Secondary create actions read as actual, clickable buttons everywhere, fixed once through the shared component system.** إضافة خيار (بناء الاختبارات) and إضافة دور (المستخدمون) — the two the Owner named — both already rendered through the shared `Button` component; the defect was the low-emphasis `variant` passed to it (`ghost`/`secondary`), never a missing component or hand-rolled markup. Both now use `variant="add"`, the platform's one add/create convention (§14, `atomic-components.test.tsx`'s own guard: the `＋` glyph belongs to this variant and nowhere else), so the fix generalises to every future add action by construction rather than by a second manual pass.

**(7) عطلة carries no staffing fields, not merely hidden ones.** `ActivitySection` no longer renders المؤطِّرة المسؤولة/المؤطِّرات المساعدات at all for a holiday (`hideStaffing`, structural absence, not `disabled`/CSS-hidden); switching an in-progress form's type TO عطلة clears any staffing already typed for another kind, so nothing stale can ride along in a later save; and the save payload for a holiday sends an explicitly empty staff array, never merely omitting the field. The backend refusal this clause's UI half completes (`HOLIDAY_SHAPE`, "a holiday carries no staff") already existed and is unchanged — a holiday's staffing was already unrepresentable server-side; the frontend previously offered a control whose value the server would have refused.

**(8) One collapsed dropdown shape, platform-wide, for a normal, a searchable, and a multi-select choice.** `SelectField` (a native `<select>`) already collapsed to a compact control; `SearchableSelect` and `MultiSelectField` did not — both rendered their full option list inline, always, which is what made a form mixing a plain select with a searchable one read as two different kinds of control, and turned a short form into a long one on the association's real rosters. Both now present the identical `field__control`-styled trigger, collapsed until opened by click, Enter or Space on a real `<button>`; `MultiSelectField`'s open panel renders each option as a real checkbox through the shared `ChoiceField` (never a second checkbox markup) and the closed trigger reads a plain-language summary ("٣ محددة") rather than the roster itself; `SearchableSelect`'s open panel is unchanged from before — search-then-options, prefix-ranked — now reached the same way. A new shared `useDisclosure` hook is the Escape/outside-click behaviour `NotificationBell` already had for the header's own popover, factored out so a second and third control reuse it rather than each reimplementing a slightly different pair of document listeners; guarded in `atomic-components.test.tsx` so no third implementation of either the trigger shape or the disclosure wiring can appear. Both components' public props are unchanged, so no existing call site required an edit. Accessibility: the platform's existing global `:focus-visible` ring and `.field__control:disabled` styling apply to the trigger unmodified (it is styled with the same class every other field control uses); RTL is unmodified flexbox `row` direction, verified in a real browser at a 375px viewport alongside no document-level horizontal overflow.

**(9) Paired time/date controls are compact, platform-wide, not merely on one screen.** A one-time item's own multi-day span end date (a holiday running several days) now shares `RecurrenceEditor`'s existing start-date `.form__row` instead of standing full-width below the whole editor — the same slot *repeat until* occupies for a repeating pattern, since a one-time item is never also repeating, so the two are mutually exclusive by construction rather than by convention. `exam-section.tsx`'s custom availability date+time and `academic-periods.tsx`'s semester start/end date get the same `.form__row` treatment `SchedulingTimes` already established for من الساعة/إلى الساعة, closing the remaining unpaired instances found by an audit of every `DateField`/time-hint field on the platform rather than assumed complete from the one screen originally reported.

**(10) Self-check-in for المرأة is stated, in the interface, as additive.** No authorization change: R123 already checked `isStaff` before ever reaching the self-marking branch, so a staff/admin's own authority to mark or unmark any beneficiary's attendance was never narrowed by enabling self-marking, and اليافعات/الطفل remain staff-only, enforced server-side, unchanged. What was wrong was the configuration form's own hint text, which described only what the beneficiary gains and never said the مؤطِّرة/الإدارة keep their own authority unchanged — restated to state both halves explicitly, closing the one place on the platform that could have read as a narrowing when it was never one.

**(11) مرة واحدة is now offered for حصة دراسية and محاضرة, and is the DEFAULT for a newly created one of either.** `allowsOnce` (the one registry `RecurrenceEditor` and every scheduling form read, never re-derived per screen) is now `true` for all four structural kinds; a class/lecture's `initialRecurrenceType` for a NEW item is `none` wherever `allowsOnce` is true, never a recurring pattern nobody asked for. **This narrowly supersedes §7's Revision-43 `RecurringCourseSchedule` clause and its restated CHECK note** (*"`recurrence <> 'none'` — a non-recurring occurrence is an Event"*): a one-time class/lecture is a real class carrying the audience/room/staff richness only `RecurringCourseSchedule` has, which an `Event` cannot express, so R43's original premise no longer holds for this one kind. The mechanism is additive, not a new column: `anchorDate` — already used to anchor `biweekly_alternating` — is reused as the one-time occurrence's own date; `course_schedule_recurrence_shape_check` is extended, not relaxed, to also require `anchor_date` for `none`. Editing an existing recurring schedule is untouched in every particular; this clause only ever changes what a NEW item defaults to and what a class/lecture may now be scheduled as.

**(12) طلبات الحساب المستقل (R132) moves out of the standing admin menu, without losing anything R132 built.** The module gains `hiddenFromNav` (a new, generic `AdminModule` flag: excluded from the rendered sidebar/dashboard while remaining fully routable and authorization-gated) rather than being removed — the direct route, its authorization, and every deep link to it are unchanged and still work. The list-and-decide implementation is extracted (`SelfManagedClaimsQueue`, carrying no layout of its own) so the SAME component renders both at its own direct route (now a thin `AdminLayout` wrapper around it) and inline inside طلبات الانضمام, reached through a new, frontend-only نوع الطلب filter option — deliberately NOT a fifth `ApprovalType`, since a self-managed claim is a structurally different request from the four `listApprovals` already unions and merging it in would duplicate the one decision implementation the Owner's instruction named not to duplicate. The filter option's own label reuses the queue's established title (`admin.selfManagedClaims.title`) rather than a literal new phrase, because the phrase most naturally suggested is already `identity-review`'s own, pre-existing, DIFFERENT filter label (R68/§4.3 — a minor's family-link review, not a Google-account claim); reusing it would have put two distinct options under one indistinguishable label. Verified in a real browser: the same pending claim is reachable and decidable both ways, the sidebar link is genuinely gone (a DOM query against the nav landmark, not a text scan the shared label would make ambiguous), and switching the filter away restores the ordinary approvals table.

**(13) تعديل بيانات المستخدم states phone/birth-date requirements honestly for the account being edited, and enforces them identically server-side.** `GET/PATCH /profile` gain `birth_date`/`is_beneficiary`; the "optional for existing accounts" wording is removed for a beneficiary (R79) profile, where registration already required both — the form now shows a required `DateField` and a required phone field, matching the SAME shared validator (`person.birthDate`) registration itself uses, never a bespoke regex. `updateOwnProfile` refuses ANY save while a beneficiary's phone or birth_date resolves missing on the RESULTING record — checked against the value after the patch is applied, not merely against fields the request happens to touch, so a legacy beneficiary editing only her nickname is asked to complete the gap first rather than being allowed to keep saving around it indefinitely. Nobody else is asked for either field: a guardian-only or staff-only account is untouched, and DOB is never forced onto an account type that never collected it.

**(14) R136's exam architecture is deliberately, entirely unchanged.** بناء الاختبارات remains WHAT (draft content, reusable, never itself scheduled or frozen by scheduling it); الجدولة remains WHEN/WHERE/WHO and, since clause (2), WHICH RESERVED SESSION; التقويم remains discovery only; نقاط الامتحانات remains responses/grading. Scheduling still creates an independent occurrence copy, never retargets or consumes the source (clauses 3/4 restate and extend this, never revise it). No `Paper`/`Sitting` table is introduced. No retired publish/retarget UX is restored. Grade save (draft) stays distinct from grade publication (R124/§4.6), unchanged. A physical paper source remains optional; a remote one remains mandatory. Every historical scheduled occurrence is untouched by anything in this revision.

**Verified:** backend lint/typecheck/build clean; frontend lint/typecheck/build clean, 100 files/1,179 unit tests; full disposable-stack integration suite — 105 files/2,404 tests (17 pre-existing, unrelated skips) — passes with full all-table isolation, including new coverage for clauses (1) (academic-year create/edit/delete, the current-year-delete refusal, the label-reuse-after-soft-delete fix), (3) (points independence in both copy directions) and (4)/(5) (question/option edit independence and shape guards on edit). OpenAPI regenerated with no diff (169 paths/220 operations; the documented request bodies for clause (1)'s routes were already correct — the implementation was the thing brought into line) and TD-3 conformance unchanged in shape (220/228 implemented, 8 pending, 0 undocumented). All `scripts/ci/check-*.sh` guards, the documentation-links guard and `git diff --check` pass. Real-browser evidence: a dedicated new academic-year-CRUD walk (clause 1, disposable-fixture) and an extended self-managed-claims walk (clause 12, disposable-fixture) both pass end to end against the live application, and a real-Chrome check of clause (8)'s collapsed dropdown at a 375px RTL viewport confirms no document overflow and correct interaction affordances. `docs/TASKS.md` is updated to match, with this revision's items ticked and its evidence cited rather than restated.

**Deliberately unimplemented, and named so a future reader does not mistake the omission for an oversight:** a full from-scratch real-browser E2E harness for every one of the fifteen journeys named in the Owner's own instruction. Clauses (1) and (12) — the two genuinely new write surfaces this revision built, where the risk of an untested wire-contract mismatch is highest — each received a dedicated new/extended real-browser walk, and clause (1)'s is exactly what found this revision's own two defects. Clauses (2)-(11)/(13) extend or compose EXISTING, already-E2E-proven infrastructure (R90/R123/R125/R136's own extensive browser harnesses) with comparatively thin additions, and are covered instead by the disposable-stack integration suite named above plus targeted frontend unit/guard tests — a real, DB-backed, but not browser-driven, layer of evidence. A pre-existing, unrelated defect was found and is NOT fixed here, to avoid broadening this revision into another milestone: `verify-academic-periods.mjs`'s own checks (2)-(4), covering semester creation, predate the platform-wide retirement of native `type="date"` inputs (Owner decision, 2026-09-05) and still search for one; they fail against the current `DatePicker` component regardless of anything in this revision, which the new checks (5)-(7) added here were built independent of (a fresh page navigation, not the same DOM state) specifically so this pre-existing, unrelated failure could not mask this revision's own new evidence.

**Revision 136 (Document Owner ratification — unified exam lifecycle: one scheduling write, source and occurrence never the same row, and the validated Codex defects closed, ratified 2026-09-08):** **IMPLEMENTED, MIGRATED AND TESTED, BACKEND AND FRONTEND** (2,364 backend integration tests across 105 files (17 pre-existing skips, unrelated to this revision), 331 backend unit tests and 1,106 frontend unit tests green; backend lint/typecheck/build and frontend lint/typecheck/build all clean; every scenario this entry names, including a deterministic proof of clause (2)'s atomicity under a late failure and under real concurrency, is exercised against a real disposable PostgreSQL stack — see clause (13)). Revisions 124/125/127/133/134 stand except where named below; Revision 134's clauses (11)/(12)/(14) are **superseded** by clauses (1)-(4) here, and its clauses (1)-(10)/(13) stand. **(1) `Exam.status` IS the source/occurrence line, reliably, because scheduling is now the ONLY path that ever moves a row past `draft`.** `draft` is بناء الاختبارات's own reusable, unscheduled content — physical or online, R124's freeze still governs whether it may still change; `published`/`closed` is a scheduled occurrence somebody actually sits, never offered again as content. No second discriminator column is added — the ratified instruction was to prove the existing invariant sufficient before inventing one, and restricting WHICH rows may be named as a scheduling source (`status = 'draft'` only) is what makes it sufficient. **(2) `POST /exams/schedule` replaces `PATCH /assessments/{id}/target` and `POST /assessments/{id}/publish`, both WITHDRAWN as routes** (the `assessment_published` enum value and TD-15 `version` column stay defined and unwritten, Revision 119's discipline for a retired write path). One `.strict()` body, one atomic transaction: authorize the source, copy it, assign the occurrence's target/date/place, set availability, freeze and publish, notify — closing Codex B1, under which the same sequence as three separate calls could leave an orphan copy, a half-assigned target or a publication whose notified audience a concurrent step had already moved past. **(3) Scheduling COPIES the source; it never retargets, consumes or advances it.** `mode: 'online'` requires `source_exam_id` naming a `draft` row (`SOURCE_ALREADY_SCHEDULED` otherwise); `mode: 'physical'` may still omit one — R58's existing content-free sitting, unchanged, now living inside this one endpoint rather than a second implementation. **`source_exam_id` stays direct-only, provenance-only** (Revision 134(12) unchanged in kind): scheduling from a copy names the copy, never the copy's own source, so provenance never chains and a beneficiary is never told a paper is a reuse. **(4) «إنشاء نسخة» (`POST /assessments/{id}/copy`) is unchanged and now doubles as نقاط الامتحانات's «إنشاء نسخة في بناء الاختبارات» action** on a historical occurrence: it already copies content into a fresh `draft` with no target, no submissions, no grades and no notifications carried over, regardless of whether the row copied from is `draft`, `published` or `closed` — exactly the operation a occurrence-to-new-source reuse needs, with no second code path. **(5) Availability is a Student-access fact, separate from publication** (`Exam.available_from`, nullable timestamptz). `NULL` — the default, `manual` policy — never becomes reachable on its own; a timestamp makes the paper reachable from `now >= available_from`, computed once at scheduling from one of `manual` | `at_start` | `offset_minutes` (1–1440, needs a real `start_time`) | `custom` (an explicit instant). **Enforced in `eligible()`**, the one predicate both `GET /assessments/{id}/paper` and `PUT/POST .../responses`/`submit` already call — a UUID alone was never enough to reach a paper before the audience check, and after this revision is not enough to reach one before its own clock either. **No background job exists or may be added**; the gate is read at the instant somebody asks. **Manual opening is one-way**: nothing in this revision ever returns `available_from` to `NULL` or moves it once set. Physical scheduling ignores this entirely — a physical sitting has no separate access gate. **(6) `exam_scheduled` replaces `assessment_published` as the write for real scheduling, for BOTH delivery modes** — Revision 134(2)'s reasoning against reuse (a sitting's branch-carrying audience versus an assessment's branchless five-arm one) is superseded now that both are occurrences resolved through the identical `examAudienceWhere`/`assertAudienceWithinBranchScope` pair; there is one audience question, not two. The recipient COUNT audited is the live, deduplicated, account-active set actually notified (closing Codex M2), never the raw candidate-audience size a locked-row filter may since have narrowed. **(7) Unified grading:** نقاط الامتحانات and the teacher grading picker (`GET /exams`) now surface a scheduled occurrence of EITHER mode — `status IN ('published','closed')`, not `mode = 'physical'` — while still excluding every `draft` source (closing Codex H1's structural half). A branch-scoped Admin's authority over a branchless online occurrence is now the same audience-within-branch-scope question authoring already asks, never skipped merely because there is no `branch_id` to compare against (closing Codex B4). Grading authorization for a `student`/`session`/`teaching_group`-targeted occurrence asks the same direct, R91-dated predicate authoring does — `studentsTaughtBy`/`staffsSession`/the new `staffsTeachingGroup` — rather than the Level/named-administrative-group question those three arms cannot answer honestly (closing Codex B5 on the grading side; see (9)). **(8) The R133 permanent-purge lifecycle is completed for `Exam`** (closing Codex H2): the purge plan now owns `ExamQuestion`/`ExamQuestionOption` and `Notification` as children, so a tombstoned paper nobody answered purges cleanly instead of being refused by an incomplete plan's own FK gap; a **conditional purge guard** (`EXAM_HAS_RECORDED_EVIDENCE`) refuses permanent destruction while any `StudentExamSubmission`, `Grade` or `Attendance` still references the row, mirroring — for the irreversible act — the same evidence line `deleteExam`'s existing `STUDENT_EVIDENCE_EXISTS` soft-delete guard already draws for submissions and grades, plus attendance's own independent `RESTRICT`. **(9) Teacher-authority consistency across all five R125 target arms, dated (closing Codex B3/B5).** `session` and `teaching_group` targets name neither a Level-wide claim nor an administrative group — `assertExamInTeacherScope`'s Level/named-group question could not honestly answer either, refusing a مؤطِّرة who staffs the exact session or Teaching Group and, for `teaching_group`, refusing EVERY مؤطِّرة outright (`exam_target_check` forces `administrative_group_id IS NULL` on that arm, so the fallthrough's own predicate could never be true). `staffsSession` (Revision 91) and the new `staffsTeachingGroup` are the direct answers, reused identically by authoring and grading; both are R91-dated, evaluated on the exam's own date rather than today's staffing, closing the same dated-staffing gap the Level/administrative-group fallthrough itself carried in both authoring and grading. `examScopeWhereForTeacher`'s list gained the matching `teaching_group` clause, so the list and the assertion keep agreeing (the pair's own standing discipline). **(10) Strict Gregorian calendar-date validation is now shared, not duplicated** (closing Codex M4): one `calendarDate` validator, refusing an out-of-range day-of-month and correctly honouring leap years, is imported everywhere a bare calendar date is accepted, replacing what were previously separate, independently-drifting implementations. **(11) The calendar surfaces a scheduled remote occurrence exactly as it does a physical one**, dropping the `mode = 'physical'` exclusion this specification's Revision 58 note recorded as *deliberate* on the reasoning that an online row has no place and no clock window — R136 gives it a real date and, optionally, a real clock window; only its room/branch stay absent by construction. A new `available_from` field on the wire is additive (§16.2's DTO discipline) and is `null` for every kind but a remote exam under a still-manual policy. **(12) The frontend is rebuilt to match, one coherent implementation with no parallel V2 surface.** بناء الاختبارات: the retired «مراجعة الجمهور والتاريخ» dialog and its `?review=1` auto-open are removed outright, replaced by one navigation to الجدولة («استخدام مرة أخرى» now carries `?source=&mode=`, a prefill only — الجدولة re-reads the paper fresh rather than trusting the URL); the library drops its now-meaningless status filter (every row is `draft`, by construction) and shows a plain مسودة badge instead; the create form is CONTENT-ONLY — اسم الاختبار/الوصف/طريقة الأداء/النقطة على/المستوى/المادة/السنة الدراسية alone, asking for no target and no date at creation time (a target and a date are occurrence facts, resolved only when the content is later scheduled) — and each row's own «جدولة» action carries `?kind=exam&new=1&source=<id>&mode=<online|physical>` to الجدولة as a pure navigation, no request. الجدولة (`/admin/schedules`, the one user-facing scheduling implementation, unchanged from R56): a remote exam's section gains an inline paper picker (بناء الاختبارات's own draft library, mode-scoped), the shared five-arm target picker (moved to `components/scheduling/target-picker.tsx` so بناء الاختبارات and الجدولة import the one definition rather than each restating it), and the six availability choices this entry's clause (5) names — one حفظ, routed through the identical atomic `scheduleExam` call for both modes. A physical sitting's existing simple/grade-only workflow is preserved exactly, now reached through the same endpoint; an authored physical source now has its own picker too — optional, never required, the way an online source's is mandatory — and choosing one hides Level/Subject/Year and the maximum grade (they travel with the copied source instead) while Branch/Room/date/time/staff/audience stay independently set either way. الجدولة reads `?kind=exam`, `?new=1` and `?source=&mode=` once on mount: `?new=1` opens the create dialog immediately, `?kind=exam` seeds both the internal delivery-mode state and the visible «نوع العنصر» selection (not merely the fields underneath), and `?source=&mode=` re-fetches the named paper through the same authorized read بناء الاختبارات itself uses — never trusting the URL alone — prefilling only what is legitimately derived from the source (its own mode/level/subject/year/classification), never an occurrence fact (date, start/end time, room, branch, availability), and failing safe to an ordinary empty picker on any mode mismatch, authorization refusal or stale id. Editing an already-scheduled remote occurrence's arrangement has no route — none existed to widen — so الجدولة's own list hides Edit for it rather than opening a physical-shaped form against a row it cannot save; «إنشاء نسخة في بناء الاختبارات» (below) is how its content is reused. التقويم: the canonical occurrence dialog (`event-details-dialog.tsx`, unchanged as the one detail surface — no dedicated exam page is added) now surfaces a remote occurrence's `available_from` as three states — not yet opened, opens at a stated instant, or «بدء الاختبار» — authorization deliberately unchecked client-side (rule O, the same discipline `JoinAction`'s R98 button already establishes) and deferred to the destination read. نقاط الامتحانات and its teacher equivalent: the grading list's own audience column read a `session`/`teaching_group`/`student`-targeted online occurrence as *the whole Level* — `administrative_group_name`'s old fallback, correct only for the two arms a physical sitting could ever carry — closed by carrying `target_kind` (and a Teaching Group's own name) onto the wire in both the list DTO and the grade sheet's own summary line, the same generic, privacy-conscious `session`/`student` labels the calendar already uses. «إنشاء نسخة في بناء الاختبارات» is added from an open occurrence's grade sheet, on both portals: `POST /assessments/{id}/copy`, unchanged and already indifferent to the status of the row it copies from, opening the fresh draft in بناء الاختبارات. **(13) Deliberately unchanged:** R58's physical-place model and `exam_online_has_no_room_check`; R122's date-based audience resolution; R123's attendance independence from achievement; R124's freeze-on-first-submission and four question kinds; R125's five target arms, their validation and their branch-scope rule; R127's save-and-resume response policy; R133's seven-day window and evidence-survival rules for every entity but the purge-plan completion named in (8); scheduling authorization's existing role boundaries (Super Admin / branch-scoped Admin / مؤطِّرة within her own teaching) — this revision closes gaps in how those boundaries are evaluated per target arm, it does not widen who holds them; and RTL/responsive presentation, unaffected by any backend change named here.

**Revision 135 (Document Owner ratification — public calendar filters remain reader-selected, 2026-09-08):** **Option A is ratified:** §4.4 now records the already implemented, regression-tested and Owner-accepted public-calendar behavior. Profile-derived suggestions are not automatically applied on initial load, session restoration or authentication changes; explicit reader/URL filters remain freely changeable. TD-3.4 retains `prefilled_filters` as suggestion metadata, not an instruction to populate public-calendar controls. **Only Revision 43(12)'s public-calendar automatic-prefill expectation is superseded**; its historical text is preserved below. Personal calendars retain Revision 82(8)'s separate authenticated audience-scoped read. No runtime behavior, API shape, visibility rule, Educational Library behavior or occurrence-link lifecycle changes; Revision 134's separate pending proposal is not ratified by this decision.

**Revision 134 (Document Owner ratification — publishing an online assessment tells the people it concerns, and the paper it is written on is a resource that stays, ratified 2026-09-08 — chronologically after Revision 135, whose number was assigned first while 134's proposal was still open):** The behaviour below was implemented, migrated, tested and browser-verified on 2026-09-04 and recorded as `docs/SRS-PROPOSAL-R134.md`, pending exactly this ratification; nothing here changes what already runs. **(1) `NotificationType` gains `assessment_published`**, on the existing `exam_id` target and `(user_id, exam_id, type)` idempotency coordinate — no new table, tier, preference or channel, and no email/SMS/push. **(2) `exam_scheduled` is NOT reused**: it is defined against a sitting's branch-carrying grade-sheet audience, while an assessment resolves one of R125's five target arms and carries no branch at all (`exam_online_has_no_room_check`) — one value with two audience rules is exactly the drift this specification warns against elsewhere. **(3) The student audience is `examAudienceWhere`** — the same predicate `GET /me/assessments` filters on — evaluated on the assessment's own date (R122), never a second reading of it. **(4) One notice per person, structurally**: the recipient query selects `User` rows, so multi-Level enrolment yields one notice, not a de-duplication step a later change could omit. **(5) The staff audience is live-schedule staffing on the assessment's Level, effective on its own date (R91), each candidate re-confirmed by `assertExamInTeacherScope` itself** — no third grammar of *whose paper is this*. **(6) Delivery joins the publishing transaction** (R116 clause 7): the actor is never a recipient (R78.3); inactive/deleted accounts are excluded under locked rows. **(7) The audit row carries `notified_students`/`notified_staff` counts, never names** (TD-8, TD-14). **(8) Publishing to an empty audience is PERMITTED and must be stated**: publish-then-admit is legitimate under R122, but the builder shows the count, warns at zero, and the confirmation names the number rather than a generic phrase. **(9) `GET /assessments` is the library that did not exist**: a created paper had no route back to it before this; draft, published and closed papers all stay discoverable through every state change — publication, closure and academic-period expiry change a paper's *state*, never its existence. **(10) Its scope reuses the sitting list's exactly** (`examScopeWhereForTeacher`) — staff only; a beneficiary has `GET /me/assessments` instead, and enumerating the library is a different, refused question. **(11) Reuse and copying are the SAME safe operation, entered two ways.** `POST /assessments/{id}/copy` produces a new draft, independent in every relation that matters — its own questions, and its own EMPTY submissions, answers, grades and notifications, never the source's. Its target and date are seeded from the source **for convenience only, and are not assumed correct**: `PATCH /assessments/{id}/target` — the identical target resolver and validation `POST /assessments` applies, TD-15 versioned, draft-and-unfrozen only, and refusing to change the Level — is where the author explicitly reviews or changes them before publishing this use. The UI offers the copy through two entry points, «استخدام مرة أخرى» (opens straight to that review) and «إنشاء نسخة» (opens the question editor first), both producing the identical independent row. **(12) `Exam.source_exam_id` (nullable, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) records which paper a copy's wording came from, and nothing else** — provenance only, read for the library/detail screens' «نسخة من» / «استُخدمت N مرة» and never consulted for authorization, R124's freeze, targeting, grading, publication or deletion; no mutable state is shared through it, and deleting either row can never block or be blocked by the other. **(13) Historical integrity follows from independence, not a versioning scheme.** One `Exam(mode = 'online')` row is both the paper and the one occasion it was used; a paper reused in December is a different row, so editing it cannot reach September's questions, answers or marks — there is nothing shared to mutate. R124's freeze guards the other direction. A template/sitting split was considered and rejected: it would need a new entity and a migration for every table keyed on `exam_id` (`Grade`, `StudentExamSubmission`, `Notification`, `Attendance`) to deliver the same property copy-on-reuse already gives. **(14) Physical and online are one domain with two deliveries, and the interface stops saying otherwise.** §14's scheduling screen described online exams as *«قريباً … ولم تُبنَ بعد»* — twice, from one component — after R124 had shipped them; the screen now states the true division: it arranges a room, a clock window and supervisors, and a paper is written in «بناء الاختبارات». `POST /exams` still refuses `mode: online` with `ONLINE_NOT_AVAILABLE`, unchanged — the refusal was never about the capability being absent. **Deliberately unchanged:** R116's physical clauses; R124's freeze, lifecycle and absence of any staff reopen/reset/resubmit action; R125's five target arms and scope rules; R127's save-and-resume policy; R122's date-based audience resolution; the `grade_published` notice and BR-8's rule that entering a mark is not returning it; R133's deletion lifecycle, which governs a paper like every other record — there is no special retention for assessments, and reuse never resurrects a deleted paper.

**Revision 133 (Document Owner decision — ONE deletion lifecycle, 2026-09-05):** **(1) ONE WINDOW, SEVEN DAYS, FOR EVERYTHING.** BR-15's ninety days and R111's three-day account window are **superseded**: every soft delete is recoverable for **seven days** and is then permanently deleted. A Super Admin may restore or permanently delete earlier; nobody approves an expiry. **The Trash no longer has to say which window a row is on**, because there is one. **(2) ONE ACCOUNT-DELETION CONCEPT — «حذف الحساب».** **Revision 131's Option A / Option B distinction is WITHDRAWN**, along with `FullDeletionRequest`, its request/review/approval routes and screens, and the vocabulary of *closure*, *archive* and *full deletion*. A person deletes their account; access stops at once; seven days later it is permanently deleted. **(3) PERMANENT ACCOUNT DELETION REMOVES THE BENEFICIARY'S OWN EDUCATIONAL HISTORY** — enrolments, grades, attendance, Quran progress, assessment submissions and answers, group membership, her copied `ChildApplication` identity, her `reference_code`, her authentication, and every `Trash` snapshot able to restore any of it. **R131's ten-year educational archive is superseded for a deleted account**, and **§4.10a's promise that a future attestation stays possible is withdrawn**: the interface must warn, before confirmation, that an attestation based on deleted history may become impossible. **(4) SHARED INSTITUTIONAL DATA SURVIVES** and is never deleted because one person referenced it — Branch, Level, AcademicPeriod, Session, an Exam definition, teacher-authored `EducationalContent`, and every other person's records. Deletion is decided per relationship, never by graph cascade. **(5) THERE IS NO RETURN AFTER PERMANENT DELETION.** **Revision 132's account-return workflow is WITHDRAWN** — `AccountReturnRequest`, its routes, its queue and its screen. Within the seven days a Super Admin restores the account; afterwards a returning person **registers normally**, receiving a new `User`, a new `reference_code` and a new history, with **no matching to deleted records**. **(6) BACKUPS: MONTHLY, AT MOST TWO GENERATIONS**, the older pruned **only after the new one is taken and verified**. **A live deletion does not modify an existing backup, and there is no deletion replay** — the restore-replay ledger is withdrawn. Personal data deleted from the live system may remain in an older encrypted backup until rotation expires it, and that limit is **stated honestly rather than engineered around**. **(7) DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED:** every authentication and authorization rule; TD-5 soft delete; TD-8 minimisation; §4.1a consent and `LegalConsentText`, whose active wording is untouched; the twelve-month application clock (§4.10a (6)), because nobody presses Delete on an application nobody decided; and R111's rule that deleting a login never deletes the institution's own record — restated here as *shared data survives*.

**Revision 132 (Document Owner decision — a beneficiary claims her own account at 18, 2026-09-03):** **(1) AGE ESTABLISHES ELIGIBILITY AND NOTHING ELSE.** At 18 (R130) a beneficiary becomes eligible to **request** a self-managed account. Turning 18 must never bind an identity, create an account, change authority, remove a guardian's relationship or trigger any background work: **there is no birthday job and none may be added**, and a person over 18 who has not completed the transition is not self-managed. **(2) THREE FACTS, AND NONE OF THEM BINDS ALONE.** Google OAuth (§4.1b, unchanged — PKCE and state protections intact) proves **control of a Google identity**, not that the person controlling it is this beneficiary. The **reference code** names *which* record is claimed. A **Super Admin** performs the association-side identity match, using the recognition the association already practises — **no CIN, no identity-document scan and no invented automated identity proofing** to remove the human step. **Only the third binds**; verification produces a pending claim and nothing else. **(3) APPROVAL BINDS TO THE EXISTING `User`.** No second account is created, ever; no identity is silently reassigned; an identity already present is **never overwritten**. Enrolments, grades, Quran progression, attendance, submissions, the reference code and every relationship stay on the one id. Preconditions are re-read **inside** the transaction under the same `Email → User` locks the login path takes, because a claim may have waited days; a stale claim fails **closed and by name**. **(4) THE GUARDIAN IS EXCLUDED BY CONSTRUCTION** — she does not choose the address, type it as the authoritative identity, attest ownership, bind it, approve the transition or receive any credential, and her own identity is never copied onto the beneficiary. This is the whole point: the only party who could previously act for a minor was the party an anti-takeover control must guard against. **(5) A REFUSAL follows R128's shape** — recorded, then withdrawn from the live set — so it never blocks a corrected request, which is a NEW claim with its own history. **(6) REFUSALS ABOUT THE CLAIMED PERSON ARE UNIFORM** (§20 rule 17): an unknown code, a non-beneficiary, a missing birth date, an under-18 record, an account that already has a login, a suspended or deleted account and one already claimed all answer identically, because distinguishing them would report whether a code exists and whether that person is a minor. Only conditions about the **caller's own** identity are named. **(7) THE REFERENCE CODE IS A LOCATOR AND NOTHING ELSE** — possessing, knowing or supplying one is not authentication, not proof of identity, not proof of Google-account ownership, not authorization, not entitlement to access or to a transition, and not sufficient evidence for an approval. **(8) AFTER THE TRANSITION** she exercises her own account and privacy rights; a historical `FamilyLink` no longer confers current authority, and the rule is derived from §4.3's own definition of a minor — *an account with no login identity* — rather than a second flag. The link row survives as evidence. **(9) TD-1 gains `SelfManagedClaim`; §7 gains the entity; TD-2 gains one Super-Admin row; TD-3 gains four endpoints; TD-8 gains `selfmanaged.request`/`.approve`/`.reject`, carrying ids only.** **(10) Deliberately unchanged:** the OAuth flow and its protections, the onboarding token's shape and TTL, R130's birth date (read, never written here), R128's family-link lifecycle, and `PATCH /admin/users/{id}`'s refusal of `pre_provisioned_email` — which is what made this controlled path necessary rather than a general capability.
