**Revision 131 (Document Owner decisions — educational retention and two deletion requests, 2026-09-03 — POLICY AND ARCHITECTURE ONLY):** **(0) NOTHING IS IMPLEMENTED BY THIS REVISION.** No purge job, retention job or automated destruction exists, and none may be written until the reconciliations in §4.10a are closed; the deletion the platform performs is still R111's de-identification, unchanged. **(1) IDENTIFIABLE EDUCATIONAL HISTORY IS RETAINED FOR TEN YEARS AFTER THE BENEFICIARY'S LAST EDUCATIONAL ACTIVITY** — **the association's own purpose-based policy**, for educational continuity, former-beneficiary requests and attestations. **It is not prescribed, reviewed or approved by the CNDP, and no document, screen or notice may describe it as such or as a universal requirement of Moroccan law.** **(2) "Last educational activity" is DERIVED from canonical durable facts**, never a timestamp nobody maintains. **(3) TWO REQUESTS, never presented as one:** **Option A** closes the account and preserves the minimal educational archive, in which **the `reference_code` survives** as protected pseudonymous data — the archive is *not* anonymous because the login is gone; **Option B** requests deletion of the educational record too, is **Super Admin only**, is a request rather than a browser-initiated cascade, and removes the reference code with it. Option B's screen must warn **before** confirmation that an attestation may afterwards be impossible; **no dark patterns**, no preselected destructive option. **(4) NEITHER PROMISES "ZERO ROWS ANYWHERE"** — narrowly necessary evidence survives under its own rules — and **audit may never be a pretext for retaining the deleted educational content**. **(5) A MINOR'S request comes from a live approved guardian and still requires Super Admin review**; a self-managed adult (R132) exercises her own rights and a historical link makes nobody the owner of an adult's account. **(6) APPLICATIONS: twelve months maximum**, rejected from rejection and never-converted pending from their own reference point — a maximum, not indefinite retention. **(7) THE R111 ↔ R122 CONTRADICTION IS RESOLVED**: R122 promised a future attestation while R111 cleared every field that could match a returning person to her record, including the code, and neither cited the other. The code survives Option A, is removed by Option B, and **must never be a hidden back door** into history Option B deleted. **Not yet implemented** — `deIdentifyAccount` still clears it. **(8) BACKUPS:** a deletion reaches the **live** system; finite-lifetime encrypted backups may hold an older copy until they expire, and **nothing may promise immediate byte-level erasure from backups already written**. A restore must not resurrect data already deleted from the live authoritative system. Not implemented. **(9) The measured data map** — 42 foreign keys referencing `user.id`, against R111's 35 — lives in the development handbook and **extends R111 §3 rather than replacing it**. **(10) Deliberately unchanged:** R111's classification in every particular except the reference code, BR-15's ninety days, R111's three-day account window, TD-5, TD-8's minimisation, §4.1a consent, and R59.4's quarantine question, which remains open and is not authorised by anything here.

**Revision 130 (Document Owner decision — a full date of birth for every beneficiary, 2026-09-03):** **(1) EVERY BENEFICIARY CARRIES A FULL DATE OF BIRTH** — adult women, teenage and child beneficiaries alike — for four purposes: minor/adult status, age-appropriate placement, registration and placement decisions, and eligibility for the R132 transition. **(2) THE QUESTION FOLLOWS ADMISSION, NOT AUTHENTICATION.** The adult applicant is asked; **every child on a family request is asked, per child**, and never inherits a sibling's; a **guardian is not asked** (R129); a **staff request is refused** if it sends one, because a مؤطِّرة is not a beneficiary admission. **(3) `ChildApplication` carries the submitted date** and approval **materialises that exact calendar date** onto the row it creates — copied, never recomputed. **(4) A FULL DATE IS STORED AND AN AGE IS NEVER STORED**: an age is wrong the day after it is written and is derived when needed. **No age column exists anywhere.** It is a wall-clock date (TD-11), never an instant. **(5) It informs placement and gates nothing** (R62.6), and **is not an identifier**. **(6) NOTHING IS FABRICATED OR INFERRED** — not from a Category, a schooling stage, an enrolment, an age-like label or a creation date — and no sentinel is used. **(7) LEGACY BENEFICIARIES HAVE NONE and remain explicitly incomplete**, so the requirement lives at the **write boundary** while the column stays **nullable**, exactly as R122 did for an enrolment's period. **The `NOT NULL` contraction has NOT been performed and cannot honestly be performed until every live beneficiary carries a real recorded date.** **(8) COMPLETION IS NOT CORRECTION**: a missing date is recorded on the Super-Admin-only account surface and **a change to a recorded one is refused by name**, on R80.3's pattern; ordinary callers may not rewrite it. **(9) It is published on ONE read** — that same Super-Admin-only screen — and never in an audit detail or a log. **(10) Note the distinction from §4.1's structural test**: minority is decided by the absence of a login identity, not by the date.

**Revision 129 (Document Owner decisions — a platform account is not association membership, 2026-09-03):** *(No schema, route or wire contract changed; this revision states a model three services already implemented between them and nothing asserted end to end.)* **(1) HAVING A TECHNICAL PLATFORM ACCOUNT DOES NOT MAKE A PERSON A BENEFICIARY, STUDENT OR MEMBER.** A guardian may authenticate solely to register and manage children; she needs `User`, `UserIdentity`, `FamilyLink` and account settings, and **that is all it means**. **(2) THERE IS NO SEPARATE GUARDIAN ACCOUNT TYPE** and none is to be created — a parallel type would fork authentication, consent, deletion and audit for a distinction already representable. **(3) THE ENFORCEMENT IS THAT THREE FACTS SHARE ONE SET**: `Enrollment`, the durable beneficiary fact and the `student` role are all written from the people an approval **places**, and a parent who arrived with child applications places nobody. **A fourth derivation of *is this person a beneficiary* is not to be added.** **(4) SHE RECEIVES NO PERSONAL BENEFICIARY SURFACE** — no personal enrolment, Level, grade, Quran progression, attendance or assessment history — and **never appears in a beneficiary, student or member list** because she authenticated. **(5) IF SHE LATER JOINS SHE USES THE SAME ACCOUNT**: the ordinary process, the beneficiary state attached to the row she already has, her links intact, **no second `User` because a person's business-domain status changed**, and no second membership system. **(6) WHOSE EMAIL IS WHOSE.** A guardian's authenticated address **may also serve as her own contact address** — no second field is to be added merely to copy it — but it **belongs to the guardian**: it must never become the child's authentication identity and is **never copied** into the child's identity, the child's pre-provisioned address, a `ChildApplication` or a `FamilyLink`. **A child created through the minor flow has no email of any kind.** A message concerning a child resolves the **live approved guardian** and reads her **current** address, never a stored copy. The `email` on a profile or directory row is the **account's authentication address**. **(7) This supersedes any earlier reading that a minor signs in through her guardian's address**: §4.3's model stands — a minor is a login-less record reached through an approved link.

**Revision 128 (Document Owner decision — a rejected family link is recorded and withdrawn, 2026-09-03):** **(1) A REJECTION IS RECORDED AND THEN LEAVES THE LIVE SET**, in one act: status, instant, decider, reason, `deleted_at`/`deleted_by` **stamped with that same instant**, a Trash snapshot carrying the decision, and an audit row with both party ids and the reason and **no name**. **(2) THE WEAKER OUTCOME WAS THE MORE PERMANENT ONE**, which is the defect this closes: a revoked link is soft-deleted and frees the pair, while a rejected one stayed **live** and occupied the partial uniqueness rule for ever — so an adult refused because a relationship was recorded wrongly could never make a corrected request, and the only escape was an administrator performing a deletion in order to enable a correction. **(3) A REJECTION IS TERMINAL AND IS NEVER REOPENED**: no `rejected → pending` transition exists or may be added, and a corrected request is a **NEW** `pending` row with its own id and history. **(4) LIVE UNIQUENESS IS OTHERWISE UNCHANGED** — a second pending request and a request against an approved link are still refused. **(5) IT GRANTS NOTHING AND CANNOT BE RESTORED INTO AUTHORITY**: `FamilyLink` is not restorable from Trash, so a generic restore is refused by name. **(6) RETENTION IS BR-15's ORDINARY NINETY DAYS**; no second window exists. **(7) REVISION 118 CLAUSE (3) IS SUPERSEDED** — `DELETE /admin/family-links/{id}/rejected`, the `familylink.purge_rejected` action and `NOT_TERMINAL_REJECTED` are **withdrawn**. They existed only because the row stayed live and never reached Trash; the ordinary lifecycle now does the same work with one implementation instead of two, and two competing deletion lifecycles for one entity is how a destructive verb reaches the wrong row. §0's Revision 118 text is retained as the record of what was decided on 2026-09-02. **(8) ONE IMPLEMENTATION, because a rule stated twice drifts**: both decision paths share it, and the registration-bundle path therefore **gains** the per-link audit row a destructive step needs (§20 rule 11). **(9) Deliberately unchanged:** revocation in every particular, R68's identity review (which soft-deletes an already-*approved* link and is a different transition), TD-1's terminal states, TD-2, and every other Trash rule.

**Revision 127 (Document Owner decision — save-and-resume is the only assessment response policy, 2026-09-03):** **(1) THE CONTRADICTION RESOLVED.** R124's ratified §4.6 said, unconditionally, *"A student saves an incomplete draft and returns to it"*, while §17 said *"`single_submission` exams skip resume"*. Both were in force. **Save-and-resume is the only policy: SAVE ≠ SUBMIT, a saved response stays resumable, and the explicit Submit is final.** **(2) `Exam.access_policy`, the `ExamAccessPolicy` enum and the `SINGLE_SUBMISSION_FINAL` error are WITHDRAWN.** Audited before removal: one writer (a development fixture), one reader that selected the column and never branched on it, no write boundary, no interface, and an error code with **no thrower for its whole life**. **(3) AN UNUSED CAPABILITY IS STILL A CAPABILITY** (R111 clause 2): an empty alternative is a declared behaviour a future maintainer implements by guessing its semantics from an enum name, so it is removed rather than documented as dormant. **(4) §18 loses an unsatisfiable acceptance criterion** — *"single_submission vs save_and_resume enforced"* named behaviour that never existed — and gains the three properties that do: a saved response stays resumable, Save does not Submit, a submitted response is final. **(5) The migration REFUSES rather than assumes**, counting rows that state the withdrawn intention and stopping if any exist; a default value is not a decision somebody made. **(6) The initial migration is not rewritten**: the column's history stays where it happened. **(7) Deliberately unchanged:** the submission lifecycle, every route, R124's freeze rule and its absence of any staff reopen/reset/resubmit action, and the postponed marking engine.

**Revision 126 (Document Owner decision — student educational evidence forbids deleting an assessment, 2026-09-03):** **(1) DELETION IS REFUSED once an assessment holds ANY `StudentExamSubmission` in any state or ANY `Grade` in any status** — either alone is sufficient, and state does not soften it: an in-progress submission is work somebody did and a draft grade is a mark somebody awarded. The refusal carries the two counts and **nothing about any person** (TD-14). **(2) PUBLICATION ALONE DOES NOT BLOCK** — publishing creates no student record, and a published paper nobody sat is still a plan; this is R118.1's rule for schedules applied to the same shape of question. **(3) ATTENDANCE ALONE DOES NOT BLOCK** — R123 makes attendance a fact about the *occurrence* rather than about achievement, and blocking on it would make a cancelled sitting undeletable because one person was marked present. **(4) THE MEASURED FINDING.** Deletion is a soft delete, so none of the foreign keys referencing `exam` ever fired and nine materially different situations behaved identically; every reader's `deleted_at IS NULL` filter then withdrew the paper, the marks and the register together. **(5) R59 CLAUSE (3)'s RATIONALE IS CORRECTED, NOT REWRITTEN.** It admitted `Exam` to the restorable set because *"its deletion cascades to exactly one child table, `ExamStaff`"* — true in July 2026, and made false by R123 attaching attendance and R124 attaching four more tables. §0's historical sentence stands as the record of what was decided then; **the current reasoning is this clause**: restorability now holds for a **narrower row and a better reason**, since the deletable class is exactly the exams carrying no student evidence, for which the cascade really is `ExamStaff` alone. **(6) NO `cancelled` STATE IS INTRODUCED** and **grade visibility is unchanged** — both were considered and both are separate decisions. **(7) THE PERMANENT PURGE IS UNCHANGED AND WAS ALREADY SAFE**: a hard delete trips the foreign keys and is reported as `DEPENDENTS_EXIST`.

**Revision 125 (Document Owner decisions — a Level target does not override branch authorization, and a مؤطِّرة addresses THIS student rather than a whole Level, 2026-09-05):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**(1) A LEVEL TARGET DOES NOT OVERRIDE BRANCH AUTHORIZATION.** A Level spans branches. A **branch-scoped Admin** may create, use or publish a Level-targeted assessment **only when the resulting eligible audience is entirely within her authorized branches**. Beneficiaries at other branches are never silently included, and the target's being a Level widens nothing. **A Super Admin is unaffected** and may author Level-wide assessments across branches under existing global scope. No other Admin permission changes.

**(2) THE RULE IS STATED OVER THE AUDIENCE, so there is one of it.** The check resolves the target through **`examAudienceWhere`** — §4.4c's single definition of *who is this for* — and refuses when anybody it reaches has **no live enrolment at a reachable branch**. That is one rule for all five arms rather than five per-arm branch lookups, and it introduces **no second source of truth for branch membership**: enrolment is the branch fact (§20 rule 22). A beneficiary enrolled at two branches, one of them hers, is somebody she already administers and does not make the target escape.

**(3) IT IS RE-ASKED AT PUBLISH.** The audience is resolved, never stored (§20 rule 22), so a Level entirely at her branch when she drafted the paper may have gained a second branch since. Publishing is the moment it reaches people, so it is the moment the question is asked again. Refused with `403 FORBIDDEN` and `TARGET_OUTSIDE_BRANCH_SCOPE`, naming how many people escape.

**(4) A مؤطِّرة MAY ADDRESS AN INDIVIDUAL BENEFICIARY WITHIN HER OWN TEACHING.** The question is *may this مؤطِّرة address **THIS** student*, **not** *may she address every student in this Level*. The pre-R125 path asked the second — a `student` target names no group, so the scope check degenerated to *do you teach the whole Level* — and a مؤطِّرة staffing one Administrative Group inside it was refused for a student she teaches every week. **The Owner has ruled that too broad.**

**(5) `studentsTaughtBy` IS THE ANSWER, REUSED AND NOT RESTATED.** §4.4c already names it as the derivation behind Quran logging on *her own students* (§4.5), exam authoring (§4.6), sensitive social data (§4.10) and hidden-event visibility (§4.4). It resolves the union of the audiences of the schedules she staffs, including R91's single-occurrence arm. **No new authorization source is introduced.** A مؤطِّرة gains **no association-wide beneficiary lookup**: she may not address a student outside her teaching, another branch's unrelated beneficiary, or anybody merely because she knows a UUID or a reference code.

**(6) OUT OF SCOPE IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ABSENT** (§20 rule 17). A student a caller may not address answers `404`, never a `403` — otherwise the refusal is a lookup, and a reference code becomes a way to discover who exists.

**(7) THE TARGET PICKER IS SERVER-SCOPED, AND IT IS NOT THE BOUNDARY.** `GET /assessments/targets?kind=` answers *what may **I** address*, per caller: the students `studentsTaughtBy` resolves and the occurrences she staffs for a مؤطِّرة; what stays inside her branches for an Admin, including **withholding a Level whose audience escapes them**; everything for a Super Admin. **Staff only** — a beneficiary or a guardian is refused outright rather than handed an empty list. **Every refusal is made again on the write**, so the list exists to stop an author being offered a target that would be refused, never to decide the refusal. **No authorization rule is implemented only in the client**, and ordinary beneficiary-directory access is not broadened: this is a smaller question, not a wider permission.

**(8) THE BUILDER NO LONGER ASKS FOR A UUID.** Four of the five targets required an author to paste an identifier, which is unusable and made the client the only thing between an author and a target she may not address. The picker composes the platform's existing `SearchInput` and `SelectField`; **no second generic picker is introduced** (§14.3).

**Revision 124 (Document Owner decision — the online assessment builder is built, 2026-09-04):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**(1) ONLINE ASSESSMENTS REUSE `Exam`.** §4.6's *"`online` is declared and refused until it is planned as its own feature"* is **superseded**: it is planned, and it is built. **No second assessment entity exists**, and that is the substantive decision rather than a convenience: `Grade` is keyed `(exam_id, student_id)` and already carries the maximum-grade scale (R81), the draft/published split (BR-8), the grade sheet, the student's results screen and the parent visibility rule. A parallel `Assessment` table would have forked all five, and the requirement to reuse the existing grading model would have been impossible to honour.

**(2) ONE INFRASTRUCTURE, FIVE AUDIENCES.** The same paper, questions, responses, grading and builder serve a **formal online exam**, a **quick test attached to one Session**, a **Level** assessment, a **Teaching Group** assessment and an assessment addressed to **one individual beneficiary**. What distinguishes them is the stored `target_kind` and nothing else; there is no second builder and no second grading path. The Level remains required on every arm — it is what the paper is written for.

**(3) QUESTION TYPES, AND THE JUSTIFICATION RULE.** Four kinds and no fifth in v1: **short text**, **long text**, **single choice (UCQ)** and **multiple choice (MCQ)**. A choice question may ask for a written justification — `none`, `optional` or `required` — and **a text question may not**, because a text answer is already its own justification. Question order is **explicit and stable**, not the order rows happen to be returned in.

**(4) SAVE IS NOT SUBMIT.** A student saves an incomplete draft and returns to it; submitting is a separate, explicitly confirmed act. **Nothing autosaves and nothing autosubmits** — an assessment that submitted itself because a browser closed would be a mark nobody chose to hand in. Completeness is required only on submit; refusing to save an incomplete paper would defeat having a save.

**(5) THE FIRST STUDENT SUBMISSION FREEZES THE PAPER.** After it, no question may be added, edited, removed or reordered: the answer contract cannot silently change under an answer already given. **There is no question versioning in v1** — the freeze is the whole mechanism, deliberately, and versioning may be added later without changing what is stored. A draft in progress freezes nothing.

**(6) THERE IS NO STAFF REOPEN, RESET OR RESUBMIT ACTION IN v1.** A submitted response is final. No route offers one, and its absence is a decision rather than an omission: reopening would let answers a teacher has already read change underneath her, and if the association needs one it is a new explicit act with its own audit row.

**(7) GRADING IS MANUAL, AND IS THE EXISTING GRADE.** No automatic scoring, no MCQ auto-marking, no AI grading. R81's per-exam `max_grade` and `Grade.score` are unchanged and **no second scale is introduced**. **Publishing the assessment is not publishing the grade** (BR-8) — two separate acts, and an unpublished grade is invisible to student and parent alike.

**(8) EXCLUDED FROM v1, so a later revision adds them deliberately rather than by drift:** branching logic, file-upload answers, images in questions, rich-text editing, mathematical notation, timers, proctoring or webcam, geolocation, random question pools, random option order, negative marking, plagiarism detection and analytics dashboards.

**(9) ELIGIBILITY IS DERIVED; A SUBMISSION IS A FACT.** Nobody is copied into an assignment table (§20 rule 22). *Who may start now* resolves from live enrolment against the `AcademicPeriod` covering **the assessment's own date** (Revision 122); *who answered* is a row no later enrolment change touches. A beneficiary who leaves the Level keeps her submission and keeps it readable — **a submission is its own permission to read** — and loses only the ability to start a new one.

**(10) NEW ENTITIES** (§7): `ExamQuestion`, `ExamQuestionOption`, `StudentExamAnswer`, `StudentExamAnswerOption`. **`Exam.questions` and `StudentExamSubmission.answers` — both `jsonb` — are retired**, because a blob cannot express stable explicit ordering, an answer that *references* the option it chose, or a database that refuses to delete an answered question. Both were snapshotted into `Trash` before removal. `Exam` gains `status` (`draft` | `published` | `closed`), `target_kind` and its three new target columns; `is_published` is retired.

**(11) A STUDENT'S ANSWER IS EDUCATIONAL PERSONAL DATA and never leaves the surfaces that need it.** It is not copied into `AuditLog`, a notification or a log line (TD-14). Question text and option labels are treated the same way: *what did the paper ask* is not a question the audit trail exists to answer.

**Revision 123 (Document Owner decision — attendance is built, 2026-09-03):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**(1) ATTENDANCE IS BUILT.** §4.7's *"nothing in this section is built in the MVP"* and §20 rule 16's naming of attendance among the postponed features are both **superseded**. §4.7 below is replaced in full; rule 16 keeps its sentence *«a specification written ahead of its build is not permission to build it»*, which was right, and keeps it attached to session announcements.

**(2) WHAT ATTENDANCE MEANS IS A PROPERTY OF THE SCHEDULING TYPE**, stored as `attendance_mode`: **`disabled`** — no sheet exists at all — **`optional`**, or **`required`**. `SchedulingType.attendance_required`, a boolean, is retired: it collapsed two different questions (*may presence be recorded* and *are people expected*) into one, and a vacation and an optional activity were both *«not required»* while needing completely different behaviour.

**(3) THE CATALOGUE'S RATIFIED VALUES.** **عطلة — attendance `disabled`.** **حفل — attendance `disabled`.** **اختبار — attendance `required`.** **حصة دراسية — attendance `required`.** **محاضرة and نشاط — `optional` or `required` according to their configured scheduling type**, which a Super Admin sets on أنواع الجدولة. A type recording no catalogue row at all is refused: *we do not know whether this takes attendance* is answered by refusing, never by guessing the permissive branch.

**(4) A PRESENCE ROW MEANS ATTENDED, and there is no absence row.** Present is a live `Attendance` row; not marked is the absence of one. **No explicit absence row is created merely because somebody is unchecked** — that would record, thousands of times, that nothing happened.

**(5) REQUIRED ATTENDANCE OPENS ON THE EXPECTED ROSTER**, derived using the `AcademicPeriod` covering **the occurrence date** (Revision 122) — never the period covering today unless the occurrence is today. A historical sheet therefore shows who was expected *then*. An enrolment recording no period is unclassified history and is never expected. **`optional` attendance starts empty** and names are added as people arrive.

**(6) ENROLMENT DETERMINES *EXPECTED*, NEVER *ALLOWED*.** A registered beneficiary who is not enrolled in that particular occurrence may still attend and be marked present; the sheet reports her as beyond the roster. **No arbitrary free-text external attendee exists in this revision** — attendance is recorded for people the association has registered.

**(7) WHO MAY MARK.** For **المرأة**, an occurrence is `staff_only` **or** `self_or_staff` according to its own configuration; where self-marking is permitted a woman may record **only her own** presence, and may not mark, remove or edit anybody else's. For **اليافعات and الطفل**, attendance is **always** recorded by an authorised member of staff, **enforced server-side**: a self check-in is refused for them even if an occurrence is configured `self_or_staff`. The rule is read from `Category.self_attendance_allowed`, a column — never from a Category's name (§4.4b) and never from `schooling_stage`, which R62.7 says gates nothing.

**(8) ATTENDANCE IS INFORMATIONAL AND GATES NOTHING** — not grades, not certification, not level completion (BR-11). The Fluid Engagement Model is why: an absence in this association routinely means *watched the recording*.

**(9) A RECURRING EVENT'S OCCURRENCE DATE IS PART OF ATTENDANCE IDENTITY.** An `Event` is one row expanded over many dates, so presence on 15 June must never mean presence every week. A Session and an Exam carry their own date; an Event's is half the occurrence's identity and is stored with the record.

**(10) EXAM ATTENDANCE, EXAM SUBMISSION AND EXAM GRADE ARE THREE INDEPENDENT FACTS**, and nothing gates any of them on another. All of these are legitimate states: attendance with a submission and a grade · attendance with no submission · a submission with no attendance. A sitting is `required` for attendance because somebody sat in a room; whether she answered, and what she scored, are separate records.

**(11) EXCLUDED, and not by omission:** geolocation, biometrics, signatures, IP-address tracking, device fingerprinting, attendance photographs, attendance analytics or statistics, and QR self-check-in — the last remains §10.3's later phase and is not this.

**(12) NEW ENTITY `Attendance`** (§7), naming exactly one occurrence — a `Session`, an `Event` **with its date**, or an `Exam` — plus the beneficiary, who recorded it and when. Removing a mistaken mark is a soft delete with a `Trash` snapshot and an audit row (§20 rule 11): *she was marked and then unmarked* is a correction a register has to be able to show. **`Category.self_attendance_allowed`** and the `attendance_marking` column on `RecurringCourseSchedule` and `Event` arrive with it.

**Revision 122 (Document Owner decision — enrolment has an academic-period lifecycle, and Level IS the studies year, 2026-09-02):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**(1) AN ENROLMENT WAS «ACTIVE FOREVER», AND THAT IS THE DEFECT.** `Enrollment` carried `enrolled_at` and `deleted_at` and nothing else, so a row was current until somebody remembered to soft-delete it. *Is this beneficiary enrolled right now* had no answer the database could give, and any retention rule built on it would call somebody active a decade after they left. **`deleted_at` is REMOVAL — a correction or a withdrawal — and must never be read as the end of a semester.** Conflating the two is what produced the open-ended row.

**(2) THE ASSOCIATION ENROLS BY SEMESTER, and the old uniqueness made its ordinary case impossible.** `enrollment_student_level_unique (student_id, level_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` permitted ONE live enrolment per student per Level, so a beneficiary who takes Semester 1 of a Level and then Semester 2 **of the same Level** was refused by the database. Uniqueness becomes **`(student_id, level_id, academic_period_id)`** over live rows. **BR-21 is not weakened**: it said *one live enrolment per student per Level* when a Level had no notion of *when*, and it now says *one per student, per Level, per period* — the same rule stated over the dimension the association actually enrols in.

**(3) ACADEMIC YEAR AND STUDIES YEAR ARE DIFFERENT CONCEPTS, and nothing may assume they correspond.** One academic year may contain enrolments at **several** studies years for one beneficiary; Semester 1 and Semester 2 of one academic year need not belong to the same studies year; and a beneficiary may enrol in Semester 2 with no Semester 1 row for that year. All four are legitimate and are asserted directly.

**(4) `Level` IS the studies year — no entity is added for it.** Each Category already holds an ordered progression (المرأة seven Levels, الطفل seven, اليافعات six) ranked by `display_order`; «first studies year» is the Level at rank 1 of that Category. A `StudiesYear` model would duplicate a concept §7 has carried since R43, so this revision adds only what was genuinely missing: **when**.

**(5) NEW ENTITY `AcademicPeriod`** (§7) — `(academic_year_id, sequence, start_date, end_date, version)`, unique on `(academic_year_id, sequence)`. **One set per academic year**: a period carries no Category and no Branch, because the association's semesters are the same across all three Categories; varying them later adds a column rather than restructuring the table. `end_date` is **inclusive**. **The dates are the association's own and are never computed** — nothing assumes a September start or a January boundary. **Two periods of one year may not overlap**, enforced in the service with a coded refusal because PostgreSQL can express it only through an exclusion constraint requiring `btree_gist`, which this installation does not load; overlap matters because *which semester is it* must have exactly one answer.

**(6) `AcademicYear` GAINS NO DATES.** They would be a second, driftable copy of `min(start_date)`/`max(end_date)` across its periods.

**(7) NO ENROLMENT STATUS COLUMN.** *Current* is derived from the period's dates and `deleted_at`, and nothing else. A status column would be a second answer to a question the dates already settle, and it would go stale the day a semester ends with nobody logged in to update it. **Withdrawal is not given its own state** in this revision: soft delete already means *removed*, and inventing a third state without a stated requirement would be over-engineering.

**(8) `Enrollment.academic_period_id` is REQUIRED AT THE WRITE BOUNDARY AND NULLABLE IN THE COLUMN**, and the two are deliberately different. Every new enrolment names a period — through the form, through `POST /admin/enrollments`, through the roster route, and at approval, where it is the period covering **today**. Rows written before this revision name none, and **no period was guessed for them**: nothing in an old row says which semester it belonged to, `enrolled_at` is the moment of data entry rather than a period, and inventing one would fabricate a fact about somebody's education. `NULL` reads as *period not recorded*, honestly. **An approval with no period covering today FAILS CLOSED** (`NO_CURRENT_ACADEMIC_PERIOD`) rather than writing an open-ended row.

**(9) HISTORY IS NEVER OVERWRITTEN, because a future attestation depends on it.** The association must be able to answer «كنت أدرس عندكم وأريد شهادة تثبت المستوى الذي وصلت إليه» years later, so each enrolment is its own row naming its own academic year, semester and Level. **A beneficiary's record is appended to, never updated in place**, and the progression is reconstructible by joining enrolment → period → year and ordering by the Level's `display_order`. **No certificate is generated by this revision.**

**(10) TD-3 gains three endpoints** — `GET /admin/academic-periods` (reference-data read, TD-2 R26/R30) and `POST` / `PATCH /admin/academic-periods{/id}` (**Super Admin only**, TD-15 on the patch, the academic year not editable). **TD-8 gains `academicperiod.create` and `academicperiod.update`.**

**(11) §15.1 SEEDS NO PERIODS.** Inventing semester boundaries would fabricate a fact about the association's calendar; the Super Admin records them once per year.

**(12) This revision implements NO retention.** It makes a future retention rule *possible* — «does she hold an enrolment whose period is current?» is now answerable — and deliberately adds no `last_activity_at`, no job and no de-identification trigger.

**(13) Deliberately unchanged:** R66's optional Administrative Group and the `(administrative_group_id, branch_id)` composite FK; R27's sex eligibility; the Level's immutability on an enrolment edit; TD-5 soft delete; and every rule `enrolAtPlacement` already applied, which both approval paths and both write routes still share.

**Revision 121 (Document Owner decisions — generic free text is withdrawn, the Google scope is minimised, and the Category names are the association's own, 2026-09-02):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**Part A — `User.notes` is removed.** **(1) A field with no stated purpose is not a field the platform may keep.** `notes` was 2 000 characters of unbounded free text rendered as «ملاحظات (اختياري)» on the **public registration form**, so an applicant could volunteer a health condition, a custody arrangement or a judicial matter into a platform that deliberately collects none of them; it was also staff-editable and shown to approvers. **No requirement anywhere states what it is for or who must read it.** **(2) This is R62.1's reasoning applied to everybody.** That clause already excluded `notes` from the child shape in terms — *"free text about a child, with no stated purpose and no reader, is where a diagnosis or a custody arrangement gets written in good faith"* — and the Owner now extends it to every person. **(3) Hiding the control would not withdraw the capability**: the column would still accept 2 000 characters through the API, and an empty column is still a declared purpose. The field is therefore removed from the schema, both validators, every service, DTO and projection, the registration form, the Super Admin edit dialog and the approvals display. **(4) The drop is guarded**: the migration refuses to run while any row holds a non-blank value and reports how many, because a dropped column cannot be recovered and this one was the only place its content ever lived. Blank-but-not-null counts as no value, deliberately — refusing on whitespace would block a deployment over an artefact rather than over somebody's words. Verified read-only first: **Localhost 73 users / 0 values, Staging 14 users / 0 values**; Production is not deployed. **(5) THE PRINCIPLE, stated so this is a rule rather than one deletion: the platform does not provide generic free-text collection fields without a defined operational purpose. A personal-data field must have a specific, documented purpose.** **(6) Deliberately untouched:** the bounded free-text fields that DO have one — `ChildApplication.internal_note` (R62.8), `FamilyLink.decision_reason`, `Session.cancellation_reason` — and the `notes` key on the §5.2 Session page projection, which is a different field on a different entity and is unrelated.

**Part B — the Google OAuth scope is minimised to `openid email`.** **(1) `profile` was requested and never read.** The authorization request asked for `openid email profile`; `verifyGoogleIdToken` extracts **`sub` and `email` only** and discards the rest, so the platform was asking Google for a person's name, picture and locale in order to throw them away. **(2) The requested scope is now `openid email`** — `openid` yields the `sub` an account is bound to, `email` yields the address `UserIdentity` stores and the `email_verified` claim §4.1b step 7 hard-stops on. Those two are the entire identity contract. **(3) Nothing else changes**: PKCE, `state`, the sealed flow cookie, token validation, `email_verified`, the Google subject binding, the account-chooser prompt, and the rule that **no Google token is ever persisted and no Google API is ever called after login** are all unchanged. **(4) The public privacy page is corrected in the same revision** — it told applicants the platform asks Google for their name and picture, which it no longer does. A policy naming a scope the application does not request is as wrong as one omitting a scope it does.

**Part C — the Category names are the association's own, and Revision 27 is clarified.** **(1) THE CURRENT CATEGORY NAMES ARE `المرأة`, `اليافعات` and `الطفل`** (Document Owner, 2026-09-02). They are the association's own product vocabulary and are **not** to be renamed. **(2) Revision 27 is NOT reversed, and its substance stands.** Its purpose was to make the sex restriction *machine-readable*: before it, *«teens are girls only»* was expressed **only by the feminine plural in the category name**, where no query could read it. R27 moved that fact into `Level.gender_restriction`, paired it with the new `User.sex`, and **that half is correct, load-bearing and untouched.** **(3) What is clarified is the rename that travelled with it.** The migration's sex-neutral names were a *consequence* of that normalisation rather than a product decision, and this document then recorded them as the seeded reality in several places. The Owner's names are authoritative; the prohibition to carry forward is on **encoding a restriction in a name where logic must read it**, which `gender_restriction` now holds instead. **(4) No migration and no data change is created.** Both installations already hold the Owner's names with no duplicates, and a fresh install seeds them correctly, because the R27 rename is guarded by the legacy name and finds nothing on an empty table. **(5) Two live defects caused by the stale assumption are corrected**: the library's category ordering matched the sex-neutral names, so **not one of the three matched a real row** and the adult→teen→child progression it exists to impose was silently absent; and the public landing page named two stages the association does not use. **(6) A known upgrade-path hazard is recorded rather than fixed here**: on an installation that carried the legacy rows *before* 2026-07-28, the R27 migration renames them and the §15.1 seed — which matches by name — would then create the Owner's names as **new** rows. No such installation is known to exist; if one appears, it is a data reconciliation and an Owner decision, not a silent seed change. **(7) No legal conclusion is drawn here about these names or about `User.sex`; both remain open compliance questions.**

**Revision 120 (Document Owner decision — the StudentSocialProfile capability is withdrawn, and what a lesson recording actually is, 2026-09-02):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**Part A — `StudentSocialProfile` is removed from the platform.** **(1) THE ASSOCIATION DOES NOT COLLECT THIS DATA, AND THE PLATFORM MUST NOT BE ABLE TO.** `StudentSocialProfile` held **health condition**, family situation, home address, siblings count and both parents' names and professions — about **children** — and **no product surface ever collected any of it**: not registration, not «تسجيل طفل», not the beneficiary profile, and no parent, مؤطِّرة or administrative screen. The frontend contained no reference to it whatever. **(2) An unused capability is still a capability**, and that is the whole reasoning: an endpoint nobody calls is a live ability to collect health data about a minor, an empty column is a declared purpose, and neither is withdrawn by not being used. **The feature is therefore removed, not disabled** — model, table, routes, service, controller, authorization path, audit actions and tests. **(3) §4.10's sensitive-case-file clause and BR-16 are WITHDRAWN**, and **Revision 28 is superseded in its entirety**: it settled the permissions and the audit for a feature that no longer exists. **(4) TD-2 loses both rows** — *View StudentSocialProfile* and *Create/edit StudentSocialProfile*. **(5) TD-3 loses `GET` and `PUT /students/{id}/social-profile`.** **(6) TD-8 loses `socialprofile.view` and `socialprofile.update`.** **(7) TD-12's high-risk freshness list loses its `StudentSocialProfile` reads entry**; every other entry stands, and the per-request freshness mechanism is unchanged. **(8) The drop is guarded rather than trusted.** The migration refuses to run against a non-empty table and reports the row count, because this DROP is irreversible and the table was the only place its data ever lived; a human must decide the fate of any row before the capability is withdrawn. Verified read-only before the migration was written: **Localhost 0 rows, Staging 0 rows**; Production is not deployed. **(9) THE POLICY, stated so a future reader knows this was a decision and not an omission: the platform does not collect categories of personal data that are not necessary for the association's current operational purposes.** Re-introducing a case file is a new Owner decision and a new revision, never a restoration. **(10) Deliberately unchanged:** every other §4.10 clause (soft delete, Trash, AuditLog, the CSV postponement, two-step self-deletion, the `AcademicYear` constraint, the no-plaintext-credentials rule); §4.1a consent in every particular; the R111 de-identification model, which simply has one satellite fewer.

**Part B — what a lesson recording IS, corrected as a matter of fact.** **(1) The platform does not record a classroom, and it does not record beneficiaries.** **(2) MVP: a مؤطِّرة may VOLUNTARILY record HER OWN VOICE** giving or explaining a lesson, and publish it as educational content. **(3) Post-MVP: she may voluntarily record HERSELF on video** doing the same. **(4) Teacher-authored lesson material must not be described as classroom capture**, as surveillance, or as inherently containing a student's voice or image — it is a teacher speaking, authored by her, on purpose. **(5) NO TECHNICAL SAFEGUARD PREVENTS A MICROPHONE FROM CAPTURING SOMEBODY ELSE, and this document does not claim one.** What exists is §4.1a/BR-2's consent gate over *publication*, which is a different mechanism and is unchanged. **(6) Photographs and video from association events and parties are a SEPARATE scenario.** Such media is intended for publication only where the required consent has been obtained from identifiable people in it, or from a parent or legal representative where applicable. **No implementation is created or changed by this clause**; it records the intent against which a future surface will be designed. **(7) Internal naming is not churned.** `SessionRecording` and the `session-recording` queue keep their names: this revision corrects the **product meaning and the documentation**, not identifiers whose renaming would buy nothing.

**Revision 119 (Document Owner decisions — the calendar filters by the scheduling-type catalogue, and the legal consent wording becomes versioned evidence, 2026-09-02):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation, at the Owner's instruction.)*

**Part A — the scheduling-type catalogue is what the calendar filters by.** **(1) `أنواع الجدولة → إضافة نوع → يُنفَّذ باعتباره` offers all four structural kinds.** R110(9) made `holiday` a kind of its own and it reached the enum, the migration, the seed, the write boundary and the label map; the create form's option list alone still offered three, so a Super Admin could see a عطلة row and had no way to create another. **(2) `الجدول الزمني → النوع` filters by SchedulingType, not by the occurrence vocabulary.** `session | event | exam` is the *storage* taxonomy and cannot express the association's own words: three catalogue rows are stored as an `Event`, so `type=event` returned نشاط, حفل and عطلة together and **a holiday could not be filtered for at all**, while «حصة دراسية» and «محاضرة» are both `class`. The filter offers **the live catalogue, read from `GET /calendar/bootstrap`** — never a hard-coded list, because the catalogue is the administration's to change (§4.4b, R110). **(3) `RecurringCourseSchedule` and `Exam` gain `scheduling_type_id`**, nullable, `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, validated against the row's structural kind exactly as `Event.scheduling_type_id` is. Only `Event` recorded a catalogue row before, which is why two class types could not be told apart and a class type had nothing to narrow by. **`structural_kind` is NOT superseded and is not conflated with the type**: it remains the answer to *which entity delivers this*, and the type is the separate answer to *which catalogue row is it*. **(4) `GET /calendar` and `GET /me/calendar` gain `scheduling_type_id`.** The server resolves the named type's `structural_kind` first — which decides *which of the three sources can hold such a row at all* — and then narrows within that source by id; that ordering is what lets two types of one kind be filtered apart. An id naming no type returns an empty set rather than being ignored. **`type` remains accepted**, so a link saved before this revision still narrows the same grid; both may be sent and each narrows independently. **(5) Every occurrence carries `scheduling_type_id`, `scheduling_type_name` and `structural_kind`**, so a client marks a عطلة **from the row** rather than by matching an Arabic name the administration is free to rename. **(6) `GET /calendar/bootstrap` gains `scheduling_types`** — the live catalogue with its kinds and order. Public for the reason `subjects` is (R84): every occurrence the anonymous calendar returns already carries the type's name. **(7) LEGACY ROWS ARE NOT GUESSED.** Every schedule and sitting created before this revision records no type, **none was backfilled**, and such a row **matches no type filter** while remaining visible unfiltered. Inferring one from a name, or falling back to `structural_kind`, would make one untyped class answer to «حصة دراسية» *and* to «محاضرة» — two contradictory claims from a row that records neither, and the name-matching §4.4b forbids arrived at from the other direction. The column is editable so that somebody who *knows* what a row was may say so.

**Part B — the legal consent wording becomes versioned, immutable, bound evidence.** **The defect.** The Arabic wording lived in the frontend's i18n catalogue, the version lived in the `legal.consent_text_version` `SystemSetting`, and a `ConsentRecord` stored **only that string**. Nothing bound the three together: the wording could change with no version change and the version with no wording change, in either direction and silently, and the platform could not answer *«what exactly did this person agree to»* from its own data. The relationship was human discipline. **(1) `LegalConsentText` is the authority**: a stable id, a unique human-readable `version_label`, the **exact Arabic wording**, a SHA-256 digest of it, `draft | active | superseded`, and creation and activation provenance with timestamps. **(2) Immutable once in force.** A version that has ever been activated cannot have its wording or its label edited — new wording is a NEW version, refused with `CONSENT_TEXT_IMMUTABLE`. Only a never-activated draft is editable. **(3) Exactly one active version, enforced by the DATABASE** — a partial unique index over `status = 'active'`. Two concurrent activations cannot both win, and no application-level check is trusted for it. **(4) Nothing is destroyed.** There is no delete; superseding is a status change, and every old wording stays readable forever because it is the evidence. **(5) `ConsentRecord.consent_text_id` is the technical binding** the string never was, `ON DELETE RESTRICT`; `consent_text_version` is **retained beside it**, because it is what an export, an audit row and a compliance reader use. `ChildApplication` carries the same pair, so a child's consent is written against **what the parent read**, not against whatever is active when an administrator gets to the application. **(6) LEGACY EVIDENCE IS PRESERVED HONESTLY AND NOT FABRICATED.** Records written before this revision keep their version string and carry `consent_text_id = NULL`. **No `LegalConsentText` is manufactured for a historical string**: the wording those people saw was never stored, and attaching them to a row containing today's text would assert that they read those words, which nobody can prove. NULL means *the wording is not resolvable*, and no existing evidence is altered, restamped or discarded. **(7) The registration form displays the exact stored wording it is about to record**, read from `GET /registration/consent-text` (anonymous — the notice is reached before any account exists), and **submits that version's id**. The server refuses a mismatch with `CONSENT_TEXT_SUPERSEDED`: a Super Admin activating new wording while a form is open must never turn *displayed X* into *recorded Y*, and a wording taken out of force may have been withdrawn because it was wrong. **(8) FAIL-CLOSED IS UNCHANGED.** With no active version, registration and staff-recorded consent answer `503` with `CONSENT_TEXT_VERSION_NOT_CONFIGURED`, exactly as before; §2.3 keeps approving the wording an Owner compliance task, and **no wording is seeded in production**. **(9) `legal.consent_text_version` is retired as an authority.** It is removed from the writable-settings allow-list, nothing reads or writes it, and there are not two editable answers to *which wording is in force*. **The stored row is not deleted** — it is the only record of which label was last in force before the cutover — and, being unreadable and unwritable, it cannot drift. **(10) TD-3 gains five endpoints**: `GET`/`POST /admin/legal-consent-texts`, `PATCH /admin/legal-consent-texts/{id}`, `POST /admin/legal-consent-texts/{id}/activate` — **Super Admin only**, asserted against live rows (TD-12) — and the anonymous `GET /registration/consent-text`. **(11) TD-8 gains `legalconsenttext.create`, `.update` and `.activate`**, carrying the digest and, for an activation, the id of the version replaced. The **digest, never the body**: audit rows are purged on their own schedule and the wording must not be duplicated into a table whose retention is not the evidence's. **Ids, never labels** — TD-8's minimisation rule refuses a copied label, and it is right to, because `LegalConsentText` rows are permanent and the label is one join away. **(12) `إعدادات المنصة` manages versions, not a string** — the active version and its exact wording, when it took effect, the history read-only, and a create → write → label → review → activate flow with activation as an explicit separate act. No hash or UUID is presented as something an administrator manages. **(13) Deliberately unchanged**: `consent_forced_private` and every other consent semantic; §4.1a's requirement that each record carry the exact text agreed to, which this makes true rather than promised.

**Revision 118 (Document Owner decisions — lifecycle closure: schedule purge, retained tombstones, and terminal rejected family links, 2026-09-02):** *(Recorded in §0 with the implementation; these answer the three questions `docs/development/audit-2026-09-01-trash-lifecycle.md` left open.)* **(1) A deleted `RecurringCourseSchedule` that has NEVER materialized a Session may be permanently purged; one that has must not be.** The previous contract was type-wide and refused every schedule with `CASCADE_CHILDREN`. It is now a fact about the **row**: a schedule nobody ever taught is a plan, and there is no history in it to protect, while **one Session — live or tombstoned, protected or not — makes the schedule permanent**, because a materialized coordinate is the institutional record R59 keeps. The refusal is coded `MATERIALIZED_HISTORY` and is asserted **twice**: once when the Trash is listed, so no interface offers an action that cannot succeed, and again inside the purge transaction, where it is authoritative because a materialization job may have run between the two. The schedule's `CourseScheduleStaff` rows are owned by it (R91 makes an assignment a row ON the schedule) and are destroyed with it; **Sessions are never part of the plan**, since their existence is what forbids it. **This supersedes nothing about R59**: no Session, no audit row and no consent evidence is destroyed, and ordered purge — child before parent — remains how a blocked reference is released. **(2) Records kept because history references them are presented as RETAINED, not as ordinary Trash.** A row that can be neither restored nor purged is not awaiting a decision; it is being kept. The Trash read gains a `view` of **`actionable`** (the default — rows a restore or a purge exists for), `retained` and `all`. **No row is moved, hidden, altered or deleted**: referential and historical integrity are untouched and every record stays reachable. This is a lens over the existing Trash rather than a new archive subsystem, and the default stops the surface offering two buttons that can never work. **(3) A terminal `rejected` FamilyLink may be permanently removed, and the audit trail is the evidence.** Such a link grants no authority over any child and is not Trash — it records a request that was refused and then had no transition to leave by, which is why it accumulated. `DELETE /admin/family-links/{id}/rejected` (TD-3) removes the operational row under the same fresh-role check and the same roles as revoke (TD-12), writing **`familylink.purge_rejected`** (TD-8) **before** the delete and in the same transaction, carrying both parties, who rejected it and when. `AuditLog.target_id` is deliberately not a foreign key, so *who asked, who refused, and who removed the record* is reconstructable from the trail alone (§7's attribution invariant) — and that is precisely what makes removing the operational row acceptable rather than a loss. **`pending` and `approved` are refused by name** (`NOT_TERMINAL_REJECTED`): a pending link still owes somebody an answer, and an approved one is live authority that is **revoked** — soft, with a Trash snapshot — never removed. **(4) Deliberately unchanged.** BR-15's 90-day window and R111's 3-day account window; every restore rule; TD-5 soft delete; the R59.4 quarantine-destruction question, which remains open and is not authorised by anything here; and the `User` and content lifecycles in every particular.

**Revision 117 (Document Owner decision — registration review and guardian semantics corrected after controlled UAT, 2026-09-01):** **(1) A `registration_review_required` notice is actionable only through the existing authorized queue.** Its exact `subject_user_id` links to `/admin/approvals?review_user_id=<user>`. The server returns at most that still-pending review item; a missing, already-decided or deleted coordinate renders the queue's ordinary unavailable state and never a blank screen or a broader fallback list, while a malformed UUID is rejected by the ordinary request validator. No other R116 notice acquires an invented route. **(2) The queue row remains compact and gains an authorized `عرض التفاصيل` view.** Before deciding, an Admin or Super Admin can read the submitted applicant/guardian Arabic and optional French names, nickname, sex, phone, verified email, notes and current data-processing-consent provenance, plus a separate block for every child containing the submitted names, nickname, sex, schooling stage, that child's requested Category and Branch, and that child's data-processing/media decisions with their text version and submission instant. This representation is staff-only and is an explicit DTO, never an ORM row. **(3) A parent/child submission is a guardian request, not self-enrolment.** Approving the parent's own Pending account activates it but does not mark it beneficiary, grant Student, or create any Enrollment. The Parent role and child access arise through an approved child decision as R62 requires. A parent who separately asks to study follows the ordinary adult-beneficiary path; family registration never infers that second intent. **(4) Every child retains her own requested Category and Branch.** The review and placement dialog read the exact `ChildApplication` row. Siblings may request different Categories and Branches; each child's offered/default Level comes only from her requested Category. The compact family row may show a common Category or Branch only when every child states the same coordinate; mixed or legacy-missing requests render *not stated* there while the detail blocks retain every exact value. Names, including optional French parts and nickname, are persisted on the application so review does not reconstruct or lose submitted data. **(5) New public registrations require `phone` at both client and server boundaries.** The existing `User.phone` column remains nullable: historical, system and already-provisioned accounts receive no fabricated backfill and continue to authenticate. Authorized reviewers see the submitted phone; audit payloads still exclude it. **(6) The family option is labelled as registering children in the applicant's capacity as `وليّة أمر`; it must not imply that the applicant is also registering herself as a beneficiary.** **(7) Parent's role home is the supported `/dashboard/student`, never the removed `/dashboard/parent`.** The active-child coordinate is meaningful only in Parent context. Switching to any non-Parent role clears it; a genuine Parent+Student acting as Student resolves herself, never the last child. A Parent-only account exposes Parent plus linked-child contexts and no standalone Student context unless a live Student role genuinely exists. **(8) Transactional guarantees are unchanged and explicit.** Each child decision atomically creates/links the child, grants the structural Student role and beneficiary fact for a newly admitted child, creates placement and FamilyLink, materializes the submitted consents, grants Parent where required, and writes the useful notification/audit. A failure rolls back the whole child decision; siblings remain independently approvable/rejectable. **(9) Deliberately unchanged:** R62's per-child decisions and multi-parent safeguards; R67's per-child request model; TD-2 authorization; Google-only identity binding; legacy nullable contact data; R116 delivery/idempotency/privacy; and every B-01/B-02/B-03 invariant.

**Revision 116 (Document Owner decision — the bounded in-app inbox carries actionable account, relationship, staffing and exam changes, 2026-09-01):** **(1) Revision 77's minimum `Notification` entity is EXPANDED, while §10.1's notification framework remains postponed.** This revision adds useful delivered facts to the existing caller-owned inbox; it creates no tier, preference, delivery channel, parent-forwarding rule, background job or parallel notification model. The actor never supplies recipient ids: every audience is resolved inside the domain transaction from authoritative rows, inactive/deleted recipients are removed under locked User rows, and the actor is excluded. **(2) `Notification` gains a fourth real target, `subject_user_id`, for account and relationship facts.** Exactly one of `session_id`, `event_id`, `exam_id` and `subject_user_id` is non-null, enforced by one database CHECK. `subject_user_id` is the affected person and is distinct from `user_id`, which is always the recipient; it is a foreign key, never an unconstrained payload identifier. Existing rows must satisfy the former three-target invariant before the migration widens it. **(3) The account/relationship catalogue is exact.** `registration_review_required` is written at a complete adult or parent/child submission for the live Admin/Super-Admin approval population; `registration_approved` goes to the adult applicant after activation, or to the active parent after a child approval with that child as the exact target; `registration_rejected` goes to an active parent whose child application was rejected. An adult applicant who becomes `Rejected` receives the existing deactivated-status screen instead, because TD-1 deliberately gives that account no authenticated inbox and a row it cannot read would falsely claim delivery. `family_link_requested`, `family_link_approved`, `family_link_rejected` and `family_link_revoked` go to the parent and target the child; `role_assignments_changed` goes to the affected User only after the assignment set actually changes; `platform_ownership_received` goes to the new Owner after the singleton transfer; and `enrollment_changed` goes to the affected student after a create, move, material placement change or removal. These rows join the same transaction as the state they announce. **(4) Staffing changes are responsibilities, not optional audience announcements.** `session_assigned`/`session_unassigned` and `event_staff_assigned`/`event_staff_unassigned` are automatic on a real addition/removal; a same-set save is silent, removal withdraws the stale assignment statement, and re-addition withdraws the stale unassignment and resurfaces the assignment. The existing optional `POST /sessions/{id}/notify` and `POST /events/{id}/notify` remain separate decisions for occurrence/activity announcements and are not reinterpreted as staffing delivery. **(5) Physical Exam lifecycle facts use the existing `exam_id` target.** Creation writes `exam_teacher_assigned` to each eligible assigned staff member and `exam_scheduled` to the sitting's authoritative grade-sheet audience (the named Administrative Group, or the whole Level at the Exam branch). A dual-role person receives BOTH types because assignment and attendance are different facts. A real staff removal/re-addition produces `exam_teacher_unassigned`/`exam_teacher_assigned`; a date or clock change produces `exam_rescheduled`; a material non-time detail change produces `exam_changed`; an audience transition schedules newly included students and cancels the stale statement for students who left scope; deletion leaves each still-authorized current recipient with only `exam_cancelled`. Draft grade saves remain silent and the existing `grade_published` still fires only on Publish. **(6) Hidden is a disclosure boundary for the inbox as well as the calendar.** A hidden Session is notification-readable only by its responsible `teacher`, a hidden Event only by `EventStaff.responsible`, and a hidden Exam only by its `supervisor`; students and assistants receive no target-bearing row. Changing a previously visible target to hidden deletes already-delivered rows for people who lose access rather than replacing them with a cancellation/unassignment that would still reveal the hidden title/date. A later optional send resolves the current hidden audience. Public/private targets keep their normal authoritative audience rules. **(7) Delivery and reconciliation are serialized with the domain write.** Automatic rows are written in the same PostgreSQL transaction as the account, relationship, staffing or Exam change, after one globally sorted User-lock acquisition for the complete recipient set where sets overlap. No committed change may lose its automatic notification obligation, and no row may announce a rolled-back change. The established R82/R83 optional-send exception remains deliberate: the saved change commits first and the actor separately chooses whether to announce it. **(8) Idempotency is semantic, not merely absence of duplicate ids.** The unique coordinate is `(recipient, exact target, type)`. Retrying or saving an unchanged state creates nothing and does not make a read row unread. A genuine repeat transition (remove/re-add, a later role/enrollment change, or another material edit of the same kind) reuses the one semantic row, makes it unread and moves it to the top; stale opposite facts are withdrawn. Distinct meanings for a dual-role recipient coexist because their `type` values differ. **(9) Educational-content completion deliberately emits NO notification.** The current upload completion is storage finalization, not a deliberate publish transition; notifying there could surface a stale, replaced or consent-restricted coordinate. A future content-publication lifecycle requires its own Owner decision and must not be inferred from upload success. **(10) Deliberately unchanged:** caller-only notification reads and `404` for another user's row; R77 restore reconciliation; R82/R83 optional announcement semantics; R92 occurrence audiences; R93 Event assignment meaning; R109 visibility; the B-01/B-02/B-03 consent/storage invariants; §10.1's tiers/preferences/channels/parent forwarding; and TD-7's job catalogue.

**Revision 115 (Document Owner decision — Platform Owner lifecycle, framing willingness and controlled-UAT Staging, 2026-08-31):** **(1) Platform Owner is one protected lifecycle relationship, not a sixth RBAC role and not a renamed Super Admin.** Exactly one `PlatformOwner(singleton_key = 'platform')` row exists after bootstrap and points to an active, undeleted User holding one live global (`branch_id IS NULL`) `super_admin` assignment. The current owner cannot be suspended, deleted, permanently de-identified or stripped of that assignment; ownership must first be transferred. Transfer is current-owner-only, requires an exact high-impact confirmation, accepts only another already-eligible Global Super Admin, updates the singleton atomically, and leaves the former owner's role intact. The singleton is locked before both User rows, which are locked in deterministic id order; concurrent transfers therefore converge on exactly one successor. Database constraints/triggers backstop application checks, the relationship cannot be deleted, and no committed transfer state contains zero or multiple owners. **(2) The initial identity is fixed by this Owner decision:** `safae.elmessoussi@gmail.com`, صفاء المسوسي, female, active Global Super Admin and Platform Owner, with general framing willingness `both` at all current and future branches and no fabricated weekly availability. The seed pre-provisions the verified-email claim; it creates no `UserIdentity` and never invents a Google provider subject. First verified Google login binds the real subject through the ordinary §4.1b transaction and bypasses registration. **(3) Revisions 22–23 are superseded only as to their bootstrap gate and recovery behaviour.** The gate is now absence of the Platform Owner singleton, not absence of an active Super Admin. Before that row exists the seed requires the exact approved email and female sex, serializes concurrent bootstrap, and fails before partial owner creation on conflict. Once the row exists every rerun is an ownership no-op regardless of environment values, later transfer, or the population of other Super Admins: seed never reclaims the original owner, creates an automatic successor or reopens merely because administrators disappear. Recovery after a legitimate transfer is an explicit ownership operation, never a seed side effect. **(4) A public adult request to join هيئة التأطير records planning willingness, not authority.** It requires one general mode (`in_person | online | both`). `online` carries no physical branch. A mode containing in-person work carries either one/multiple explicit live branch ids or `all_branches = true`; the latter means every current **and future** physical branch and is never expanded into today's ids or stored as a fake branch. The preference is shown to the approver and thereafter remains visible read-only on the teaching profile; neither view may derive a role or branch scope from it, and the approver states authority separately. **(5) Each `TeacherAvailability` interval may independently carry the same mode, nullable for honest legacy/not-stated rows.** A scheduling appraisal may warn on explicit incompatibility or unknown mode, but this is advisory only: modality, preference and declared availability never grant or revoke teaching authority, which remains assignment-derived. General `both` willingness does not imply weekly hours. **(6) Staging becomes controlled UAT for exactly the initial Owner identity above.** That one real staff identity may exist there solely for Global Super Admin, Platform Owner and OAuth acceptance. All beneficiary, educational, content and other staff data remain fixtures/synthetic unless separately authorised; no Production dump may enter Staging, and Production residency, infrastructure, DNS and deployment rules are unchanged. **(7) Deliberately unchanged:** the RBAC matrix apart from the owner-only transfer capability; Google-only authentication and exact identity binding; R111 historical-record retention; branch authority semantics; R88/R90 planning-only rules; every Production data-residency requirement; and the prohibition on weakening Staging security controls.

**Revision 114 (Document Owner reconciliation — teacher-managed capabilities, and an Umm al-Qura baseline for the Hijri calendar, 2026-08-30):** **This revision records behaviour the Document Owner had already approved and that is already implemented (commit `fca3b2e`). It is a documentation reconciliation: no permission is broadened here that was not already granted, and no implementation changes because of it.** **(1) R88.2's remaining reservation is SUPERSEDED: a مؤطِّرة now self-manages her declared Subjects and Categories as well as her availability.** R88.2 reserved the whole teaching profile to the administration; R106 took only the availability half and explicitly left *what she may teach* as *"the administration's record of her"*. The Owner now takes the other half. She writes `TeacherSubjectCapability` and `TeacherCategoryCapability` for **herself**, through `PUT /me/teaching-profile/capabilities`. **The administration's `GET`/`PUT /admin/users/{id}/teaching-profile` are unchanged** and still refuse her; an administrator may still record and correct anybody's profile. **(2) THESE CAPABILITIES ARE PROFILE/PLANNING METADATA AND GRANT NOTHING — this is the condition on which the grant is made, and it is normative.** A declaration means *the administration may consider me for this*; it is **never** authorization. It grants **no** scheduling authority, **no** assignment authority, **no** administrative reach, **no** Quran-progress authority, and no operational authority of any other kind. R88.3 and §4.4c are unchanged in every particular: **teaching authority is an ASSIGNMENT** — `CourseScheduleStaff` / `SessionStaff`, resolved through the effective-assignment rules R91 states — and neither self-service route writes either table. A مؤطِّرة who declares every Subject on the platform therefore reaches no beneficiary, no class, no roster, no grade sheet and no memorisation record, and holds exactly the authority her assignments give her and no more. R90's appraisal continues to read these declarations as **warnings an administrator may override** (R88.4), never as a filter or a block. TD-2's `⊘` on a Teacher creating or editing a Recurring Course Schedule stands **completely unchanged**, as R71.0, R72.1, R94.2 and R106 each require. **(3) Three properties hold the grant where it is, and each is normative.** **(a) The route carries no `{id}`** — the subject is the caller's own token, so there is nowhere in the request to name another person; this is construction rather than a check that could be forgotten, the same shape `/students/me/quran` uses. **(b) Two self-service routes, each replacing only the half its path names**, both `.strict()` against the other's field: `PUT /me/teaching-profile/capabilities` **refuses** a body carrying `availability`, exactly as R106 made `PUT /me/teaching-profile/availability` refuse `subject_ids`. A silently ignored field beside a response echoing the profile would look precisely like a successful rewrite of the half that was not sent, and a client holding a stale copy of one half must not be able to erase it. **(c) The audit row (TD-8 `settings.change`) carries `self_service: true`**, for R88.2's own second question — whether the administration may *rely* on a declaration is answerable later only if the trail distinguishes one she asserted from one an administrator recorded for her. **(4) A retired Subject or Category is REFUSED, not silently dropped**, from the same validation the administrative writer uses (`UNKNOWN_SUBJECT` / `UNKNOWN_CATEGORY`), because a profile that quietly lost a declaration would leave the administration planning against something it cannot see. **(5) `GET /me/teaching-profile` additionally returns the Subjects and Categories she may choose from.** She holds no reference-data permission — Revision 30 makes reading reference data an administrative concern and Teacher `⊘` — and that stands: the selectable list travels on the read she already makes rather than by widening `/admin/subjects` to her. **Widening a permission to make a screen work is prohibited**, and this clause exists so that the alternative is not attempted later. The projection is names and identifiers of live Subjects and Categories only; it discloses no person and no enrolment. **(6) TD-3.4 REGAINS AN IMPORT ROUTE, AND REVISION 32(2) IS SUPERSEDED ONLY AS TO THIS NARROW CASE.** R32 removed `POST /admin/hijri-calendar/import` because **the Ministry of Habous publishes no machine-readable calendar**, so an *official* importer could only ever answer *not configured*. **That reasoning is untouched and remains correct: no official Moroccan importer exists or is specified here.** What is added is a different thing under a different name — **`POST /admin/hijri-calendar/{year}/import`, an Umm al-Qura BASELINE**, whose only purpose is to spare the Super Admin typing twelve dates a year from nothing. **(7) Revision 31's architecture is untouched: `HijriMonthStart` remains the sole source of every Hijri value the platform displays.** Umm al-Qura is **not** the official calendar, is **never** consulted at runtime by any read path, and never becomes authority. Morocco fixes each month by **local moon sighting** and Umm al-Qura is **calculated**, so the two differ with some regularity — 1 Muharram 1448 falls on 17 June 2026 officially where Umm al-Qura gives 16 June. **(8) The import fills ONLY missing months and NEVER updates an existing one.** A month that already has a row is skipped — whatever that row says and whoever wrote it. This is deliberately stated as *never update* rather than as *do not overwrite a correction*: any test of whether a human has touched a row is a test that can be got wrong, and getting it wrong would silently replace an official Moroccan date with a computed one, a failure invisible until a month is kept on the wrong day. Re-running the import is therefore idempotent by construction. **The Super Admin remains authoritative**: she corrects any imported value through `PUT /admin/hijri-calendar/{year}/{month}` under TD-15, and no later import can undo her correction. **(9) Imported values arrive as `draft`, and provenance is retained per row.** `HijriMonthStart.source` — the column §7 already defines for exactly this — records `umm_al_qura_icu` for a derived value; a value a Super Admin records or corrects reads `manual` and **keeps** it. Because only `published` months render anywhere, nothing derived is displayed to anybody until the Super Admin has reviewed the year and published it. A derived month whose date would violate the ordering invariant against an already-stored neighbour is **skipped** rather than forced, and the remaining months still import. **(10) Revision 32(1)'s vocabulary rule is unchanged and governs this surface too.** The action is a **baseline prefill** and must never be presented as an official announcement, an import *of* the official calendar, or a decision about when a month begins: *record official month start*, *publish official month* and *official Ministry announcement* remain the required vocabulary for the recording actions, and *choose*, *define* and *set* remain prohibited everywhere. **(11) Revision 32(3) is honoured in substance and refined in letter.** R32 kept `recordMonthStart` as *"the single write path"* so a future importer would inherit its ordering rule, its optimistic locking, its draft state and its audit trail. The baseline importer inherits the **ordering rule**, the **draft state** and the **audit trail**, and is **insert-only** — which is precisely why it does not take the optimistic-locking path: there is no existing row for it to lock, because it never touches one. `recordMonthStart` remains the single path by which any month is **created by hand or changed**. The unique `(hijri_year, hijri_month)` constraint is what guarantees a concurrent import cannot double-write a month. **(12) The source is the ICU Umm al-Qura tables shipped with the runtime — no network call at any time, in any environment.** An installation whose runtime lacks that calendar **fails loudly** rather than falling back to an arithmetic Islamic calendar, which would produce plausible dates that are simply wrong. **(13) TD-2 gains no new actor.** The capability write is the Teacher's own, listed beside R106's availability row; the Hijri import is **Super Admin only**, exactly as every other write on that screen already is. **(14) Deliberately unchanged.** R88.5–R88.9 (one range covers a class, ranges are never merged, overlaps refused and touching ranges accepted, a conflict is a time overlap, one profile serves teacher and assistant alike, nothing is backfilled); R90 in every particular; R91's effective-assignment model; R73's Quran-progress scope; Revision 30's Teacher `⊘` on browsing reference data; Revisions 31 and 32 except as narrowly stated in (6) and (11); and §20 rule 16 — **this revision moves no teaching authority whatever, and adds no capability that was not already approved and shipped.**

**Revision 113 (Document Owner decision — the association's partners are Super-Admin-owned reference data, 2026-08-28):** *(Recorded in §0 by the Revision-114 sweep; the decision was taken and applied on 2026-08-28 and this document's §5.7, §14.1 and TD-2 have carried it since. Reconstructed from `docs/SRS-PROPOSAL-R113.md` as applied — no requirement is added, reinterpreted or widened here.)* **(1) Partners are a TABLE, not four lines of copy.** §5.1's landing section renders what the database holds, exactly as the public branch directory already does, so **adding a partner is an administrative act rather than a deployment** — a partner added in the back office appears on the landing page with no frontend change and no release. **(2) A partner is a NAME.** No logo, no URL, no description and no contact: the brief is explicit that none of it may be invented, and **a column exists for a fact the platform holds rather than one it might**.
