An empty logo frame on a public page is worse than no frame, because it reports an absence the data does not have. Adding a logo later is a column and a migration; nothing here forecloses it. **AMENDED as to the description by Document Owner decision, 2026-08-28 (confirmed 2026-08-30):** the Owner supplied that fact, so `Partner.description` exists — nullable `VARCHAR(500)`, carried through the validators, the service, **both** DTOs, the management form and table, and §5.1's landing section. **This is the column following the decision rather than anticipating it**, which is the rule this clause states; **logo, URL and contact remain absent** and are not authorised by this amendment. **(3) `is_visible` is not `deleted_at`.** Withholding a partner from the public site while a relationship is being renewed is an ordinary need and is **not** the act of removing the record. Two questions, two controls. **(4) The landing section renders NOTHING AT ALL when no partner is visible** — specified behaviour, not a degraded state: having no partners is ordinary, where having no branches would be a fault. **(5) TD-3 gains five routes.** `GET /partners` is **public and unauthenticated**, the landing section's only source: live and visible rows, ordered by `display_order` then name (BR-19), exactly `id` and `name`, and **unpaginated**, because the set is bounded by the association's real relationships and a public section showing a subset would misstate who its partners are. `GET`/`POST /admin/partners`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /admin/partners/{id}` are **Super Admin only** (OD-01's sub-decision), asserted in the service; the management read returns every live partner including withheld ones, with the TD-15 `version`; `DELETE` is TD-5 soft delete, and **nothing references a Partner**, so no blocked-delete case arises. **The public and management representations are two DTOs, not one with a filter** (§16.2, Revision 38) — building the public shape by trimming the management one is how a field added for the back office reaches a public page. **(6) §14.1 gains one node — `الشركاء`, `/admin/partners`, inside `الإدارة`, Super Admin only** — placed after `أنواع الجدولة` and before `سلة المحذوفات`, which is that section's own stated logic (the dependency chain, then the standalone nodes) and where Revision 110's proposal placed the scheduling-type catalogue for the same reason. **R105's sequence is extended, not reinterpreted.** **(7) TD-2 gains one row:** *Manage the partner catalogue* — Super Admin `✔`, every other role `⊘`. **(8) The catalogue SHIPS EMPTY** (Owner decision, 2026-08-28, recorded in `docs/CHANGES.log`): partners are entered through the new screen rather than seeded, so no name is fabricated and no deployment is needed to add one.

**Revision 112 (Document Owner decision — global account administration is Super Admin's; picking a person is a smaller question, 2026-08-28):** *(Recorded in §0 by the Revision-114 sweep; the decision was taken and applied on 2026-08-28 and this document's §5.6, §5.7 and TD-2 have carried it since. Reconstructed from the Owner's clarification of 2026-08-28 as recorded in `docs/SRS-PROPOSAL-R111.md` §6a and `docs/SRS-PROPOSAL-R112.md` — no requirement is added, reinterpreted or widened here.)* **(1) `المستخدمون` (`/admin/users`) is SUPER ADMIN ONLY**, and moves in §5 from the Admin surfaces to §5.7. An Admin does not reach global account administration merely because they manage operational data. **(2) The enforcement is SERVER-SIDE, asserted in the service** — hiding the navigation node is not enforcement, and the refusal must reach a typed URL, a forged request, a test and a background job alike. **(3) §20 rule 17 is preserved by UNIFORMITY rather than by `404`.** Rule 17 makes an out-of-reach row answer `404` because *"exists, but not yours"* is itself a disclosure — the right shape while an Admin could edit **some** accounts. An Admin is now refused the **capability** rather than the row, so the same `403` answers an out-of-scope user, a user inside the caller's own branch, and an id that does not exist; **it is the uniformity that makes the answer non-disclosing**, and an implementation that answered `404` for one of the three would reintroduce exactly the disclosure rule 17 forbids. **(4) WITHDRAWING THE ACCOUNT PAGE MUST NOT WITHDRAW AN ADMIN'S WORK.** `GET /admin/users` had served two unrelated purposes under one role list: global account administration, and **picking a person while doing operational work** — staffing a class, enrolling a beneficiary, filling a roster. Only the first is withdrawn. **(5) TD-3 therefore gains `GET /admin/directory` — Admin or Super Admin**, the operational people-picker. **Branch-scoped identically to `GET /admin/users`** (§4.2, Revision 25) — the same query, shared rather than reimplemented, so the two cannot drift — with filters `role`, `branch_id`, `beneficiaries_only` and TD-10 search and paging. **A minimal projection**, and the narrowness is the point: **no address, no phone, no account status and no TD-15 `version`**, because a picker can do nothing that would need them. **No write exists at this path.** **(6) TD-2 changes on two rows:** *Create/edit users; assign roles & branch scopes* moves from Admin + Super Admin to **Super Admin**, and a new row records that an Admin may **read the operational directory** with no account-management capability. **(7) Deliberately unchanged.** R111's deletion model in every particular; the ordinary role-specific management screens, which stay separate; and §4.2's branch-scope rule, which this revision applies rather than alters.

**Revision 111 (Document Owner decision — a person may delete their account; deleting a login does not delete the institution's record, 2026-08-27, with the Owner's authoritative clarification of 2026-08-28):** *(Recorded in §0 by the Revision-114 sweep; the design was ratified on 2026-08-27, clarified on 2026-08-28 and implemented, and this document's §5.7 and TD-2 have carried it since. Reconstructed from `docs/SRS-PROPOSAL-R111.md`, whose status line records the ratification — no requirement is added, reinterpreted or widened here.)* **(1) DELETION IS DE-IDENTIFICATION OF A SURVIVING ROW, NOT ROW REMOVAL.** The measured finding decides the design: of the relationships a `User` carries, the great majority are the institution's record of what happened — attendance, grades, memorisation, consent, audit — and they must survive. Credentials and planning data are deleted; the institutional record is preserved; and the account itself remains as a **tombstone**. **(2) The tombstone reads «حساب محذوف»** — the same wording everywhere it appears, including on a preserved grade or attendance row a مؤطِّرة can still read. It says two true things at once: the record survives, and the person's details were removed. A per-role variant («مستفيدة سابقة» / «مؤطِّرة سابقة») was **considered and rejected**: it says what somebody *was* rather than what happened to the account, and needs a different word per role. **(3) A THREE-DAY WINDOW, SEPARATE FROM BR-15's NINETY.** A deleted account is restorable for **3 days**; **Trash stays at 90** and is not disturbed. This is a second, shorter window for one entity type, never a change to the first — and because the two differ, **Trash must show which window a row is on**, since a Super Admin looking at a 3-day account beside a 90-day record cannot be left to infer the difference from the entity type. The purge is a **pg-boss job modelled on `content.quarantine-purge`** — durable, idempotent and retryable — because a de-identification that half-ran is worse than one that has not run. **(4) During the window, restoration restores THAT account**, and no new account may claim the identity while the old one can still come back. After it, the Google binding is gone and a genuinely new registration is possible. **(5) SELF-DELETION IS AVAILABLE TO EVERY USER, مؤطِّرات INCLUDED.** Teaching responsibility does not remove the right to delete an account; it decides only *when* the deletion may proceed. **(6) LIVE RESPONSIBILITY BLOCKS, WITH A CLEAR EXPLANATION — and blocked is not refused-forever.** A staff user still holding future or live responsibilities that require a responsible person is blocked **until those responsibilities are reassigned**, and the refusal **names what is holding it**: an administrator told *«three future classes and one hidden activity»* knows exactly what to do. The action does **not** offer to reassign in the same step — reassignment stays where it already lives, with its own authorization and its own audit. **(7) WHO MAY DELETE WHOM (the Owner's clarification of 2026-08-28, which overrides anything earlier that reads differently).** **Every authenticated user may soft-delete their own account** — Student, Teacher, Admin and Super Admin alike. **A Super Admin may delete their own account only while another active Super Admin remains**; the last active Super Admin is never deletable, and this is the **existing `LAST_SUPER_ADMIN` guard applied to deletion**, not a second rule with its own drift. **Only a Super Admin may delete another person's account.** On `المستخدمون` a Super Admin may act on any account with **soft delete** and **permanent delete where the classification allows** — and permanent delete **is not row removal**: it is the tombstone performed **now** instead of after three days. **(8) An administrator's deletion uses the SAME 3-day window**, and the person is signed out immediately (R102 revocation) while the account stays restorable — a deletion clicked by mistake is recoverable, which an immediate purge would not be. **(9) EXPLICITLY NOT DECIDED HERE, and not to be inferred from anything above.** How long the preserved records themselves are kept — no legal or operational retention rule is established, and this revision keeps them without claiming they are kept forever. The exact wording shown to the person deleting, which must say plainly that the educational and safeguarding record survives, but is copy. Automatic quarantine destruction (R59.4), which remains an open Owner decision and is **not** authorised by anything here.

**Revision 110 (Document Owner decision — the scheduling-type catalogue is managed reference data, 2026-08-26; ratified into this document 2026-08-30):** **(1) `SchedulingType` is a reference entity** (§7) carrying `name`, `structural_kind`, `attendance_required` *(→ `attendance_mode`, Revision 123)*, `display_order` and TD-15's `version`, soft-deleted under TD-5. It is **seeded, not hardcoded**: the seed establishes the initial state and is never a whitelist, so a subsequent run preserves every rename, reorder, re-flag and addition the Owner has made. **(2) The initial catalogue is five rows:** حصة دراسية (`حضور إجباري` نعم, `class`, order 1) · اختبار (نعم, `exam`, 2) · محاضرة (لا, `activity`, 3) · حفل (لا, `activity`, 4) · عطلة (لا, `activity`, 5). **(3) Five types, THREE entities — and no fifth scheduling model.** R56's routing is stored rather than re-decided: `class` → `RecurringCourseSchedule`, `activity` → `Event`, `exam` → `Exam`. **`structural_kind` is data on the row and is never inferred from the Arabic name** — §4.4b already requires rules to be *"checked generically … rather than hardcoded against a level name"*, and a catalogue whose behaviour depended on its label could never be renamed. It is **fixed after creation**: changing it would re-point every activity recorded against the row at a model that cannot represent them, exactly as §4.4 freezes a course schedule's subject and target. **(4) `attendance_required` is structural data, and it drives the form** (OD-03). Attendance-specific controls are presented **only** where it is true. It is not derivable from the name — اختبار takes attendance and محاضرة does not, and nothing about either word says so. ***(SUPERSEDED by Revision 123: the boolean becomes `attendance_mode` — `disabled` | `optional` | `required` — because it collapsed «may presence be recorded at all» with «are people expected», and عطلة and حفل need the third state. The clause's substance — structural, on the row, never read off the name — is unchanged.)*** **(5) `Event.scheduling_type_id`** records which type an activity is. **Nullable in the schema and required at the write boundary**, which is §7's standing division (R35): R56 told administrators to write عطلة in the title, so every activity created before this revision records its type nowhere a query can reach, and inferring one from that title is the name-matching §4.4b forbids. The foreign key is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` and deletion of a type is refused while any activity names it: a retired type must stay resolvable by the activities that used it, or tidying the catalogue destroys the record of what an activity WAS. **(6) عطلة is an ordinary schedulable activity** (OD-03) with **`attendance_mode = disabled`** *(Revision 123; `attendance_required = false` as originally written)*, shown on the calendar like any other. **It is not a suppression mechanism**: BR-17 keeps non-teaching activity out of the timetable and §4.4(6) makes a cancellation an edit to a Session row, so a holiday cancels no class. R56's finding is preserved, not reversed. **(7) Authorization.** **Reading is any staff member who may schedule** — Admin, Super Admin and a مؤطِّرة (R93/R94), because a picker that refused her would be a form she cannot open. **Writing is Super Admin only** (OD-01's final sub-decision: scheduling types stay undelegated until an Owner decision says otherwise), which is also what keeps R105's الإدارة heading a fact about permission. §14.1 gains one node, **أنواع الجدولة**, inside الإدارة — an addition the Owner asked for, not a reshuffle of the R105 order. **(8) R56 is NOT superseded.** R56 declined `Event.type` because a category would *"drive no rule, job or report"*, and named the condition for reversing itself — *"when filtering or reporting by category becomes a real requirement"*. `attendance_required` is that requirement, so the condition is met rather than the finding overturned; R56's other half stands untouched. **(9) Clauses (2), (3) and (6) were subsequently AMENDED by the Document Owner on 2026-08-28**, and that amendment is implemented and migrated: **محاضرة is `class`** (a lecture is taught — it carries a Subject, a Level and a teaching mode), **عطلة is its own fourth `structural_kind`, `holiday`** (it has no staff, no room, no Subject and no attendance — only which branches and which Categories are off; it is still stored as an `Event`, and the write boundary accepts branch and category scopes only, refusing staff, Levels and groups), and a sixth row **نشاط** is added as the generic `activity` that حفل had been carrying alone. Existing rows were corrected **in place**, ids preserved, and no `Event` referenced any of them. **A holiday still cancels no class** — clause (6)'s substance survives its structural reclassification.

**Revision 109 (Document Owner decision — a scheduling item's visibility is its own fact, 2026-08-26; ratified into this document 2026-08-30):** **(1) Every kind of scheduling item carries a visibility tier**, drawn from the single `visibility` enum §4.4 already defines (`public | private | hidden`): `Event`, `RecurringCourseSchedule`, `Session` and `Exam`. §4.4's *"Sessions are PUBLIC — anonymous visitors browse the timetable"* and §4.6's *"An exam has no visibility tier of its own"* are **superseded**. Every row that existed before this revision is `public`, so the browsable timetable is unchanged in fact; what changes is that it becomes a decision an administrator takes rather than a property of the model. **(2) The tier travels schedule → occurrence through the mechanism that already exists.** `RecurringCourseSchedule.visibility` is the **default**; `session.materialize` **snapshots** it onto each occurrence exactly as it snapshots `room_id` (Revision 43.4) and `delivery_mode` (Revision 97); a future, un-protected occurrence is resynced by a schedule edit; and an `overridden` occurrence is never rewritten. **There is no second override marker** — a `visibility_overridden` column would give *«did a human decide about this occurrence?»* two answers that drift. An `Exam` takes one column and no snapshot, because a sitting materializes nothing. The Revision 50 split carries the tier onto the successor and may change it. **(3) `hidden` means the RESPONSIBLE PERSON, plus Super Admins, and nobody else.** §4.4's *"Visible to Teachers only for events whose scope intersects their own teaching scope … and to **all Admins regardless of branch scope**"* is **superseded in both arms**. Who is responsible is a fact each kind already records: `EventStaff.position = 'responsible'` (R71.3), `SessionStaff.position = 'teacher'`, `ExamStaff.position = 'supervisor'`. **This NARROWS reach that exists today** — every Admin currently sees every hidden Event — and the narrowing is deliberate. An assistant does **not** read a hidden item: R87 §G makes an assistant the main teacher for *operational authorization* on the class she staffs, and `hidden` is not an operation on the class but a statement of who it belongs to, exactly as R71.3 already separates *both positions see* from *only `responsible` may edit*. **(4) A hidden حصة resolves its responsible مؤطِّرة on THAT OCCURRENCE'S OWN DATE**, never as of now. Revision 91 withdrew `@@unique([scheduleId, userId])`, so *"at most one main teacher on any given date"* is an enforced invariant while *"one main teacher for the series"* is not true at all. Resolving as of today would strip a replaced مؤطِّرة of the occurrences she actually taught and hand her ones she did not. `SessionStaff` is the resolution: Revision 43.4's snapshot is written from `CourseScheduleStaff` effective on that occurrence's own date, and where the two can differ — a past, overridden or otherwise protected occurrence — the snapshot is the correct answer, in Revision 91's own words (*"schedule staffing answers who is assigned for this period; `SessionStaff` answers who took this class"*). **(5) `Event.visibility` defaults to `public` for NEW rows only.** The schema default was `private`, which made *«nobody outside the office can see this»* the outcome an administrator got by not choosing. Stored tiers are untouched: rewriting them would publish activities somebody deliberately hid. **(6) The tier is a PUBLICATION rule, not an administration one.** It gates the calendar and the public occurrence surfaces — `GET /calendar`, `GET /me/calendar`, the §5.2 session page, and the sessions a content item is used by — where a caller who may not read an item receives **`404`, never `403`** (§20 rule 17). It is **not** applied to the management lists (`GET /admin/events`, `/admin/course-schedules`, `/admin/exams`), which remain governed by role and branch scope: an Admin who could no longer see a hidden class in the management list could no longer un-hide it, so applying the tier there would make hidden items *unadministrable* rather than confidential. **(7) The `private` tier is unchanged.** §4.4(2) stands as written, including its two accepted trade-offs: private is not filtered by a student's own branch or group (Risk R-6), and staff are bounded by branch scope — so a branch-scoped Admin sees less private material than any approved beneficiary does. Risk R-7 (existence leaking through conflict detection) now applies to a hidden حصة for the same reason it applied to a hidden نشاط. **(8) Deliberately NOT changed.** No new entity, no new marker and no second mechanism — the tier reuses the `visibility` enum, the R43.4 snapshot, the `overridden` flag and the R50 split. The `private` tier. Who may reach a management surface (TD-2 is untouched). §4.9 content visibility, which is a different concept on a different entity.

**Revision 108 (Document Owner correction — initial Production Subject baseline, 2026-08-26):** **(1) Revision 107's Quran-domain model stands, with one naming correction:** the single atomic recitation/correct-pronunciation Subject is canonically **ترتيل وتجويد القرآن**. ترتيل and تجويد are one curriculum area, never two seed rows. The other initial Quran Subjects remain **أحكام القرآن**, **حفظ القرآن**, and **تفسير القرآن**; القرآن الكريم remains the department/domain and never a Subject. **(2) The initial Production Subject baseline is exactly eight rows, in order:** أحكام القرآن, حفظ القرآن, ترتيل وتجويد القرآن, تفسير القرآن, فقه, السيرة النبوية, العقيدة, الأذكار. **محو الأمية is not part of a fresh Production seed.** Only حفظ القرآن carries `tracks_quran_progress = true`. **(3) This is a bootstrap baseline, not a closed curriculum enumeration.** A Super Admin may add other Subjects, including other Quran-domain Subjects, without the seed deleting, renaming, rewriting, or marking them on a later run. Historical rows such as محو الأمية or ترتيل القرآن likewise remain Owner-managed data in an upgraded database. **(4) Runtime memorisation authorization remains structural:** it resolves the unique live `tracks_quran_progress` marker and never compares any Subject name. A new or other Quran-domain Subject receives no memorisation authority merely because of its name or department. Revision 107 remains in force for the marker cardinality, fail-closed states, `LevelSurah`, Tafsir alignment, coverage and BR-9 semantics; this revision supersedes only its canonical recitation name and six-row seed list.

**Revision 107 (Document Owner decision — Quran is a curriculum domain, not a Subject, 2026-08-26):** **(1) The broad term القرآن الكريم names the Quran curriculum/domain and is never itself a `Subject` row.** Revision 43(9), Revision 73(0)/(4), §4.4b, §7, §15.1, BR-9, J6, the Educational Model acceptance line and §20 rule 23 are superseded only where they treated that broad domain as one schedulable Subject. Scheduling continues to use ordinary atomic Subjects: **أحكام القرآن**, **حفظ القرآن**, **ترتيل القرآن**, and **تفسير القرآن**. In this curriculum **تجويد is the same Subject as ترتيل القرآن**, not a second seed row. **(2) `Subject.tracks_quran_progress` has one precise meaning:** staffing a schedule for the marked Subject authorises §4.5 **memorisation** operations for that schedule's resolved audience. It belongs only to **حفظ القرآن**. It does not mean “belongs to the Quran domain”, configure a tracking engine, change coverage, or add `subject_id` to a log. **أحكام القرآن**, **ترتيل القرآن**, and **تفسير القرآن** are ordinary schedulable Subjects and grant no memorisation authority. Teaching and assisting still count equally; Admin and Super Admin scope is unchanged; the absence of a marker still fails closed. **(3) Cardinality has two layers.** The database partial unique index continues to enforce **at most one** live marked Subject, because an empty database must exist before bootstrap and a temporarily unconfigured installation must remain representable. A launch-ready Production seed must establish and assert **exactly one**, and that row must be حفظ القرآن. A conflicting live marker or duplicate live حفظ القرآن rows makes the seed fail loudly; the seed never silently renames, deletes, or reclassifies Owner-managed historical Subjects. **(4) `LevelSurah` records the Surahs selected per Level for the حفظ القرآن memorisation syllabus; تفسير القرآن follows that same per-Level Surah selection pedagogically.** Only حفظ القرآن uses it for §4.5 progress and BR-11 completion. تفسير القرآن remains an ordinary unmarked Subject: staffing it does not authorise memorisation, and it does not contribute to or alter coverage. `QuranProgressLog` remains keyed by student + Surah with no Subject foreign key. أحكام القرآن and ترتيل القرآن use ordinary `LevelSubject` curriculum and do not use `LevelSurah` as progress. **(5) BR-9 preserves the shared Surah curriculum without conflating authorization.** A حفظ القرآن `LevelSubject` creates no generic grading component; when the postponed grading-template engine is built, each Adult-stage `LevelSurah` creates the two historically specified draft components, `[Surah] Memorization` and `[Surah] Tafsir`. Because تفسير القرآن follows those same selected Surahs, its `LevelSubject` likewise must not create a competing generic Tafsir component. No component tables or runtime generation are introduced by this revision. **(6) Production seed Subjects are, in order:** أحكام القرآن, حفظ القرآن (the only marked row), ترتيل القرآن, تفسير القرآن, فقه, محو الأمية. The seed does not create a separate تجويد synonym, القرآن الكريم, or the ambiguous bare تفسير. The list is an idempotent fresh-deployment baseline; existing runtime-edited reference data, including an historical تجويد row, is not overwritten, renamed, or deleted on a re-run.

**Revision 76 (Document Owner decision — list sorting and manual reordering, 2026-08-18):** **(1) TD-10 gains an optional sort.** Every paginated or listed collection accepts **`?sort_by=<field>&sort_dir=asc|desc`**, snake_case like `page_size` beside it. **`sort_by` names a FIELD IN THE CONTRACT, never a database column**: each endpoint declares an **explicit allow-list** mapping a public name to its own ordering expression, and a name outside that list is **`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`** — never ignored, and never passed through to the query. `sort_dir` accepts exactly `asc` and `desc`; a direction with no field is refused, because the caller believes they are sorting and are not. **Sorting is performed by the database, never by the client**: a client sorting a page of a paginated collection would order *that page* and misreport it as the collection's order. **(2) Absent parameters preserve BR-19 exactly.** The existing `display_order` → collated-name order remains the default for every collection and is what a caller receives when it asks for nothing — this revision adds a capability and moves no default. **(3) Every resolved order ends in a deterministic tiebreaker** (`id`), because TD-10's pagination is offset-based and two rows sharing a sort value may otherwise appear on two pages or on neither. **(4) A reorder contract joins TD-3: `PATCH /admin/{resource}/order`** with body **`{ ids: [...] }`** — **the sequence itself, not a per-row number**. The server assigns `display_order` from the sequence's positions, so **duplicate and gapped values are structurally impossible** rather than validated against, and no client performs arithmetic that races another client's. The request is refused unless `ids` is **exactly the live set** of that resource in the caller's scope — no duplicates, none missing, none foreign (`VALIDATION_FAILED`, naming which via `DUPLICATE_ID` · `UNKNOWN_ID` · `INCOMPLETE_ORDER`) — because a partial sequence cannot say where the omitted rows belong. A foreign id and a nonexistent one answer identically (§20 rule 17). It is applied in **one transaction**, is **idempotent** so a retry after a dropped response is safe, and returns the resulting order. **(5) The reorder authority is the resource's existing write authority**, and TD-2 gains **no row**: whoever may edit a Branch may reorder Branches, and the live set is read through the caller's own visibility so a scoped caller cannot reorder rows they may not see. **No TD-15 `version` and no `Trash` entry**: `version` answers *did this row change under me* and R59 snapshots *deletions*, whereas a reorder is a statement about the collection — treating it as N row edits would fill the audit with rows nobody edited. **(6) `display_order` semantics are settled: after any reorder a collection's values are `1..n`, contiguous and unique.** NULL remains valid for a row that has never been ordered and continues to sort last. **(7) Six entities carry `display_order` and FIVE are manually orderable** — `Branch`, `Category`, `Subject`, `Level`, `AdministrativeGroup`. **`TeachingGroup` is deliberately excluded**: it carries the column and no interface has ever set it, so ordering circles is not a decision anybody takes today, and adding the gesture would invent a workflow rather than expose one. **(8) §14.2 gains the interaction rules.** A sortable column's header is a **button** whose first press sorts ascending, second descending, third ascending again, with the current direction shown; a non-sortable column carries no sort affordance and **the actions column is never sortable**. The **`الترتيب` column is removed from every table** — a persisted ordering is expressed by **dragging a row**, not by typing a number — while the underlying field stays. **Drag is available only while the table is in its canonical order**: under any temporary column sort the visible sequence is not the business one, so dropping a row into it would persist a position the reader never intended, and the table states which mode it is in rather than failing silently.

**Revision 74 (Document Owner decision — enrolment gets a screen, 2026-08-13):** **Revision 66 made the Administrative Group optional and gave the service `enrolInLevel`, but only the approval path ever called it.** The single enrolment endpoint is `POST /admin/administrative-groups/{id}/roster`, which **requires a group** — so once a مستفيدة was approved there was no way to enrol her into a **second Level**, or into a **Level nobody has subdivided**, even though R66 made that state ordinary. The consequences were observed rather than theorised: the platform held **one** enrolment, a whole-Level exam's grade sheet resolved **no** audience, and *"where are مستفيدات assigned?"* had no answer any screen could give. This is the **fourth** occurrence of one pattern — a capability living in a service with no route or node to reach it — after R69 (`مواد المستوى`), R70.1 (grade entry) and R72 (Teacher activity authoring). **(1) `التسجيلات` joins §14.1** as `/admin/enrollments`, in الشؤون التعليمية beside the group roster. **It is not a second roster**: the group roster is the per-group view of the very same `Enrollment` rows, and this is the Level view of them — *the* primary enrolment fact, since §7 (R66) makes the Level the thing a student is enrolled **in** and the group an optional subdivision of it. **(2) No new model, no new authorization, and no new service logic.** `enrolAtPlacement` is called exactly as the approval path calls it, so **one implementation** decides a placement: `enrolInGroup` when a Group is named, `enrolInLevel` when one is not. Every rule already enforced there stays where it is — `assertCanManage` for the role, **`assertCanActOnBranch` for the branch**, R27's `gender_restriction` eligibility, and **BR-21's one-enrolment-per-Level** refusal with its explained conflict rather than a raw constraint violation. A Group, when named, must belong to the chosen Level **and** branch, which `enrolInGroup` already asserts and the composite foreign key already guarantees. **(3) The screen shows its data.** Per the principle recorded with the `حلقات المواد` redesign — *filters narrow visible data; they must never be the precondition for it appearing* — it lists the enrolments the caller may see on load, branch-scoped for an Admin and unrestricted for a Super Admin, each row reading **مستفيدة → الفئة — المستوى → المجموعة (إن وجدت)**. The Level control is the platform's shared selector. **(4) The exam consequence is a consequence, not a feature.** R58's audience resolution is untouched: a whole-Level exam resolves *the Level's students at the exam's branch* (`Enrollment.branch_id`, R66), so a مستفيدة enrolled here **appears in that exam's grade sheet automatically**. **No manual Exam → Student assignment is added**, and none is needed — the existing scope model already answers it. **Deliberately absent:** no second roster, no change to approval semantics (which still place a student at approval through the same function), and no unenrolment path for a group-less enrolment — the group roster's removal is unchanged, and extending it is a separate, smaller gap recorded rather than bundled here.

**Revision 73 (Document Owner decision — Quran progress: a home, an audit row, and the scope that actually applies, 2026-08-12):** **(0) What the M4 audit found already settled, recorded first because it decides the size of this revision.** R43's model is **kept**: the Quran is a Subject for scheduling, `QuranProgressLog` stays keyed on `(student, surah)`, and **no `subject_id` is added** — §7 still calls that a defect. The link between Quran teaching and Quran progress is **operational**, through the schedules a مؤطرة staffs, not a foreign key. **Teaching and assisting count equally** (R43 gave them one table and one rule), and **no new Role and no parallel authorization** is created. **TD-15's existing ruling is also kept**: `QuranProgressLog` gains **no `version`** — see (4). **(1) §14.1 — Quran logging had no reachable node.** The sitemap listed `/teacher/students/{id}/quran`, whose path carries an id, so **no menu could link to it** and the teacher registry had no entry — the **third** occurrence of one defect, after R69 (`مواد المستوى`) and R70.1 (grade entry). **`/teacher/quran` joins §14.1** with the student as **`?student=`**, the `/resources?level=` precedent §14.1 already sets. The screen asks which مستفيدة when the link supplies none, choosing from **the مؤطرة's own Quran students**, so the selector cannot offer somebody the server would refuse. `/dashboard/student/quran` is unchanged — a student reads their own and needs no id. **(2) TD-8 recorded the correction but not the entry.** The grid carried `quranlog.update` and `quranlog.delete`; **the write preceding both had no action type**, the same gap R70.3 closed for `grade.enter`. **`quranlog.create` joins TD-8** (student, surah, range, category, recalculated coverage), outside R19's purge allowlist like every non-`auth.*` type. **(3) Quran progress requires QURAN teaching scope, and the conflict is with TD-2 rather than §4.4c.** §4.4c defines *own students* as **the union of the audiences of every schedule a مؤطرة staffs, whichever Subject each teaches** — that definition is correct, is used by content, exams and social data alike, and **is not amended**. What was wrong is the TD-2 row citing it: a مؤطرة teaching a مستفيدة only Fiqh could log that مستفيدة's memorization. The row now reads **✔ own students *whose Quran they teach***: she must staff a **live** schedule whose Subject carries `tracks_quran_progress` and whose audience contains that مستفيدة, in **any** of §4.4c's three modes — `entire_level` (that Level's students at the schedule's branch, `Enrollment.branch_id` per R66), `administrative_group`, or `teaching_group` (the حلقة). **Implemented as an optional Subject filter on the existing `studentsTaughtBy`**, which stays the single implementation of §4.4c — never a second resolver. Admins and Super Admins are unchanged: their scope is the branch and TD-2 grants them the row unqualified. **(4) `Subject.tracks_quran_progress` — the marker, and why a marker is unavoidable.** (3) cannot be enforced without knowing **which Subject is the Quran**, and `Subject` carried `name`, `display_order` and `version` and nothing else. **Matching on the name is prohibited**: R27 made Subjects generic editable reference data and §4.4b already requires rules *"checked generically … rather than hardcoded against a level name"*, so a name test would stop protecting anybody the day somebody renames it; and **`LevelSurah` cannot answer it**, joining a Level to a Surah and saying nothing about which Subject delivers it. This was **the same open gap R64.7 recorded for the adult Category**. The marker is **one boolean, default false, with at most one live Subject carrying it** (partial unique index — §4.5's engine is singular, and two Quran-tracking Subjects would make *which* teaching authorises a log ambiguous). **It configures nothing else**: no per-Subject tracking mode, no second engine, and no change to how coverage is computed. A `SystemSetting` holding the Subject's id was **rejected**: a settings row carries no foreign key, so deleting that Subject would silently disable Quran authorization instead of being refused. **(5) TD-15.5's reason is corrected without changing its conclusion.** It read *"coverage is derived-on-read from committed rows"* — **Revision 10 superseded that** with the `StudentSurahProgress` cache, so there is an aggregate row now. The conclusion survives because R10's **read-side self-heal guard** compares the cache's stamp against the newest log and repairs on mismatch, making a stale aggregate structurally unobservable. Appends by co-teachers still interleave freely. The one uncovered case — two staff correcting the **same** log row — stays **last-write-wins deliberately**, because BR-13 recomputes the union from whatever rows exist, so a lost correction is re-correctable; a lost grade is not, which is why `Grade` carries a `version` and this does not. **Deliberately absent:** no `subject_id` on the log, no `version` on the log, no auto-exam trigger (§10.1), no averages, and no new formula — BR-13's union is the only arithmetic.

**Revision 72 (a capability with nowhere to happen — the Teacher's Activity authoring, 2026-08-12):** **TD-2 has granted a Teacher *"Schedule/edit Events (all visibility tiers) … own scope; hidden allowed"* since Revision 43, and `event.service.ts` has enforced exactly that scope ever since — but no §14.1 node let anybody use it.** §14.1's `/teacher/schedules` described *"the Recurring Course Schedules this teacher staffs"* and their Sessions, and closed with *"they do **not** create or edit schedules"*; the teacher portal has three other nodes, none of them about events. So the platform has carried a working, tested, branch-scoped write capability that the interface offered nowhere — **structurally the same defect Revision 69 found for `مواد المستوى` and Revision 70.1 for grade entry**, and the third time this document has paid for a capability and a sitemap being written apart. **(1) The clause is clarified rather than reversed.** *"They do not create or edit schedules"* meant **Recurring Course Schedules**, and that stands — Revision 71.0 records why it is load-bearing rather than merely conservative: §4.4c derives a Teacher's entire scope **from the schedules they staff**, so creating one would let them widen their own reach, while an Event carries no staffing and expands nothing. The sentence now says which of the two it means. **(2) `/teacher/schedules` gains the Activity branch of the SHARED scheduling form**, restricted to that one kind. **No new node and no second screen**: Revision 56 already made scheduling one form whose *type is a field*, so the teacher view offers the one type they may author rather than a parallel page — which is exactly what Revision 69 spent a revision undoing elsewhere. **(3) Nothing about the scope changes.** A Teacher still may not scope an event to a branch, a category, a level or the Global scope, and must name Administrative Groups they teach (§4.9, TD-2, unchanged); the server refuses every other combination and the form cannot express one. **Deleting an event remains Admin** (§4.4, unchanged), and a Teacher who creates one is recorded `responsible` for it in the same transaction (R71.3), so the event she just made is one she can still edit. **No schema change, no new endpoint, no new permission** — this revision moves nothing but the sitemap.

**Revision 71 (Document Owner decision — an event has somebody responsible for it, 2026-08-12):** **(0) What the audit found already correct, stated first because it decides the size of this revision.** The association's domain — *مؤطرة* is any non-beneficiary person working with the association, *مستفيدة* the person receiving the service, and a مؤطرة may hold several responsibilities at once — is **already how the platform is built**. §2.1 says *"a single person may hold multiple roles concurrently … switching context via an account switcher"*; `UserBranchRole` is a join unique per `(user, role, branch)`, so **each capability already carries its own scope**; and **assisting is already a position on an assignment rather than a job**, because Revision 43(8) introduced `CourseScheduleStaff` precisely to give assistants *"a home without inventing a Role"*. Nothing requires a staffed person to hold the `teacher` role. **No new Role is created, no capability table, no parallel authorization system, and §4.4c's teaching derivation is untouched.** The `teacher` position value also keeps its spelling: it names a *position on one schedule*, and renaming a stored value to improve a label would be a migration paying for presentation. **(1) The one real gap: an `Event` carries four audience joins and NOBODY responsible for it.** There is no staff table on `Event` in the schema, the code or this document, so *a main responsible مؤطرة and one or more assistant مؤطرات* — the association's own description of how a celebration is run — **cannot be recorded**. **`EventStaff (event_id, user_id, position)`** joins §7 with **`EventStaffPosition` (`responsible | assistant`)**, shaped exactly like `ExamStaff` and `CourseScheduleStaff` and following Revision 59's lifecycle for all three: tombstone what is no longer wanted, revive what returns, and **no Trash entry**, because reconciliation is one field of an update. **(2) The gap is an AUTHORIZATION one, which is why it matters.** TD-2 grants a Teacher *"Schedule/edit Events (own scope)"* and that scope resolves through `teacherEventScope`, which derives **from teaching schedules** — so a مؤطرة responsible for a celebration who teaches nothing had **empty event scope and could manage nothing**. **Event scope becomes a union**: the events she staffs through `EventStaff` **∪** the events her §4.4c teaching scope already reaches. It is added as **one arm inside `policies/roster-resolution.ts`**, the single implementation of what a member of staff may reach, never as a second resolver. **(3) Position is authorization-bearing here, and the divergence is deliberate.** The existing `*Staff` tables record a position that no authorization reads — `staffsSession` counts co-teachers and assistants identically, because both deliver the same class and R43 gave them one rule on purpose. **An event is asymmetric where a class is not:** the association names **one** main responsible مؤطرة, and *responsible* means answerable. So a **`responsible`** مؤطرة may see the event (Hidden included) **and edit its attributes regardless of teaching scope**; an **`assistant`** may see it and **not** edit it. **(4) Assigning staff is Admin and above.** Being answerable for an event is not authority to decide who else answers for it, and allowing it would let a مؤطرة with momentary edit rights make herself permanently responsible. **One structural exception, which is not a grant:** a مؤطرة who *creates* an event is recorded `responsible` in the **same transaction**, because creating it is what makes her answerable. **Deletion stays Admin and above** for the reason Revision 70.4 gave about exams — `Event` carries no `created_by`, so *"her own but not another person's"* is not expressible, and **no column is added to make it sayable**. **Creation scope is unchanged**: a مؤطرة still may not scope an event to a branch, category, level or the Global scope and must still name groups she teaches, so one with no teaching scope cannot conjure an event from nothing — **an Admin creates it and names her responsible**, which is how the work is actually assigned. **(5) TD-2 gains two rows and TD-8 gains `event.staff_change`** (detail: event, positions assigned), a decision distinct from `event.update` because *who answers for this celebration* is not an attribute edit; Revision 19's allowlist keeps it out of `audit.purge`. **Deliberately absent:** no `created_by` on `Event`, no event-scope editing on update (§4.4's populate-at-creation rule with backfill as the one later path is untouched), and no change to `UserBranchRole` or to any educational authorization.

**Revision 70 (Document Owner decision — grade entry gets a home, an audience and an audit row, 2026-08-12):** the M5a audit found §4.6's model **complete** and its exam half **built** — `Exam`, `Grade`, `ExamStaff` and `StudentExamSubmission` all exist, exam CRUD shipped with R58, and **`Grade` has no service, route, adapter or screen**. That single gap is in-school grade entry. This revision closes the four *documentary* gaps in front of it and changes **no schema, no entity and no concept**. **(0) What stands unchanged, because it is load-bearing together.** §4.4c derives a Teacher's entire reach **from the schedules they staff**, so TD-2's `⊘` on *Create/edit Recurring Course Schedules* is not a convenience: a Teacher who could create one and staff themselves onto it would **widen their own scope** — branch, level, subject and every student in the resolved audience. Events carry no staffing and expand nothing, which is why the same table grants them `✔ (own scope)`. **Both rows are unchanged.** Equally unchanged: R58's single `Exam` entity (**no `ExamSitting`**), an exam's freedom from term boundaries (**a past date is permitted and no stored retroactive flag is added** — «سُجّل لاحقًا» is derived from `created_at > date` at render time), and §10.1/R12's postponement of averages, transcripts and the template engine, including *"do not hardcode an interim average formula"*. **(1) §14.1 — grade entry had nowhere to happen.** §14.1 listed authoring and grading at `/teacher/exams` while R56/R58 put exam *scheduling* on `/admin/schedules`, so **an Admin had no node from which to enter a grade**, and `/admin/grading` is reserved for §10.1's postponed template engine and must not be borrowed. **`/admin/exam-grades` joins §14.1**, carrying the exam id as **`?exam=`** — the `/resources?level=` precedent §14.1 already sets and R69 applied twice, a second path segment being a node §14.1 does not list. **`/teacher/exams` is unblocked and reaches THE SAME screen**: one grade sheet with two ways in, never two implementations. **(2) BR-7's roster predated the audience it describes.** *"Every student on the **Group roster** without a score"* was written before R58 made an exam's audience *the whole Level unless a group is named* and before R66 made the group optional — taken literally it initialises nothing for a whole-Level exam and misses every ungrouped student. It now reads **every student in the exam's AUDIENCE**: the named Administrative Group, or, when `administrative_group_id` is NULL, the students enrolled in the exam's Level at the exam's branch, resolved through **`Enrollment.branch_id`** (R66). The timing (R10 — the teacher's first draft save), the replaceability of rows before and after Publish, and *0 counts as 0* are all unchanged. **(3) TD-8 recorded publishing but not marking.** The grid carried `grade.publish`, `grade.republish` and `grade.passfail_override`, and **the write that precedes all three had no action type** — the most frequent grade operation would have left no trail, on a record about a child's attainment. **`grade.enter` joins TD-8**, detail *exam, students affected count*, **written once per sheet save rather than per row** (a sheet is saved as a sheet; one row per student would bury the decision in its own volume). **It is not purgeable**: Revision 19 fixed `audit.purge` to an enumerated six-item `auth.*` allowlist and prohibited extension by inference. **(4) TD-2's exam row conflated three acts, and the implementation disagreed with all of it.** *"Author exams; publish/re-publish grades"* covered authoring a paper, booking a sitting and publishing attainment under one grant — while `exam.service.ts` refused Teachers **every** exam write, contradicting §4.5's *"teachers create exams manually"*, §2.1's *"schedules/grades exams"* and the row itself. **The row splits in four**: *create/edit an exam sitting*, **delete** an exam sitting, *enter/amend grades*, and *publish/re-publish grades*. Three are **Super Admin ✔ · Admin ✔ own branches · Teacher ✔**; **deletion is Admin and above**, because `Exam` carries no `created_by` and *"not another person's exam"* is therefore not expressible — and a column is **not** added to make a narrower grant sayable, since editing already covers a mistake a Teacher needs to correct. **A Teacher's exam scope is §4.4c and nothing new**: the branch among those they staff, the `(level, subject)` pair among those they teach, and any named group among their own — a whole-Level exam only where they staff that Level — with rooms still validated against the branch as R58 requires. **Deliberately not granted:** deleting another person's exam, the Global content/event scope, and anything on the Recurring Course Schedule. **(5) Recorded, and fixed with this revision rather than by it:** `studentsTaughtBy` and `assertCanAccessStudent` both still resolved a student's branch through **`AdministrativeGroup.branch_id`**, which R66 replaced with `Enrollment.branch_id`. Every student enrolled directly in an unsubdivided Level was therefore invisible to their own teacher and to a branch-scoped Admin — a live authorization defect on exactly the students grade entry must reach. §4.4c already says the right thing; the code did not.

**Revision 69 (Document Owner decision — the two hierarchies get their own navigation, 2026-08-12):** **(0) The audit's finding first, because it decides the size of this revision: the MODEL and the AUTHORIZATION are already right, and neither changes.** A Level belongs to a Category and not a Branch (§7); a Level may exist with no Administrative Group and students may be enrolled directly in one (**R66**); a Subject is independent and needs no Teaching Group, an unsplit Subject being taught to the whole Level through `entire_level` (§7, §4.4c). The permissions asked for are already in the services: **Level, Category, Subject and the Level↔Subject pairing are Super-Admin WRITE and Admin-readable**, and a Teaching Group's **structure is Super Admin while its MEMBERSHIP is Admin** and branch-scoped (R43.3). **No schema change, no service change, no matrix row.** **(1) What was actually wrong: two screens had no way in.** `مواد المستوى` and `حلقات المواد` exist and work, but their paths carry ids — `/admin/levels/{id}/subjects` and `.../{subjectId}` — so **no menu can link to them**, and §14.1 listed Subject Organisation while no navigation node could reach it. The consequence is what was observed in use: the only entry points were **row actions borrowed by unrelated screens**, so `المستويات` grew a Subject action and `مجموعات المستويات` grew another. That is a navigation defect, not a modelling one. **(2) One node each, with the id as a QUERY parameter** — the pattern §14.1 already uses for `/resources` (Revision 43: *"one level's contents behind `?level=` — because a second path segment would be a navigation node §14.1 does not list"*). **`/admin/level-subjects`** (`?level=`) and **`/admin/teaching-groups`** (`?level=&subject=`) join §14.1; each screen selects what it needs **in the page**, and the parameter is a deep link rather than a second node. The existing parameterised paths **redirect** to them, so bookmarks survive. Adding menu entries to the old paths was rejected: a menu entry cannot supply an id, which is the whole reason these screens had no node, and a "pick a Level first" landing page would be a third screen for a question the destination can ask itself. **(3) §14.1 is restructured along the two hierarchies.** **Administration (Super Admin), in dependency order: الفئات → المستويات → المواد → مواد المستوى.** `المستويات` **moves here from Academic**: a Level is curriculum structure, its writes have always been Super Admin, and R66 removed the last operational thing it did — creating a first group. **Its read endpoint stays Admin-accessible**, because scheduling, the approval queue's placement dialog and the groups screen all feed selectors from it, and gating the data rather than the screen would break an Admin's daily work — the rule Revision 61 set for `GET /admin/branches`, applied again. **الشؤون التعليمية (operational Admin): مجموعات المستويات · حلقات المواد** — both are where an administrator subdivides *because subdivision is actually needed* and places students. **(4) One responsibility per screen, and the duplicated Subject actions are removed.** `المستويات` answers *which Levels exist, in which Category* and no longer assigns Subjects; `مواد المستوى` answers *which Subjects a Level teaches*; `مجموعات المستويات` answers *how a Level is subdivided and who is in each group* and no longer links to Subjects; `حلقات المواد` answers *how a Subject WITHIN a Level is subdivided and who attends*. Neither of the two screens that had grown a Subject action is about Subjects. **(5) Deliberately absent: no schema change, no automatic creation** — a Level still gets no Group and a Subject still gets no Circle merely by existing (R66, §7, both untouched) — **no renaming**, Administrative Group and Teaching Group keeping their distinct names and meanings (§20 rule 22), **no Subject-side model change**, since §7 already specifies it correctly and altering it for symmetry with the Level side is precisely what was ruled out, and **no authorization change in either direction**.

**Revision 68 (mechanism for a behaviour Revision 62 already mandates — the identity-binding review item, 2026-08-12):** §4.3 (R62.9) says *"binding a first identity to a student who has approved links raises a **non-blocking review item**, and an administrator decides"*, and specifies no mechanism. This supplies one, and nothing else. It closes the last open item of Revision 62. **(1) The state is reached by the STAFF path**, which is why it is worth building: staff pre-provision a student against a Google address, a parent is linked to them — **permitted**, because R62.9 restricts linking to accounts with no `UserIdentity` and a pre-provisioned account has none — and the student then signs in, at which point `bindIdentity` (§4.1b step 4b) makes a minor with parents attached into a person with their own login. That is simply how an older student who previously had a parent acting for them starts acting for themselves. **(2) `FamilyLink` gains `identity_review_raised_at`** (nullable). Binding a first identity stamps it on **every approved, live link naming that student**, in the same transaction as the binding. **Non-blocking is structural rather than promised**: nothing reads the stamp for authorization, `resolveActingStudent` is unchanged, the links keep working, the student keeps their new login, and **no branch of `bindIdentity` can refuse because of it**. A **single nullable timestamp, not a status** — the link's `status` is a decided value (TD-1) and overloading it would make *"is this link approved"* and *"has anyone looked at this"* one question. The stamp is cleared when an administrator decides; the decision lives in the audit trail, which is where *who looked at this child's arrangement, and when* belongs. **(3) It appears in the approvals queue as a fourth type**, `identity-review`, **one item per student**, grouping every stamped link and carrying the parents attached — the decision is about the relationship, not one row. **Derived exactly like the other three**: nothing is enqueued, and the item exists while a stamped, approved, live link does, which is what stops the queue showing an item nobody can act on. **No branch**, for the reason Revision 43.3 gave about Teaching Groups: the entity has no branch of its own, and resolving one through the student's enrolment would make one filter mean two different things. **(4) Two outcomes, through the queue's existing verbs.** **Approve** means *the links stand* — the stamp clears and the links are untouched. **Reject** means *this person now acts for themselves* — the links are soft-deleted, which is §4.3's revocation mechanism since Revision 16, and the stamp clears. Rejection requires a reason, as every rejection in this queue does (§5.6). **`familylink.identity_review` joins TD-8**, recording the student, the links and the outcome; `familylink.revoke` already exists and is written by the rejection path, so the trail carries both *what was decided* and *what it did* — one row would have had to mean both. **(5) Deliberately absent: no automatic revocation at majority** (R62.9 records that whether the law compels it is **pending legal/CNDP confirmation**, and nothing here assumes an answer — an administrator decides every time); **no age and no birth date**, the trigger being structural in the same way §4.3 defines a minor without one; and **no new endpoint**. **One nullable column, one TD-8 row, one queue type, no authorization change, and nothing that can refuse a login.**

**Revision 67 (Document Owner decision — a child's branch and stage are the CHILD's, 2026-08-12):** A single request must support **different children asking for different Categories at different Branches**. **(1) The storage was already right and three places were not.** `child_application` has carried `requested_category_id` since R62 and `requested_branch_id` since R64.2, **per row**, and approval already decides one child at a time reading that child's own values — so there is **no migration and no schema change**. What treated them as request-level was `parentChildRegistrationSchema` (one of each for the whole request), `registration.service.ts` (which copied both onto every child) and the `/profile/register-child` form. R62's *"one stage for the family, chosen once"* and R64.2's *"the family's chosen branch is copied onto each application"* are **amended**: both are now collected per child. **(2) `childCore` gains `requested_branch_id` and `requested_category_id`, both REQUIRED** — required because they always were, and moving a mandatory question does not make it answerable by silence. An approver must know for **each** child what was asked: §4.1 step 1 preselects a Level from the Category, and Revision 39 makes the branch what the §14.2 queue filters on. **They remain requests, never placements** (R39's rule, unchanged) — nothing reads either to choose, validate or refuse, and R66.5 lets an approver place any child either way regardless. **(3) The parent's own `intended_branch_id` is DERIVED, not asked twice.** Revision 39 records *the branch the applicant asked for*; on the parent+child path the applicant is a **parent, not a student** — they enrol in nothing, and the branch they are associated with is wherever their children go. Asking separately would put a request-level branch back on the very form this revision removes it from, and produce two answers that must agree or confuse. So it is **taken from the first child's requested branch**. Nothing downstream changes: the column keeps its meaning and type, the §14.2 filter still reads it for the registration item, and a family spread across branches is filed under the first — a reasonable answer to *where did this parent apply*, and the only one that invents nothing. **The adult path is untouched**: `branch_id` and `category_id` stay top-level there, because the applicant IS the student and the fields describe them. **(4) One shape, both flows.** The two controls join the **shared** child fieldset, so `/register` and `/profile/register-child` cannot diverge again — the failure R64 and R65 were each written to repair. `POST /child-applications` already accepted both per child (R64) and its contract is unchanged. BR-1, R62.3b and R62.7 are untouched: the per-child media release and schooling stage simply gain two more genuinely per-child fields beside them.

**Revision 66 (Document Owner decision — a student is enrolled in a Level; a Group is a subdivision, 2026-08-11):** **(0) What does NOT change, stated first.** The **Subject side is untouched**: §7 already says *"A Subject with no Teaching Groups in a Level is taught to the entire Level. Creating Teaching Groups is not a prerequisite for teaching a Subject"*, Subjects remain independent entities reaching Levels through `LevelSubject`, Circles remain scoped to `(Subject, Level)`, and `entire_level` remains how an unsplit Subject is delivered. A Level still belongs to a Category and to no Branch. **`AdministrativeGroup` and `TeachingGroup` keep their names** — they encode §20 rule 22's organisation-versus-delivery distinction, which this revision must not blur; the Arabic labels «مجموعات المستويات» and «حلقات المواد» are presentation and need no clause. BR-21's `UNIQUE (student_id, level_id)` is unchanged. **(1) §7's Enrollment clause is amended.** *"A student belongs to one or more Levels, and to **exactly one** Administrative Group inside each enrolled Level"* becomes: **a student is enrolled in a LEVEL; a Level that needs no subdivision needs no Administrative Group and a student may be enrolled in it DIRECTLY; when a Level is subdivided a student holds AT MOST ONE group within it.** A Group is required **only when the Level is actually subdivided** — creating one is not a prerequisite for admitting anybody, exactly as it is not a prerequisite for teaching a Subject. `enrollment.administrative_group_id` becomes **nullable**. **`StudentLevel` stays withdrawn** (Revision 43): `level_id` was always a column on `Enrollment`, so a student's Level was never derived and this introduces no second source of truth. **(2) The branch moves to the Enrollment, and this is the whole revision.** A student's branch existed **only** through their group — `Enrollment → AdministrativeGroup.branch_id`, named identically by §5.2 and Revision 43.3 and read at **13 code sites**; R43.3 relied on it explicitly, calling the group's branch *"a referent that does exist"* because a Level spans branches. Making the group optional destroys that referent for every ungrouped student, taking Admin branch scoping, Teaching-Group placement authority, the Educational Library's own-branch ordering and `entire_level` audience resolution with it. So **`Enrollment` carries `branch_id`**, **required**, and it is the single answer to *where is this student*: **the enrolment is the operational assignment**, and that is where a branch belongs, because a Level spans branches and a Subject spans branches while a placement does not. **Groups keep their own `branch_id` unchanged**, so a subdivided Level still reads as the association describes it. **The two cannot disagree, and that is enforced rather than intended:** a composite foreign key `(administrative_group_id, branch_id) → AdministrativeGroup(id, branch_id)` — **the same device Revision 43 already uses** for `(administrative_group_id, level_id)` — and because PostgreSQL does not enforce a composite FK when any column is NULL, the ungrouped case needs no exception while the grouped case cannot drift. **§5.2 and Revision 43.3 are corrected together:** *"the person's own Branch"* is now **`Enrollment.branch_id`**, still one rule in one place, and still **never** `User.intended_branch_id` (Revision 39). **(3) Three rules retire, each having existed only to guarantee that a Level always had a group.** **TD-4.6b** — `createLevel` created the Level *and* its first group atomically: **retired**, a Level is created alone and its form asks for no branch, because a branch was never a property of a Level. **TD-4.6d** — the bootstrap backfill of `المجموعة 1` into every Level: **retired**, being the same guarantee applied to existing data. **`LAST_GROUP_IN_LEVEL`** — a Level could not lose its last group: **retired**; deleting a group that still holds students stays refused by `ENROLMENTS_EXIST`, the rule that actually protects people, while an empty group may now be deleted even when it is the last, leaving the Level directly enrollable. **The consequence is the point:** Levels with no group stop being a defect and become ordinary Levels nobody has needed to subdivide. **(4) Placement, approval and delivery.** §4.1 is unchanged — *"an approved account with no enrollment is a person the platform admitted and then lost"* — but the placement it demands is now **a Level and a branch**, with a group only where one exists, so **an approver is never blocked by a missing group**. **`entire_level` resolves by `Enrollment.branch_id`** rather than the group's: the clause's meaning is unchanged (*that Level's students at this schedule's branch*) and it now answers correctly for ungrouped students instead of omitting them. `administrative_group` and `teaching_group` modes are unchanged. **`Grade.administrative_group_id` (sitting provenance, R43) is ALREADY nullable and stays so, for the same reason**: a student who sat an exam while in no group has no group to record, and inventing one would be a fabricated record. **(5) Migration is TD-6b expand → backfill → contract and every value is derived from data that already exists:** add `enrollment.branch_id` nullable and make `administrative_group_id` nullable; backfill `branch_id` from the row's own `administrative_group.branch_id`; then `SET NOT NULL` and add the composite FK. No row is deleted and no column is dropped. **No new entity, no new concept, and no change to authorization** — branch-scoped authority reads the same fact from a column instead of a join.

**Revision 65 (Document Owner decision — the personal section is role-independent, 2026-08-11):** Registering a child is an act of **a person**, not of a role: a مؤطِّرة who is nobody's student must be able to request registration of her own child. **(1) The model already existed and was not used.** §5.2 is titled *Shared / Cross-Role* and `Profile (/profile)` is already in it; §14.1 lists the node; neither has ever been implemented — so Revision 64, looking for somewhere to put the child-registration page, put it under **a role's area** at `/dashboard/student/register-child`. §4.3's prose carried the same mistake, describing *two* role-shaped entry points (*"a parent … from the role switcher's action; an adult student … from their own student area"*) for an act R62 had already unified into **one service and one endpoint**. **(2) This changes no authorization whatever.** `POST /child-applications` sits on the guarded router and requires only an authenticated active account; `submitChildApplications` performs **no role check by design**, because both its callers establish the caller by other means. A Teacher could therefore always have submitted one — the interface simply gave her nowhere to do it. **TD-2 gains no row and loses none.** **(3) `/profile` is implemented as the personal section**, reached from the header's account menu by **every** authenticated account whatever its active role, carrying the acts that concern the person rather than a capacity they are working in: their own details, editing the part that is theirs to edit, registering one or more children, and the status of the requests they have already made (`GET /child-applications/mine`, already registered). It is **not role-gated**, and §14.4's no-permission state does not apply to it: every account has a person behind it. **(4) `/profile/register-child` replaces R64.3's node**, which is removed from §14.1. The page moves verbatim; only its address and entry point change. **The Student Dashboard keeps no registration link** — one entry point rather than one per role is what stops the two field sets diverging again, which is the exact failure R64 was written to repair. §4.3's sentence is corrected to name the personal section instead of the switcher and the student area. **(5) `ولي الأمر` is unchanged.** The Parent role is about reaching **already-approved** children; selecting `ولي الأمر` → a child remains the way into that child's Student Dashboard, and **no registration action goes inside it**. **(6) `GET /profile` and `PATCH /profile` join TD-3.1.** §5.2 asks for *"view/edit own basic contact info (sensitive profiling fields remain restricted)"* and no endpoint existed — `GET /me` answers *which account is this* and deliberately carries no personal detail (R63). `GET` returns `id`, `name_arabic`, `name_french`, `nickname`, `phone`, `email`, `sex`, `account_status`, `reference_code` and `version`; **`PATCH` accepts `phone` and `nickname` and nothing else**, and the exclusions are the specification rather than an oversight: **names are identity** and a rename is a staff act on the §14.2 screen where it is reviewable; **`sex`** feeds §4.4b's `gender_restriction`, so self-editing it would let a person move themselves past an admission rule; **`email`** is the Google identity the account is keyed to (§4.1b); **`account_status`** is an approver's decision (TD-1); and everything under §4.10/BR-16 is staff-only safeguarding data that never reaches this surface. **TD-15 optimistic locking applies** (`version` in, `409 VERSION_CONFLICT` otherwise) and the write reuses the **existing `user.update`** audit action — the same act by a different actor, and a second action name would split one question across two rows. **The endpoint takes no id**: the subject is the JWT `sub`, so there is nowhere for a caller to name someone else, exactly as R63 argued for `GET /students/me`. **(7) Account deletion is NOT built by this revision, and the reason is recorded.** §4.10's *"two-step account self-deletion"* is five words with no route in TD-3, no state in TD-1 and no screen in §14.1, and **`docs/SRS-PROPOSAL-R54.md` drafted exactly this and has never been approved** — it reverses Revision 52's prohibition and depends on work R52 deferred. The personal section therefore offers **no deletion control**: building an irreversible, unapproved capability because a page now exists to host it would be the worst possible reading of this revision. **R54 remains the Document Owner's decision**, and this revision settles only where its screen will live when it is taken.

**Revision 64 (Document Owner decision — the child-registration flow reconciled, 2026-08-11):** R62 built a second child-registration path without reconciling it against the first, and three defects followed from that one omission. **(1) `ChildApplication.requested_branch_id` is added** (nullable, `ON DELETE RESTRICT`) and **written by BOTH submission paths**. A parent adding a second child supplied strictly less than the same parent supplied for their first: `POST /registrations` records the family's branch on the applicant's `User` row and the child's Category on each application, while `POST /child-applications` had **no branch field anywhere** and a Category the interface never collected — so an approver received a request naming neither of the two things §4.1 step 1 and Revision 39 exist to give them. **Revision 39 is completed, not contradicted**: `User.intended_branch_id` remains *the applicant's own* request and is unchanged; this records *the child's*, which previously had nowhere to go once the parent already existed. Nullable because applications submitted before this revision have no answer, and *not stated* is a real state. Two consequences fall out and are intended: the §14.2 queue item reports `branch { id, name }` instead of the `null` it always sent and **the branch filter reaches it**, and §4.1 step 1's Level preselection works for these items because the request finally carries a Category. **It is a request, never a placement**, identically to Revision 39 — nothing reads it to choose, validate or refuse anything. **(2) `/dashboard/student/register-child` joins §14.1**, as a child of `/dashboard/student` beside the `/grades`, `/quran` and `/calendar` nodes already listed there, and the «＋ تسجيل طفل» action **leaves the role switcher**. A switcher lists the contexts a person may work in; **registering is a task, not a context** — the action sat inside the `ولي الأمر` group, so an account holding the role with no approved children opened a menu containing one item that was not a context at all, and the dialog it opened could only ever carry a subset of the fields the public form collects, which is how the divergence in (1) was introduced. The page is available to **any adult account**: a parent adding a second child and an adult student registering one are the same act, which is why `POST /child-applications` already accepts both. **(3) `ولي الأمر` is offered only once at least one child is approved.** R62.9 grants the role on the first approval, so the role-without-a-child state cannot normally occur — but every link may later be revoked, and the group would then be an empty menu. **An entry that opens onto nothing is the same defect as a button that renders a blank page** (§14.4). **(4) A child-registration request is NEVER decided as a bundle, and the server now says so by name.** R62.2 narrowed TD-4.2 to one child, so `POST /admin/approvals/{id}/approve` has no meaning for these items: their queue id is a `request_id`, naming no `User` and no `FamilyLink`, so both lookups missed and an administrator received **`404 NOT_FOUND` for an item the queue had just rendered**. That id is now refused with `VALIDATION_FAILED` and `reason: DECIDE_PER_CHILD`; each child is decided through `POST /admin/child-applications/{id}/decide`. **(5) Approving a child requires a placement.** §4.1 (R43) is unchanged by R62 — *“an approved account with no enrollment is a person the platform admitted and then lost”* — but R62's per-child path made `administrative_group_id` optional, so the family route obeyed §4.1 and the per-child route quietly did not: two implementations of one rule, which is the failure this document keeps paying for. `ENROLLMENT_REQUIRED` is answered on this path exactly as on the other. **Linking an EXISTING account stays exempt**, since that student is already placed and demanding a second enrolment would mean a Level per parent. **(6) نوع التسجيل and الفئة are both necessary and do not overlap; one was mislabelled.** نوع التسجيل is *who is registering whom* and selects the payload shape (§4.1b step 4c defines exactly two); الفئة is *which educational stage the STUDENT is admitted to*. The apparent overlap was a label — the first option read «تسجيل شخصي (الكبار)», naming a Category inside a field that is not about Categories, while الفئة then offered الطفل to that same self-registering adult. The parenthetical is removed and both hints state which question is being asked. **(7) RECORDED AS AN OPEN GAP, deliberately not closed here: nothing marks the adult Category.** §2.1 and §4.1b are explicit that adult students hold their own logins and minors do not, so a self-registering adult **is** in الكبار and a child application is **never** in الكبار — yet no form can enforce either, because Revision 27 made the three Categories generic renameable rows and **nothing distinguishes the adult one structurally**. Matching on the name would hardcode reference data this document expects to be renamed. The Category list is therefore left unfiltered and the gap is stated rather than papered over; closing it needs an Owner decision, and the recommendation on record is a structural marker on `Category` (for example `holds_own_login`) — one nullable boolean that makes an existing normative rule enforceable while keeping the names editable. **It is not an age gate**: it turns on who holds the login, which is §4.3's structural definition of a minor, and never on a birth date or a schooling stage.

**Revision 63 (Document Owner decision — the Student Dashboard's read endpoint, and a §14.1 correction, 2026-08-11):** R62.10 ships a minimal Student Dashboard and names its content — *the student identity block: name, reference code, Category, Level, branch* — but **no endpoint returns it**, so the clause was unbuildable. Everything else on that screen already exists: today's and upcoming sessions are `GET /calendar` (TD-3.4), and *basic student information* IS the identity block rather than a second surface. **(1) `GET /students/me` joins TD-3.3**, returning `id`, `name_arabic`, `reference_code` and `enrollments[{ category, level, branch }]` — **exactly R62.10's five fields and nothing else**. No sex, no schooling stage, no French name, no consent state, no account status, no dates: this is a screen a parent looks at, and every extra field is personal data published to one more surface for no stated purpose. **(2) It is placed in TD-3.3 because that clause already states the rule it obeys** — the acting student is resolved per §4.3, and this is the **first** route to mount that resolution, which existed as middleware with no caller. **(3) There is no `{id}` in the path, and that is the security property.** TD-12 forbids trusting a student identifier from the request for authorization; a path parameter would be a second place a caller could name a student and would oblige every future edit to prove the path and the header agree. The subject is resolved once, in middleware, from the header or the JWT `sub`, so it is never in the caller's hands. **(4) `GET /me` and `GET /students/me` answer different questions and are not to be harmonised**: `/me` answers *which account is this*, `/students/me` answers *which student am I acting for* — which, for a parent, is not their own account. This is the same distinction R60.9 drew between `/me` and authorization. **(5) `enrollments` is a LIST because the model permits several** — `Enrollment` is unique on `(student_id, level_id)`, so a student may hold one per Level. R62.10's singular wording reflects the common case; a singular field would have to *choose* which enrolment to show and silently discard the rest. Soft-deleted enrolments are excluded. `reference_code` is `null` for adult students and for accounts predating R62, which is a real answer rather than a missing one. **(6) No new error code, no new audit row, no schema change.** `404` for a header resolving to no approved `(parent, child)` link — with no distinction between pending, revoked, deleted, nonexistent or another parent's child (§4.3, §20 rule 17) — and `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for a Parent-only caller sending no header. **A staff caller receives that `400` too, and correctly so**: staff reach a student's record through §14.2 and the TD-2 matrix, a different authorization path, and one endpoint serving two audiences must get the difference right on every future change (the argument Revision 35 made for `GET /branches`). **Reads are deliberately NOT audited**, unlike `StudentSocialProfile` (§4.10, Revision 28): a student or their approved parent reading a name and a Level is ordinary use, not a security-sensitive act, and TD-8 gains no row. **(7) §14.1's `Dashboard` line is corrected.** It still listed `/dashboard/parent` while the `Family` line four rows below recorded that R62.9 removed it — two lines of one section disagreeing. Revision order already governs, so the implementation was never in doubt; the stale line is corrected rather than left to be rediscovered, because a sitemap is read as a list of what exists and a reader who finds the path there has no reason to look four rows down.

**Revision 62 (Document Owner decision — parent and child registration, 2026-08-11):** A parent registers **one or more children in one request**; an administrator decides **each child independently**;
